# Event Versioning And Upcasting

This is the Story 6.5 AD-13 normative artifact. It specifies a future Story 6.6 implementation; it changes no runtime behavior. **Story 6.6 is unauthorized until the content-bound human receipt in §12 validates.** The words MUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD and MAY are normative. There are no deferred implementation decisions.

## 1. Current path and authority inventory

| Boundary | Current source and observable behavior | Required 6.6 seam |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Contract identity | `Contracts/Events/IEventContract.cs`, `Client/Events/EventContractResolver.cs`, `Client/Conventions/NamingConventionEngine.cs`: static `EventType` and `Domain` validate kebab case; no payload version. | Keep that exact validation and register an explicit payload descriptor. |
| Writer and carrier | `Contracts/Results/DomainServiceWireResult.cs` serializes CLR type name to a whole byte array; its `DomainServiceWireEvent` has a three-argument positional constructor and no metadata version or canonical pair. `DomainService/DomainServiceRequestRouter.cs` calls it for normal and rejection results; `Server/DomainServices/DaprDomainServiceInvoker.cs` maps to `ISerializedEventPayload` wrappers, losing any future extra fields. | One bounded, capability-selected V1/V2 writer, additive nullable wire-event triplet and lossless intermediate mapping. |
| Persist and commit | `Server/Events/EventPersister.cs` serializes/protects events and stages Server `EventEnvelope` with metadata version 1; `Server/Actors/AggregateActor.cs` owns `SaveStateAsync`, ambiguous-save readback, outbox and later publication. | Validate and serialize before global ID reservation; preserve actor-owned save/readback. |
| Read and protection | `Server/Events/EventStreamReader.cs` loads typed actor envelopes and snapshots; `Contracts/Events/EventEnvelope.cs` normalizes null extensions to empty; `Contracts/Security/EventStorePayloadProtectionMetadataCarrier.cs` parses `eventstore.protection` and may derive ProviderOpaque. | Bounded raw read, raw presence/evidence before normalization, shared allow-listed evolution. |
| Dead-letter ingress and capture | `Operations/Capture/DeadLetterEnvelopeParser.cs` recognizes legacy structured JSON `data`; `Operations/Endpoints/DeadLetterOperationsEndpointExtensions.cs` currently defaults to a 1 MiB capture limit and can return HTTP 200 without retaining an oversized body. | Decode authenticated Binary and structured `data_base64` carriers, and acknowledge only after durable exact-byte capture/readback under the admitted 128 MiB raw-carrier ceiling. |
| Replay and command | `Client/Aggregates/{ApplyMethodResolver,AggregateReplayer,EventStoreProjection}.cs`, `Client/Handlers/DomainProcessorStateRehydrator.cs`, `DomainService/DomainServiceRequestRouter.cs`, `Server/DomainServices/DaprAggregateStateReconstructor.cs`, `Server/Actors/CoordinatedCommandActor.cs`. Synchronous replay and old full-array/target `GetEventsAsync(0)` paths exist. | Authenticated complete-prefix pages for every Apply, token-aware async route, bounded old adapter and target retry. |
| Projection and rebuild | `Server/Projections/{ProjectionEventWireBuilder,ProjectionStreamPageValidation,ProjectionDeliveryRetryWorker,EventStoreProjectionDeliveryHistoryReader,ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator,NamedProjectionDispatchCoordinator}.cs`, `DomainService/DomainSharedProjectionRebuildDispatcher.cs`. | One source validation route before fingerprints, dispatch, tail Apply or checkpoint. |
| Publish and subscribe | `Server/Events/EventPublisher.cs` unprotects then calls generic DAPR object publishing; `Client/Subscriptions/EventStoreDomainEventProcessor.cs`, `Client/Registration/EventStoreDomainEventsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`, `DomainService/EventStoreDomainEventsEndpointExtensions.cs` use separate CLR-name registry/DTO ingress and marker completion. | Pinned exact raw delivery bytes, verified transport context, global ID and effect-safe route markers. |
| Public inspection | `Controllers/{StreamsController,AdminStreamQueryController,AdminTraceQueryController,ReplayController}.cs`, `Admin.Abstractions/Models/TypeCatalog/EventTypeInfo.cs`; several Admin paths materialize `GetEventsAsync(0)`. `Testing/Builders/EventEnvelopeBuilder.cs` constructs legacy fixtures. | Same validated pages and additive stored/effective public evidence; retain legacy constructors/fields. |
| Authorized backup export | `Admin.Server/Services/DaprBackupCommandService.ExportStreamAsync` invokes the stream-read endpoint through `ReadStreamPageAsync` and collects `StreamReadEvent` pages, rejects protected/provider-opaque content, then emits JSON or CloudEvents. | Route each page through authenticated bounded source/identity validation before export; keep the authorized content-export policy and explicit stored provenance in the export, without treating an ordinary Admin/trace view as content authorization. |
| Read-model query visibility | `Client/Projections/DaprReadModelStore.GetAsync/GetManyAsync` accept only store name and key and resolve the existing coordinated-batch marker; neither method has a projection route or checkpoint row-set pointer. | Add a route-aware named-projection query seam that verifies the committed checkpoint pointer and complete immutable row set under the shared fence before selecting values; preserve existing non-versioned reads and batch-marker semantics. |
| Handler context | `Client/Subscriptions/EventStoreDomainEventContext.cs` is a public unsealed positional record with six arguments, generated deconstruction, nullable `long? GlobalPosition`, and optional init members `Domain`, `CausationId`, `UserId`. | Keep that source/binary shape; verified-only fields belong to an additive wrapper and never narrow an existing member. |
| Cancellation | Query, async projection and many server methods take tokens; `IDomainProcessor.ProcessAsync` and `IAggregateReplay.Replay` do not, and legacy projection is synchronous. | Exact token-aware additions and commit-phase outcome rules in §9. |

The actor stream, not an adapted DTO, remains replay authority. Ordinary reads never mutate event keys, stored payload, `MessageId`, sequence, original `DateTimeOffset` offset, correlation/causation, protection metadata or outbox pin. Domain code returns a result; only the actor stages/commits state. Reflection remains load-bearing; this contract claims no AOT/trimming support. Epic 8 protection-engine work is not a dependency.

## 2. Identity, metadata and compatibility

`EventContractType` is an ASCII string of 1–64 characters matching the existing `NamingConventionEngine.ValidateKebabCase` regex **`^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$`**. Repeated interior hyphens are allowed; no additional aggregate-type grammar is introduced. The unique canonical event key is `(Domain, EventContractType)` under ordinal comparison. `PayloadVersion` is an `int` from **1 through 1024**, positive and monotonically advanced for a canonical key. Metadata-envelope `MetadataVersion` is independent: persisted V1 is 1, V2 is 2. `DomainServiceVersion` names producer software and is never interpreted as payload version. The existing `EventTypeName` remains the stored discriminator: exact registered CLR alias for V1; for V2 it MUST equal `EventContractType` byte for byte. A registered V1 alias declares its actual source payload version, which may be 2 or greater even when MetadataVersion is 1; no generic version-1 default overrides it.

Add optional `string? EventContractType` (`eventContractType`) and `int? PayloadVersion` (`payloadVersion`), null by default, to **both** `Contracts.Events.EventMetadata` and persisted `Server.Events.EventEnvelope` without breaking existing constructors/deconstruction. Add `int? MetadataVersion => null`, `string? EventContractType => null` and `int? PayloadVersion => null` to `ISerializedEventPayload` as default interface implementations, preserving source and binary compatibility for third-party implementers; V2 concrete serialized/rejection carriers explicitly supply metadata version 2 and the non-null pair. Thread both through serialized and rejection wrappers, `DomainServiceWireEvent`, `DaprDomainServiceInvoker.ToDomainResult`, persister, actor state serializer/readback, stream reader, publisher and all public mappings. Add optional `string? WriterMode` (`writerMode`, exact `V1` or `V2`) and `string? RegistryFingerprint` (`registryFingerprint`, 64 lowercase hex SHA-256) to `DomainServiceRequest` and `DomainServiceWireResult`. Add nullable `byte[]? CommandStateProof` (`commandStateProof`, canonical padded RFC 4648 Base64 in JSON, at most 2 MiB for the entire encoded JSON property value including Base64; decoded bytes necessarily fit) and `IReadOnlyList<VerifiedEffectiveCommandEvent>? VerifiedEffectiveEvents` (`verifiedEffectiveEvents`) to `DomainServiceRequest` without changing its positional constructor. Each `VerifiedEffectiveCommandEvent` has exact JSON fields `sequence:long`, `storedDigest:byte[32]`, `eventContractType:string`, `payloadVersion:int`, `serializationFormat:string`, `effectivePayload:byte[]`, `routeClaim:byte[]`, `routeKeyId:string`, `routeSignature:byte[64]`; all byte arrays use canonical padded RFC 4648 Base64. It is an ordered, bounded internal carrier, charged with its effective payload and proof bytes to the §8 complete-array budget. `DomainServiceCurrentState.Events` remains the original stored `EventEnvelope` list for compatibility and never asserts effective bytes. Retain old constructors and the static `FromDomainResult` as an explicit V1 helper; production invokes one bounded writer for both success and admission rejection. An authenticated old request/response with both mode and fingerprint absent is V1 and has no echo. A one-sided pair, wrong value or stale explicit V1/V2 fingerprint fails `CapabilityMismatch` before producing or append. New mode-bearing endpoints echo both exact values; the gateway compares them and each event triplet. It never sends mode-bearing work to an old endpoint.

`DomainServiceWireEvent` retains exactly its existing positional `(string EventTypeName, byte[] Payload, string SerializationFormat = "json")` constructor, positional members, defaults and deconstruction. Add only nullable init-only `int? MetadataVersion`/`metadataVersion`, `string? EventContractType`/`eventContractType` and `int? PayloadVersion`/`payloadVersion`; under `JsonSerializerDefaults.Web`, these names are exactly lower camel case. Old construction, JSON lacking all three and the static V1 helper remain valid. The selected V2 writer sets `MetadataVersion = 2` and both non-null canonical fields on every normal or rejection event. Gateway admission checks each raw wire event's three-member presence and values before `DaprDomainServiceInvoker.ToDomainResult` or `EventPersister` can lose them: mode V2 rejects absent/null/partial triplets, wrong casing, `EventTypeName` disagreement, invalid type/version or a triplet inconsistent with the echoed mode/fingerprint. Mode V1 accepts the historical all-absent or all-null pair with metadata version absent/null/1 under the rule below, and rejects a mixed triplet. The invoker maps all admitted members to additive serialized-payload carrier members and the persisted Server envelope; no conversion drops a V2 version.

Wire V1 admits only exact domain-scoped registered aliases when the new pair is both absent or both explicitly JSON null and metadata version is absent/null/1. Mixed or partially non-null pair fails `MalformedMetadata`; raw JSON presence is kept until that decision. Stored V1 has normalized required MetadataVersion 1 but retains raw pair/extension presence as evidence. V2 requires metadata version 2, both non-null canonical pair members, registered current write descriptor, and `EventTypeName == EventContractType`. Before case-insensitive typed binding, every known V2 raw property name, including nested registered metadata properties, MUST have its exact declared lower-camel spelling; even one wrong-case spelling is `MalformedMetadata`. V1 retains its historical case-insensitive known-property mapping. Reject duplicate JSON names ordinally and with **.NET `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase`** recursively before binding with the pinned `JsonSerializerDefaults.Web` options. Invalid Unicode, non-object JSON or conflicting names fail before identity extraction. Exact case-sensitive aliases are registered; unknown or reused aliases with indistinguishable schemas block readiness and need separately approved offline evidence migration, never guessing.

Preappend and predispatch validate addressed tenant, domain, aggregate ID and registered aggregate type against envelope/key/route, positive contiguous sequence, global position bound, `MessageId`, and stored discriminator/version. For V2 current CLR payloads check `IEventContract` domain/type; for V1 apply the alias's explicit schema-specific aggregate-ID extractor or `NoPayloadIdentity`. The envelope/key identity is mandatory in either case. Check version-specific identity and schema after **every** upcast hop, before the next. `AggregateTerminated` is an explicit per-domain framework rejection descriptor/adapter even though it is `IRejectionEvent` without `IEventContract`; any other non-contract rejection needs its own registered adapter. No arbitrary CLR type loading or silent skip.

Replay and projection DTOs retain old `EventTypeName`, `Payload` and `SerializationFormat` as **stored** evidence. Add nullable `StoredEventContractType`/`storedEventContractType`, `StoredPayloadVersion`/`storedPayloadVersion`, `StoredSerializationFormat`/`storedSerializationFormat`, `EffectiveEventContractType`/`effectiveEventContractType`, `EffectivePayloadVersion`/`effectivePayloadVersion`, `EffectiveSerializationFormat`/`effectiveSerializationFormat`, `EffectivePayload: byte[]?`/`effectivePayload`, `StoredEventTypeName`/`storedEventTypeName`, `StoredDigest`/`storedDigest`, `RegistryFingerprint`/`registryFingerprint` and `IsAdapted`/`isAdapted` only where absent; retain `ReplayEventEnvelope.MetadataVersion` as its existing required `int`, and add nullable metadata version only to DTOs that lack it. The gateway alone sets effective fields from verified source; receiver verifies signed route/source hash before Apply, otherwise retrieves original evidence. Existing narrow DTOs need not expose tenant or raw protection fields. Subscription DTO adds missing aggregate type, domain service version, metadata version, stored/readable format, stored/effective provenance and nullable `ReadableExtensions`/`readableExtensions`; reuse existing `GlobalPosition`, `CausationId`, `UserId`. Old positional constructors remain callable; old DTOs trigger authenticated source lookup and never assert adaptation.

The additive `Contracts.Events.VerifiedEffectiveEventView` is the **handler-facing** current-payload carrier for projections and subscriptions. Its required properties and exact Web JSON names are `SequenceNumber:long`/`sequenceNumber`, `StoredDigest:byte[32]`/`storedDigest`, `EventContractType:string`/`eventContractType`, `PayloadVersion:int`/`payloadVersion`, `SerializationFormat:string`/`serializationFormat`, `EffectivePayload:byte[]`/`effectivePayload`, `RouteClaim:byte[]`/`routeClaim`, `RouteKeyId:string`/`routeKeyId`, and `RouteSignature:byte[64]`/`routeSignature`. Byte arrays use canonical padded RFC 4648 Base64; the payload is charged to the §8 page/array and 128 MiB scratch budgets, and its final hop is at most 1 MiB (a verified zero-hop current V1 source may retain its measured legacy ceiling). Neither a caller-created instance nor JSON binding makes this view verified. The gateway builds it from `IEventEvolutionReader` output; the receiver verifies its purpose-01 route claim under the current key/fingerprint, recomputes the effective-payload SHA-256, compares exact type/version/format, sequence, StoredDigest, consumed-metadata hash and addressed source, and matches the claim to the corresponding signed complete-prefix entry before passing it to a handler. A missing/stale/mismatched view triggers authenticated source lookup and fresh proof; if unavailable, return `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` without handler call or checkpoint.

Every public `byte[]` in `VerifiedEffectiveEventView`, `VerifiedEffectiveCommandEvent`, replay/projection/subscription DTOs, signed proof carriers and `PagedContext` is a transport copy, never a trusted live buffer. After source/signature/hash admission, the receiver copies the exact claim, signature, effective payload and consumed metadata into private, exclusively owned, charged buffers and verifies their hashes and scope again immediately before deserialization, Apply or handler dispatch. It never lends those buffers or a mutable alias to caller code, an async continuation, a pool or a handler; a handler that requires bytes receives a separate bounded copy or an immutable value detached from the authoritative buffer. No unverified public array can mutate the bytes used for state, receipt, history fingerprint or checkpoint after admission. The private copy remains owned through the operation fence, is charged to the same §8 live-scratch budget, and is cleared over its full capacity on release, cancellation and failure. A changed public array after admission cannot change the admitted event or cause a second Apply; if a fresh boundary comparison fails, return `HandlerCapabilityMismatch`/`CommandStateUnproven` before mutation.

Keep `ProjectionRequest` and its positional constructor/`Events` unchanged for authenticated zero-hop legacy adapters. Its optional init-only `VerifiedEffectiveEvents: VerifiedEffectiveEventView[]?`/`verifiedEffectiveEvents` and `EventEvolutionProof: byte[]?`/`eventEvolutionProof` are transport hints for the gateway, not a production verified-handler argument. Add a separate public immutable `VerifiedProjectionRequest` with addressed tenant, domain, aggregate type/ID, projection type and HandlerRouteId, a defensively copied ordered read-only current-payload view collection, exact copied signed page/prefix/optional checkpoint proof, and safe stored provenance limited to sequence, MessageId, StoredDigest, stored type/version/format and consumed-metadata hash. It has **no** `Events`, stored `Payload` or other stored-payload byte array; its constructor rejects any source without authenticated scope, complete proof and one verified same-sequence view per page event. The gateway constructs it from private verified copies after source validation and §6 page reduction/re-signing, then the router rechecks its proof and views before dispatch. A page that cannot fit is reduced before dispatch, never repeatedly retried unchanged. Legacy handlers receive `ProjectionRequest` only when every event is verified zero-hop with identical stored/effective bytes and descriptors. Add `EventStoreDomainEventEnvelope.VerifiedEffectiveEvent: VerifiedEffectiveEventView?`/`verifiedEffectiveEvent` without changing its positional constructor, original `Payload`, `EventTypeName` or `SerializationFormat`; after signed binary delivery admission, the subscription processor obtains this view through the same reader or authenticated source lookup. The delivery claim/source digest and the view's route claim must name the same event and active domain fingerprint. The ingress adapter ignores any caller-supplied `verifiedEffectiveEvent` member in a legacy DTO and overwrites that property only from authenticated reader output. No untrusted publication body may supply an authoritative effective view.

Authorized backup export is also a shared-read consumer. `DaprBackupCommandService.ExportStreamAsync` and its `ReadStreamPageAsync` invocation pin a fixed addressed head/target and walk bounded authenticated pages through `IEventEvolutionReader`, verifying every stored identity, sequence, digest, alias/version and protection state before any content is added to the export. Page gaps, head drift, unknown aliases, malformed metadata or failed source proof stop the export with a typed reason and no partial downloadable artifact. The existing authorization/audit policy still governs content access; protected and ProviderOpaque payloads remain unexportable unless an independently approved protection policy exists. For V1 authorized JSON/CloudEvents content export, retain the exact stored alias and original readable payload with explicit `storedEventTypeName`, source `payloadVersion` and `metadataVersion` provenance; do not relabel the payload as current without producing a separately labeled verified effective representation. V2 exported public identity is canonical `eventContractType` plus `payloadVersion`, with `storedEventTypeName` as separately labeled audit provenance. The stream reader returns only bounded validated pages to the exporter. The complete encoded downloadable JSON or CloudEvents content, including escaping, metadata, separators and framing, has a hard 64 MiB cap in addition to the configured event-count ceiling. A checked byte counter charges each encoded chunk before allocation; use bounded streaming or encrypted bounded spooling, never a full event list or content string before the cap is proved. The configured count is fenced to the validated page and legacy-array budgets before serving. Crossing either bound returns typed `ExportLimit`, discards the private partial output and exposes no downloadable artifact; cancellation does the same. An exporter/provider without a preallocation or bounded streaming guarantee fails readiness. Ordinary Admin/trace responses remain support-safe and never acquire raw content merely because this export route exists.

For V2 public replay `FailedEventType` and timeline `EventTypeName` are canonical values, with separate stored alias; V1 old fields retain legacy values only with explicit `StoredEventTypeName` provenance and authorized canonical companion. Type Catalog keeps `EventTypeInfo.TypeName` as a legacy CLR alias view and `SchemaVersion` in its current catalog/schema meaning; add `EventContractType`, `PayloadVersion`, `MetadataVersion` with explicit descriptor/source mapping. Never silently reinterpret old fields. Admin/trace expose only safe identity, version, hop count, reason and sequence, never CLR assembly names as public identity, raw/protected payload, key alias or stack traces.

The V2 current-descriptor requirement is **write admission only**. A stored V2 event at any retained registered older version remains valid read input and follows its unique chain to the current descriptor within 16 hops. Source version, metadata-envelope version and target version are never substituted for one another.

Backup export stages every validated page into a tenant-scoped authenticated encrypted blob under the fixed addressed head and a checked 64 MiB **encoded** output limit. The exporter writes bounded chunks and exact content hash without releasing HTTP response bytes or a downloadable URL while the export is incomplete. After last-page validation it reads back every staged chunk, total length and hash, then atomically publishes one immutable completion pointer under the authorized export operation ID. Only that committed artifact may be downloaded or streamed; a lost pointer acknowledgement is reconciled by pointer/hash readback, never by exporting a possibly changed head again. A later-page error, cancellation, invalid protection state or limit discards or quarantines private staging and returns a typed failure with no partial artifact. Staging access, retention and cleanup remain tenant-scoped and auditable; an authorized caller cannot use ordinary Admin/trace access to obtain export bytes.

## 3. Registry, upcasting and shared read route

The immutable per-domain registry contains D current descriptors (canonical key, current CLR type, aggregate route and Apply/state schema), V records for **every reachable version** (schema validator, serializer, options, format and identity policy), A exact V1 aliases (source version/format/serializer, alias validator and identity extractor or no-identity), E one-step edges `n -> n+1`, and F optional V1 downserializers. Duplicate keys, ambiguous aliases, missing validators, gaps on an admitted source path, cycles, downgrade edges, unsupported format, non-deterministic discovery order or more than **16 hops** fail startup/readiness. A version 1 source cannot be advanced to current 18 under that bound without separately approved offline migration/retention decision; the 1..1024 field range is not a chain promise. Corpus readiness proves every retained source reaches current in at most 16 hops and that every output fits its limit.

The exact additive public contract is `IEventUpcaster.UpcastAsync(IReadOnlyPayload input, IBoundedPayloadWriter output, IBoundedScratchAllocator scratch, CancellationToken cancellationToken): ValueTask<EventUpcastResult>`. Its immutable descriptor declares source/target canonical key and consecutive versions, input/output format and serializer, edge identity policy, implementation/options hashes. `EventUpcastResult` declares domain, type, version and format; each is checked against E before further processing. The upcaster uses only the supplied writer for output and supplied allocator for large scratch, is pure/deterministic for fixed input/configuration, and has no clock, randomness, network, durable writes or effects. `IBoundedPayloadWriter.Write(ReadOnlySpan<byte>)` and `Complete(): void` charge checked capacity **before** allocation, allow no writes after Complete, enforce 1 MiB output and token checks between writes. `Complete()` is one-shot and returns no backing array, span or `ReadOnlyMemory` to the upcaster. After `UpcastAsync` returns successfully, the pipeline privately seals and takes the charged output owner, validates schema, identity and hash from its exclusive bytes, and passes a pipeline-owned immutable view to the next hop. The public `IReadOnlyPayload` exposes only `int Length { get; }` and `void CopyTo(int sourceOffset, Span<byte> destination)`; the latter checks nonnegative offset, checked range and cancellation before copying exact bytes into caller-owned storage. The pipeline supplies a private sealed facade over its exclusively owned immutable source, with no `Memory`, `MemoryManager`, array, segment, pin, pointer, stream or backing-owner return, and no public downcast to its owner. An upcaster can mutate only its own copy; a large destination must come from the charged scratch allocator. The facade captures the invocation cancellation token, invalidates its read lease when that invocation ends and rejects every later copy through a retained reference. `MemoryMarshal.TryGetMemoryManager` and `TryGetArray` therefore receive no `ReadOnlyMemory` pointing at the source. The source owner remains private across the call; inability to enforce this facade blocks readiness before upcaster invocation. Immediately before each allow-listed upcaster call, the pipeline hashes the exact input bytes; on successful return, cancellation or failure it hashes those same bytes again before accepting output or retrying. A changed input is `UpcasterContractViolation`: discard output, release and clear all owners, and perform no next hop, Apply or effect. The pipeline also rechecks the sealed output digest immediately before the following hop or Apply. Code and transitive native dependency audit remains mandatory; the post-call comparison is defense in depth, never permission to lend a writable alias. The upcaster cannot obtain, retain or mutate an alias to that owner; a retained writer reference cannot read bytes and rejects every post-completion write or Complete. No pool return/reuse occurs until the pipeline releases and zeroes the full owner after all checks and next-hop reads. A missing Complete, second Complete, attempted write after Complete or untrusted owner transfer is a typed upcaster contract failure before Apply. The additive public scratch API is `IBoundedScratchAllocator.WithScratch(int requestedCapacity, ScratchSpanAction action, CancellationToken cancellationToken): void`, with a separately declared `delegate void ScratchSpanAction(Span<byte> span)`. It reserves the exact requested capacity or the documented deterministic pool bucket's maximum before allocation, checks the actual private owner capacity against that reservation, and invokes `action` synchronously once under the captured token. Safe code receives only a writable `Span<byte>` during that callback. The API returns no span, `Memory`, array, owner, memory manager, pin, pointer, stream or return-control object; no public `Rent` or `IMemoryOwner<byte>` escape exists. The lease is invalid at callback return, exception and cancellation, and any retained-lease or second-callback attempt is `UpcasterContractViolation` before the next hop. The allocator clears the **entire actual privately owned capacity** before pool return on every path and keeps its full 128 MiB scratch charge until clearing and return finish. Unknown pool rounding, unenforceable callback isolation or unsafe/native code able to retain an alias holds upcaster activation after dependency audit; a failed allocation clears any obtained capacity before release. Source, validation and hop buffers count; implementation-local allocation cannot be guaranteed by these interfaces, so registration requires repeated-byte tests, review/measurement and process memory budget. `PayloadLimit`, `ScratchLimit` and cancellation are distinct. A V1 downserializer F is separately registered and fingerprinted with source/current schema, code/options and independent identity/semantic-preservation validators; round-trip corpus proof is required before V1 write. `PrimaryWriteAlias` is legal only when its source schema exactly matches output bytes.

One gateway-owned `IEventEvolutionReader` performs bounded raw actor/source read and source authentication; raw presence/JSON checks; scope/sequence/head validation; protection parsing and bounded unprotect; alias/version resolution; schema/identity checks; contiguous upcast with per-hop bounded output; allow-listed current deserialization; signed route and complete-prefix evidence. A V1 unsigned event is authenticated by addressed actor key, trusted provider readback, immutable head/sequence and recomputed StoredDigest, then receives a **new** signed route/prefix proof. No historic signature is invented. Every command rehydration, replay, projection live/retry/history/rebuild, subscription, publication, effect-retention authorization, coordinated-command source/target read, stream, Admin and trace path passes this route before Apply, handler, fingerprint or response. A route receiving an old or stale DTO does source lookup. No path relies on a DTO's `isAdapted` claim alone. The reader never upcasts twice in one read: stored evidence stays separate; signed effective bytes are verified and used once. For commands, the router verifies the §6 `commandStateProof` before admission stages or either old/new processor dispatch, compares every effective carrier event with its signed route and stored `CurrentState.Events` evidence, and builds private state only from verified effective bytes. A nonzero-hop event never enters `DomainProcessorStateRehydrator`'s stored-envelope Apply method. The old rehydrator may run only when every event is proven zero-hop, its stored alias resolves to the exact current Apply type and payload bytes are identical. Free typed/JSON `CurrentState`, a missing/mismatched proof or a partial page fails `CommandStateUnproven`; authenticated source lookup under §6 may supply fresh proof and carrier before dispatch. Every F downserializer output is checked on **that output** against the registered V1 alias schema, format, identity extractor or `NoPayloadIdentity` policy, and semantic round-trip validator before MessageId reservation or actor staging. A data-dependent mismatch returns `DownserializeRejected` with no append even if corpus preflight passed.

`IAsyncAggregateReplay` itself exposes `bool CanReplayAggregateType(string aggregateType)` and `ReplayAsync(AggregateReconstructionRequest request, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<AggregateReconstructionResult>` as the exact new replay seam. The domain router selects it by explicit aggregate route before legacy `IAggregateReplay.Replay`, independently of keyed `IDomainProcessor`; domain scope comes from the registered route/request, not an extra interface parameter. The old synchronous replay adapter checks token before/after its bounded call and cannot claim mid-call cancellation. `IAsyncDomainProcessor.ProcessAsync(CommandEnvelope command, object? currentState, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<DomainResult>` wins over old `IDomainProcessor` when both are registered; old processor adapter checks cancellation immediately before and after its tokenless `ProcessAsync` call, so it cannot promise mid-call cancellation; its separate bounded rehydration loop checks between events. `IAsyncDomainProjectionHandler` remains preferred over its legacy projection adapter. Every route forwards the originating token into pages, deserialization and Apply loops; cancelled scratch is discarded.

The existing `AggregateReplayer` resolves the stored `ReplayEventEnvelope.EventTypeName` and deserializes stored `Payload`. Its legacy adapter is therefore permitted only for a verified **zero-hop** event whose exact stored CLR alias and payload schema match current Apply. It cannot be handed an evolved DTO and expected to read `EffectivePayload`. Any aggregate route with a reachable retained source requiring one or more hops MUST use `IAsyncAggregateReplay` with verified effective payload and current descriptor. A serving endpoint lacking that capability blocks readiness; it never skips, mislabels or Applies older bytes as current.

`DomainProjectionDispatcher.Project`, `DispatchAsync`, `ReconcileAsync` and every live, retry, history and `DomainSharedProjectionRebuildDispatcher` entry validate the complete page/prefix and materialize `VerifiedEffectiveEventView` before history fingerprinting or handler selection. The existing synchronous `Project` helper is restricted to authenticated zero-hop identical history; the `/project` endpoint adds an asynchronous `DomainProjectionDispatcher.ProjectVerifiedAsync(IServiceProvider serviceProvider, VerifiedProjectionRequest request, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<ProjectionResponse?>` route for hopped full replay and selects it from verified source capability before legacy invocation. The gateway alone converts its incoming legacy transport request into `VerifiedProjectionRequest`; this helper never blocks on an asynchronous handler. The additive `IVerifiedDomainProjectionHandler` exposes `Domain:string`, `ProjectionType:string`, and `ProjectVerifiedAsync(VerifiedProjectionRequest request, string dispatchId, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<DomainProjectionHandlerResult>`. `AddVerifiedDomainProjectionHandler<THandler>(IServiceCollection services, string handlerRouteId, string canonicalEventType, int currentPayloadVersion, string effectMode, string receiptProviderId)` registers THandler with scoped lifetime once in the `IEnumerable<IVerifiedDomainProjectionHandler>` DI collection and a singleton immutable §5 capability descriptor for its primary type; `THandler : class, IVerifiedDomainProjectionHandler`, and its declared `Domain`/`ProjectionType` must equal the catalog route. The startup catalog builder enumerates verified, `IAsyncDomainProjectionHandler` and `IDomainProjectionHandler` collections, rejects duplicate `(domain, HandlerRouteId)` or two verified registrations for one `(domain, projectionType, canonical type, current version)` route, and selects the verified registration when it overlaps an old registration for the same route; registration order never selects a handler. A verified-only route is discoverable and its exact capability row is attested before serving. Every live, retry, history, reconciliation and shared-rebuild dispatcher uses this same selection and verifies the `VerifiedProjectionRequest` proof and current-payload views before invoking the selected handler. Its Apply/deserialization reads only each view's current type/version/format/payload; only safe stored provenance is exposed and hopped stored payload bytes are unreachable from the handler argument. The legacy `IDomainProjectionHandler` and `IAsyncDomainProjectionHandler`/legacy adapter may receive their existing `ProjectionRequest.Events` only when **every** event is verified zero-hop with byte-identical payload, identical current descriptor and supported stored format. A reachable hopped source and no verified-capable route returns `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` before invocation, effect or checkpoint; a page is never promoted to a complete live model. Shared rebuild history/inventory fingerprints use authenticated source and effective evidence, not free DTO bytes.

For the full-replay helper, `DomainProjectionHandlerResult.Completed` maps to a `ProjectionResponse` only when `State` is present, valid under the registered projection-state schema and bound to the verified source proof. `AlreadyCompleted` retrieves the durable completed state/receipt under the same route, dispatch ID and source proof and returns that state only after readback; an absent or changed receipt is `ProjectionStateUnproven`. `Retryable`, `Indeterminate`, `Failed` and a `Completed` result with absent/invalid state throw a typed `VerifiedProjectionDispatchException` carrying respectively `Retryable`, `Indeterminate`, `Failed` or `ProjectionStateUnproven`, with bounded support-safe reason and no success body. The `/project` endpoint maps these to HTTP 503, 409, 500 and 503 respectively; cancellation remains a cancelled request with no synthetic result. `null` and HTTP 404 are reserved solely for a genuinely absent registered route after authenticated route lookup. Existing `DomainProjectionHandlerResult` factories and legacy `ProjectionResponse` constructors remain unchanged. A retryable or invalid handler result never advances a checkpoint or returns a successful projection body.

`EventStoreDomainEventProcessor.ProcessAsync` first authenticates binary transport, delivery claim, committed global batch root and the matching purpose-01 route/view, then selects an explicit subscription registration by `(domain, VerifiedEffectiveEventView.EventContractType, PayloadVersion, stable HandlerRouteId)` from the allow-listed current-type catalog. `AddVerifiedDomainEventHandler<TEvent,THandler>(IServiceCollection services, string domain, string canonicalEventType, int currentPayloadVersion, string handlerRouteId, string effectMode, string receiptProviderId)` registers the exact scoped callable `ITransactionalEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>` for `AtomicStore` or `IProviderIdempotentEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>` for `ProviderIdempotency`, plus one singleton immutable §5 capability descriptor. `TEvent : IEventPayload`; `THandler : class` must implement the selected callable interface or registration fails before activation. The unchanged `IEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>` is registered only as a source-compatible facade when an audited adapter implements that selected contract; its old `Task` method is never the production effect path. `TEvent` must equal the D current CLR type, and the receipt provider must pass §7's durable probe before production activation. The registrar rejects duplicate keys, two handlers for one keyed route, ambiguous overlapping predicates and any route ID reused in a domain. Once the current view is verified, this registration takes precedence over a legacy CLR-name handler for the same route; neither old registration order nor stored `EventTypeName` chooses it. `AddTransactionalDomainEventHandler<TEvent,THandler>(IServiceCollection services, string domain, string canonicalEventType, int currentPayloadVersion, string handlerRouteId, string receiptProviderId)` registers one scoped `ITransactionalEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>` plus the same singleton route descriptor; `THandler : class, ITransactionalEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>`. `AddProviderIdempotentDomainEventHandler<TEvent,THandler>(IServiceCollection services, string domain, string canonicalEventType, int currentPayloadVersion, string handlerRouteId, string receiptProviderId)` registers a scoped `IProviderIdempotentEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>` with its external provider descriptor; `THandler : class, IProviderIdempotentEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>`. Both additions are separate from the old `Task` registration, preserve its source/binary API, require the same current CLR `TEvent` and catalog key, and reject a second implementation for that key. The router chooses the exact cataloged transactional or provider-idempotent interface before any old handler adapter; it never calls an old `HandleAsync` side effect and then writes a receipt. It deserializes **only** `VerifiedEffectiveEventView.EffectivePayload` using its verified `SerializationFormat`, validates the current schema/identity and invokes that current typed handler under the §7 effect receipt. It never resolves `envelope.EventTypeName` or deserializes `envelope.Payload` for a hopped event, and it does not mark a missing current handler, mismatched view or old-only handler complete. A verified zero-hop identical source may use the old members through its existing adapter only after the production effect-receipt gate; any other old handler route is `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` and remains unacknowledged. Routing filters evaluate signed scope and canonical current type, never an unverified stored alias.

The provider-facing additive `IAuthenticatedRawEventSource.ReadRawPageAsync(string tenantId, string domain, string aggregateType, string aggregateId, long startSequence, int maxCount, long maxRawBytes, long maxReadableBytes, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<AuthenticatedRawEventPage>` is the **only** authoritative storage entry to `IEventEvolutionReader`. Its caller supplies an addressed scope and checked start/count/byte limit, never a proof or owner. The trusted provider adapter returns exact immutable raw event bytes, durable event storage key and sequence for each item, original provider namespace/encoding sidecar bytes, actor head and ETag, and an authenticated bounded readback proof binding the addressed key range, head and exact raw-byte hashes. The returned page carries no caller-created authoritative proof; its byte owners are private, detached from typed `EventEnvelope`, and remain immutable until validation ends. `maxCount` is 1..256, `maxRawBytes` is checked and at most 128 MiB for the whole raw page, `maxReadableBytes` is checked and at most 64 MiB for the resulting readable page, each raw envelope is at most 128 MiB, and the existing 128 MiB pipeline scratch charge is separate; checked transport/stream limits enforce these ceilings **before** typed or whole-page materialization. A provider with only an unbounded typed `GetStateAsync<EventEnvelope>` path cannot advertise the capability. A production provider probe stores, reads, tampers and races exact raw bytes/sidecar/head/ETag and verifies early size rejection and proof failure; inability to prove the API holds reader readiness with `RawSourceUnavailable`. The existing `EventStreamReader` becomes an adapter after this authenticated raw source and shared validation. Every command, projection, subscription, publication, stream, Admin, trace and export read, including retained V1 migration, enters through that source rather than reinterpreting typed normalized bytes as original evidence.

`AuthenticatedRawEventPage` is an additive immutable result with `TenantId:string`, `Domain:string`, `AggregateType:string`, `AggregateId:string`, `StartSequence:long`, `ActorHead:long`, `ActorETag:string`, `ProviderNamespace:string`, `Events:IReadOnlyList<AuthenticatedRawEvent>` and `ReadbackProof:byte[]`. Each `AuthenticatedRawEvent` has `StorageKey:string`, `SequenceNumber:long`, `RawEnvelope:IReadOnlyPayload`, `EncodingEvidence:IReadOnlyPayload?`, `StoredDigestEvidence:IReadOnlyPayload?`, `V1OriginEvidence:IReadOnlyPayload?`, `ActorIntentCertificate:IReadOnlyPayload?` and `ActorCommitReceipt:IReadOnlyPayload?`. The last two are mandatory together for branch-01 new-writer V1 and absent together for branch-02 retained V1 or V2. All six byte records are immutable source output under one authenticated actor head, ETag, backend and committed provider generation; none is supplied by caller JSON. The pipeline independently verifies the proof, record presence, scope, event key, sequence and every cross-hash before typed normalization. A changed head, ETag, key, sequence or record invalidates the entire page before adaptation or Apply.

The trusted adapter's `ReadbackProof` claim is `HX-EV-RAW-READBACK-1\0 || 01 || 000e` followed by ordered tags `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U aggregate type, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` N requested start sequence, `06` I event count, `07` N actor head, `08` U actor ETag, `09` U provider namespace, `0a` U canonical backend identity, `0b` N committed provider generation, `0c` Q issuance UTC, `0d` B exact authenticated provider-fence evidence and `0e` B ordered event entries. That B contains u32 count matching tag `06`; each entry is U storage key, N sequence, U provider encoding ID, B32 raw SHA-256, then one presence byte (`00` absent, `01` present) and B32 SHA-256 when present for **each** of encoding, stored-digest, origin, actor-intent and provider-commit-receipt records in that fixed order. Counts/lengths, strict sequence, 1 MiB complete proof cap, 256-entry maximum and all six record hashes are checked before nested allocation. The claim is signed under distinct purpose `10` with the §6 P-256/P1363 carrier by the trusted provider adapter's backend-bound key; the verifier obtains that key and backend identity from a configured per-provider trust anchor, never from caller JSON. It verifies signature, purpose, fence evidence, commit generation, addressed storage keys/head/ETag and exact hashes against one authenticated provider readback. Branch-01 admission additionally verifies the purpose-11 receipt, exact pre-save intent certificate, origin branch reference, backend and every same-save staged-record hash; the post-save receipt is obtained from the provider's committed-generation lookup, never from the staged-record list. Branch-02 admission obtains its signed offline manifest, reviewer docket, original source evidence and version-2 digest sidecar through the authenticated event-key source lookup, comparing all hashes to the origin branch and raw bytes. Stale head, changed ETag, missing required record, duplicate key, caller-constructed or malformed proof returns `RawSourceUnavailable`/`LegacyEvidenceConflict` before typed normalization. For V2, origin and branch-01 records are absent; raw, encoding and StoredDigest evidence remain mandatory. For a **valid** V1 event, encoding, StoredDigest and exactly one verified origin branch are mandatory. Signed absence of encoding/digest/origin on a retained raw-corrupt item is admissible only with a matching immutable `RawCorruptDisposition`, exact raw-byte hash, actor/provider proof, durable AD-31 evidence and operator decision; the item is returned solely as a typed corruption stop, never materialized, Apply-ed, routed or checkpointed. Other signed absence is admissible only for a documented pre-migration state not serving evolved readers; it never waives active valid-V1 requirements. Tampered sidecar, swapped certificate/receipt, concurrent head advance and provider-fence failure are mandatory probes.

The optional F record has an exact additive callable contract: `IV1Downserializer.DownserializeAsync(IReadOnlyPayload input, IBoundedPayloadWriter output, IBoundedScratchAllocator scratch, CancellationToken cancellationToken): ValueTask<V1DownserializeResult>`. Its immutable descriptor selects one domain/canonical type, current input schema/format, exact V1 primary alias, declared V1 source payload version and output format, and the code/options/identity/semantic validator hashes already bound into the registry fingerprint. `V1DownserializeResult` declares that exact domain, canonical type, alias, source version and format; a mismatch is `DownserializeRejected`. The pipeline passes only the private copying `IReadOnlyPayload`, a one-shot sealed output writer and charged scratch allocator described for E. The F writer enforces the provider's measured legacy readable ceiling (never above 64 MiB per event), the 128 MiB whole-result encoded cap and 128 MiB live scratch cap **during each write and before full byte-array allocation**; `Complete(): void` transfers no mutable owner. Cancellation, missing/second Complete, post-seal writes and allocation overflow stop before reservation, actor save or result exposure. After seal, validate exact alias-specific schema, format, aggregate identity policy and semantic round-trip equivalence on the private bytes. An F implementation cannot return an arbitrary buffer or rely on a late output-size check. `PrimaryWriteAlias` still bypasses F only when its registered source schema exactly matches the serialized current bytes.

## 4. Byte primitives and immutable stored evidence

All codecs below use UTF-8 without BOM or normalization, strict scalar decoding, checked big-endian `u16`/`u32`, signed big-endian `i16`/`i32`/`i64`, raw 32-byte SHA-256 values, exact ASCII separators ending in NUL, and no trailing bytes. `U` = u32 byte length plus UTF-8; `B` = u32 length plus bytes; `N` = signed i64; `I` = signed i32; `T` = UTC ticks N plus signed i16 original offset minutes (range -840..840, offset consistent with timestamp); `Q` = UTC instant ticks N; `O(X)` = `00` absent, `01` explicit null, `02` followed by X value. Required nullable data uses `O`; absent and null do not collapse. Map `M` = u32 count, then entries sorted by unsigned UTF-8 key bytes, each `01`+U key, `02`+one-byte `00` null or `01`+U value. Null map values are accepted only where the admitted legacy raw schema permits them; never coerce null to empty. Reject duplicate ordinal/case-insensitive names and unknown/duplicate tags. Each record is one-byte tag, separately encoded primary-key components, u16 field count, then ordered one-byte field tags with declared scalar encoding. A `B32` or hash field is exactly 32 bytes with no length. Limits charge encoded bytes before allocation. Every implementation/options H hashes a checked-in canonical UTF-8 LF JSON options manifest containing exact serializer, converter, validator, reader, signer settings and implementation IDs. Parse it with the strict §4 JSON rules, then compute `OptionsHash = SHA256("HX-EV-OPTIONS-1\0" || 01 || B(recursive canonical JSON **value** bytes))`; the value bytes use the same sorted recursive object renderer but **exclude** the raw event envelope separator, presence bitmap and provider fields. The checked-in file is one canonical JSON object serialized with unsigned UTF-8 sorted member names, no insignificant whitespace, §4 canonical plain-decimal numbers and exactly one final LF. JSON text escapes quote as `\"`, backslash as `\\`, U+0008/0009/000A/000C/000D as `\b`/`\t`/`\n`/`\f`/`\r`, and other U+0000..U+001F as lowercase `\u00xx`; every other scalar is literal UTF-8, with no slash or optional Unicode escaping. Parse and re-emit this text to require byte equality for the fields actually supplied. Each registered implementation ID owns a deterministic compile-time option schema embedded in its already hashed assembly: exact allowed names, JSON types, required/optional status and canonical defaults. Startup selects the schema by descriptor implementation ID, rejects duplicate/unknown/wrong-typed names **before** expansion, fills every missing optional field with its exact schema default into one canonical value tree, and only then computes OptionsHash over that expanded tree with the existing recursive codec. Runtime settings are compared to the same expanded tree, and defaults never depend on the host environment. A missing optional field and an explicit equal default MUST hash identically; §11.3 fixes a literal equality vector. A missing required field or differing runtime value fails readiness. The V17 `fixture` implementation permits exactly seven names. `implementationId` (string), `reader` (string), `serializer` (string), `signer` (string), `validator` (string) and `version` (integer) are **required**; their literal fixture values `fixture`, `v1`, `json-web`, `ECDSA-P256-SHA256-P1363`, `v1` and `1` are examples, not defaults. Only `converters` (array) is optional, with canonical default `[]`. The literal manifest supplies all seven and retains its exact hash; omitting only `converters` expands to the same bytes as explicit `[]`. Assembly-file H and options H are separate declared fields; the renderer implementation/options digest is included in S and S-read below.

The storage raw-presence bitmap v1 has **20 positions, five bytes**, two bits per position, low bits first within each byte: `00` absent, `01` explicit null, `10` value, `11` invalid. Positions 0..19 in order: `messageId`, `aggregateId`, `aggregateType`, `tenantId`, `domain`, `sequenceNumber`, `globalPosition`, `timestamp`, `correlationId`, `causationId`, `userId`, `domainServiceVersion`, `eventTypeName`, `metadataVersion`, `serializationFormat`, `payload`, `extensions`, `eventContractType`, `payloadVersion`, and nested `extensions.eventstore.protection`. For V1, map the raw known property spelling to these logical names with the pinned case-insensitive deserializer comparer while retaining the actual decoded spelling in the renderer; a collision between spellings is already rejected. V2 emits exactly these lower-camel JSON names. The final position is extracted from the raw extensions object, absent when its parent is absent/null; the same raw member is also included in the canonical renderer. All bits beyond position 19 are zero. Exact raw presence is checked before typed normalization. Persisted V2 new pair fields are values; V1 pair may be absent/null together.

Canonical raw envelope renderer v1 parses the complete bounded JSON envelope **before** typed deserialization, retaining original provider bytes separately. Before descent or typed binding it enforces at most 128 MiB raw bytes, depth 64 (root depth 1) and 1,000,000 nodes; charge each object member and array element before recursive work, including unknown V1 members, and return typed `RawEnvelopeLimit` on excess. At every object level, decode names to Unicode scalars, reject duplicate ordinal and `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` names, sort names by unsigned UTF-8 bytes, then encode u32 property count and each member as tag `01`+U decoded name followed by one discriminator and value: `00` null, `01` false, `02` true, `03` number, `04` string, `05` array, `06` object. Strings are U. Arrays use u32 count and recursively encoded elements; objects use the same sorted member form. Numbers parse finite JSON decimal syntax to exact base-10 coefficient/exponent, strip leading/trailing insignificant zeros, emit one canonical plain decimal U (negative zero becomes `0`, no exponent or plus); reject expansion beyond the raw ceiling. JSON whitespace, property order and escape spelling therefore do not alter canonical bytes. Unknown historical V1 members require a registered versioned decoder and are still rendered and hashed; unrecognized V2 members fail. The V1 decoder and every schema or identity validator consume only this canonical decoded JSON value tree, including canonical decimal numbers. They MUST NOT inspect original whitespace, escape spelling or numeric token text. An alias whose historical behavior depends on those spellings holds for separately approved evidence migration; equal StoredDigest may never authorize different read semantics. Renderer starts `HX-EV-RAW-1\0`, codec `01`, bitmap length `05`, five bitmap bytes, then the recursive root object. The original exact provider bytes are charged to a separate 128 MiB immutable source budget, while copied parser, renderer, canonicalization, deserialization and hop buffers are charged to the 128 MiB pipeline scratch budget. Stream/decode with bounded windows or an ephemeral non-authoritative spool, releasing stage buffers before the next hop; a maximum admitted raw envelope must be processable without a second full raw in-memory copy or breach of either budget. Plaintext spooling to an unprotected temporary file is forbidden. Any spool uses per-operation authenticated encryption in a private tenant-scoped directory, reserves its bounded disk quota and keeps its ephemeral key only in charged memory; close/delete on completion or cancellation, clear the key and scratch over full rented capacity, and on startup delete orphan ciphertext without treating it as authority. If encryption, access control, quota or crash cleanup cannot be proved, use streaming only or hold readiness. No spool byte is logged. Reject with a typed limit before allocation. Save readback compares exact provider bytes as well as digest. Each new writer stages an immutable actor-owned encoding-ID sidecar with the event in one actor save and reads back both exact raw event bytes and sidecar before the event can authorize a StoredDigest. Retained V1 history needs the authenticated sidecar protocol below; an ambiguous encoding ID blocks readiness for separately approved offline evidence migration. A provider switch never recalculates StoredDigest under its new ID. Original event bytes remain unchanged.

The encoding sidecar is keyed by the immutable event storage key plus `:encoding-evidence`, with one CAS-protected record per event. Its claim is the exact bytes `HX-EV-ENCODING-EVIDENCE-1\0 || 01 || 000a` followed by tags `01..0a` in order: U tenant, U domain, U aggregate ID, U aggregate type, U event key, N sequence, U provider encoding ID, B32 SHA-256 of exact original raw provider bytes, U original provider namespace, Q signed issuance UTC. Sign that claim with the §6 signature input using purpose `06`, one purpose-06 key in the per-domain TrustMapDigest, and fixed P1363 signature; persist claim bytes, key ID and 64-byte signature as an immutable sidecar; original signing UTC is tag `0a` inside the signed claim, never a separately editable timestamp. During a separately approved offline V1 inventory, trusted raw provider readback proves exact event bytes, key, scope, sequence and raw-byte hash, but never by itself proves the original provider encoding ID. That ID must be uniquely established from retained versioned provider configuration, authenticated deployment or commit logs, actor outbox metadata, or an independently authenticated store-generation mapping whose proven generation interval and serializer ID cover this event commit. The separately signed offline migration manifest below binds the chosen evidence source, interval or commit, encoding ID, raw event key/hash and original digest/decoder behavior. An independent reviewer verifies its exact bytes before the trusted signer issues tag `0a` UTC and the purpose-06 claim. CAS then writes/readbacks the sidecar without rewriting event bytes. If the historic encoding cannot be uniquely proved, reader activation holds for separately approved evidence migration; the current provider ID or JSON appearance is never a fallback. On every read, verify signature/purpose and signed tag `0a` issuance UTC under its retained original key interval and exact sidecar obligation time, addressed scope, event key, sequence, provider namespace and recomputed raw-byte hash before using the encoding ID in StoredDigest. A missing, ambiguous or mismatched sidecar or migration source holds readiness; no current-provider guess, JSON-shape inference or unsigned store-generation substitution is allowed. New writer staging/readback binds the same sidecar schema atomically to its event, and recovered ambiguous saves compare both records.

The offline V1 migration manifest is a **separate immutable evidence artifact**, not the purpose-06 sidecar and not caller JSON. Its canonical binary record is `HX-EV-V1-MIGRATION-1\0 || 01 || 001a`, followed by ordered tags `01..1a` under §4: `01` U tenant; `02` U domain; `03` U aggregate ID; `04` U aggregate type; `05` U exact event storage key; `06` N positive sequence; `07` B32 SHA-256 of exact original provider bytes; `08` U original provider namespace; `09` U historical provider encoding ID; `0a` U evidence source kind (exactly `versioned-config`, `deployment-log`, `commit-log`, `actor-outbox`, or `store-generation`); `0b` U authenticated source identity; `0c` O(Q) proven interval start UTC; `0d` O(Q) proven interval end UTC; `0e` O(U) exact commit/generation identity; `0f` B32 hash of the exact authenticated source evidence bytes; `10` B32 original StoredDigest; `11` U original protection/raw decoder ID; `12` I positive decoder version; `13` B32 original decoder assembly-file SHA-256; `14` B32 decoder expanded-options hash; `15` B32 sealed dependency-closure hash; `16` B32 DecoderBehaviorHash; `17` U stable migration signer identity; `18` U stable independent reviewer identity; `19` U preallocated immutable review docket ID; `1a` Q issuance UTC. For interval evidence, both `0c` and `0d` are values and enclose the independently authenticated event commit time, with start <= end; for a specific commit, `0e` is a value and its authenticated source record proves the exact event commit/encoding. A source may supply both, but neither an absent interval nor an absent commit identity proves an encoding. All scope/namespace/source IDs are nonempty strict UTF-8 and checked against actor/provider readback. The record is capped at 64 KiB before allocation; each U/B is checked under that whole-record cap. Unknown source kinds, duplicate/out-of-order tags, impossible time/sequence or trailing bytes fail.

`DecoderBehaviorHash = SHA256("HX-EV-DECODER-BEHAVIOR-1\0" || 01 || U decoder ID || I decoder version || B32 assembly hash || B32 expanded-options hash || B32 sealed dependency-closure hash)`. The sealed closure is encoded with §5 G row keys/fields, rooted at the selected raw renderer, protection selector, derived-protection decoder and their transitive managed/native dependencies; hash its exact sorted bytes with `SHA256("HX-EV-DECODER-CLOSURE-1\0" || 01 || B(exact closure bytes))`. The retained allow-listed decoder table is keyed by `(decoder ID, version, DecoderBehaviorHash)` and keeps the exact executable bytes/options/dependency closure for every open historic event obligation. Equal ID/version with changed behavior hash never selects replacement code for old evidence. A missing old executable/closure or a mismatch holds reader activation/readiness; it cannot be repaired by recomputing StoredDigest under a newer decoder.

`MigrationDigest = SHA256(exact complete 26-field manifest record)`. The exact approval claim is `HX-EV-MIGRATION-DIGEST-1\0 || 01 || B32 MigrationDigest`; its two independent signatures use §6 `HX-EV-SIG-1\0` input with new purposes `0d` (trusted offline migration authority) and `0e` (independent reviewer authority), ECDSA P-256/SHA-256 fixed 64-byte P1363. The immutable carrier is `B(exact manifest) || U(migration key ID) || B(exact 64-byte signature) || U(reviewer key ID) || B(exact 64-byte signature)`, capped at 65 KiB before allocation. The two purpose-specific SPKI trust maps, valid-from/to UTC intervals, revocation state and authorized identity mappings are separately pinned offline migration configuration; neither uses the event-route TrustMapDigest nor changes the V17 fixture. Signer/reviewer keys and identities must differ; tag `1a` and authenticated review source time must fall within both key intervals and agree with the actor identities and exact MigrationDigest. The review docket exists with a stable ID before the manifest is encoded, avoiding a digest-dependent ID cycle. The reviewer retrieves the source evidence independently, checks its hash, uniqueness of historical encoding and decoder bytes/closure, then CAS-records a separate immutable authenticated approval receipt under that docket ID containing MigrationDigest, reviewer identity, UTC time and exact approval decision. The reviewer signature is issued only after that receipt is read back; the migration signer independently reads the same receipt. A signer cannot supply its own reviewer evidence. CAS-create/read back the manifest carrier and the version-2 ten-field stored-digest sidecar below under the original event key before signing/accepting its purpose-06 encoding sidecar. An existing manifest or sidecar with different bytes is `LegacyEvidenceConflict` and holds; an ambiguous CAS is reconciled by exact readback, never overwritten. Every read checks both signatures, review evidence, actor source, raw-byte hash, sidecar/manifest equality and retained decoder hash before the original StoredDigest authorizes a route. Failed verification preserves original event bytes and blocks reader cutover; diagnostics expose only source kind, reason, scope and hashes, not source content or payload.

Retention is an authority condition, not a best-effort archive. Preserve the exact authenticated offline source bytes, signed manifest carrier, independently authenticated reviewer docket/decision, original decoder executable/options/transitive managed-native dependency bytes (or immutable content-addressed references resolving to exactly those verified bytes), original purpose-0d/0e and purpose-06 verification keys and their key-interval/revocation records for at least every event's readable lifetime **plus** any outstanding backup, rollback, queue/delivery or incident-replay obligation. The retention index is keyed by original event key and manifest digest; CAS/readback binds every referenced content digest and obligation horizon, forbids early garbage collection, and checks exact referenced bytes and keys on **every** historic recheck. A reference that cannot be resolved, a changed source/reviewer record, expired necessary evidence or altered decoder bytes is `LegacyEvidenceConflict`/reader-readiness hold with original history intact, never a new derivation or silent acceptance.

`StoredDigest = SHA256("HX-EV-STORED-1\0" || 01 || B(canonical raw renderer) || O(B(decoded eventstore.protection JSON string scalar UTF-8)) || U(provider encoding ID) || B32(derived protection record hash))`. The raw extension selector is `00` when the exact key is absent, `01` for JSON null, and `02`+B of the decoded JSON string scalar UTF-8 bytes for a string value; an empty string is `02 00000000`. JSON escapes are decoded, then re-encoded as strict UTF-8 without Unicode normalization, so `"a"` and `"\u0061"` have identical selector bytes. A non-string/non-null value is malformed raw-envelope evidence; it follows the authenticated raw-corrupt path below instead of inventing a string selector or StoredDigest. The protection value retains absent/null/empty distinctions; source evidence also includes a derived typed protection record with fixed fields `(state, metadata version, scheme, key alias, content hint, O(M) flags)` and its SHA-256. An absent protection extension derives the current `Legacy()` compatibility record: `Unprotected`, metadata version 1, null scheme/key alias/content hint and present flags map `legacy=missing`; the raw extension remains absent. Unknown/malformed protection becomes fixed ProviderOpaque metadata **version 1**, null optionals, stable reason code, retaining original extension. `O(M)` distinguishes absent, null and empty flags. A decoder change is governed by EventTransformHash. Exact raw bytes are used for actor readback/transport proof, while canonical renderer makes JSON spelling irrelevant to digest. A bounded deterministic provider serializer prepares exact raw bytes, presence, protection output and proposed digest **before** global MessageId reservation; if a provider cannot preserve/preflight/read back them or enforce a 128 MiB raw bound before allocation, V2 write readiness fails.

The original `StoredDigest` is immutable evidence, not a digest to recompute under the newest parser. With every new event, the actor stages an additional immutable `:stored-digest-evidence` sidecar in the **same save** as the raw event, encoding sidecar, outbox intent and result. Its exact version-2 record is `HX-EV-STORED-EVIDENCE-1\0 || 02 || 000a` with ordered tags `01` U event storage key, `02` U tenant, `03` U domain, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` N sequence, `06` B32 SHA-256 of exact original provider bytes, `07` B32 original StoredDigest, `08` U protection-derivation codec ID, `09` I positive codec version, `0a` B32 original DecoderBehaviorHash. The earlier nine-field draft shape is inadmissible to the evolved reader; valid retained history requires the independently signed migration manifest and a CAS-pinned version-2 sidecar before reader activation; authenticated raw-corrupt items follow their separate unreadable cutover disposition. It is included in the actor bundle hash and exact readback. The codec ID/version selects immutable original raw renderer, protection selector and derived-record behavior from a retained allow-listed decoder table; missing implementation bytes or a changed decoder under the same identity is a readiness hold. Valid retained V1 history obtains those ten values, including the original behavior hash, from the authenticated signed offline migration manifest and CAS-pinned version-2 sidecar before its purpose-06 encoding sidecar is accepted. Historic read verifies original raw-byte hash and original StoredDigest using the pinned decoder ID/version/DecoderBehaviorHash and original provider encoding ID, comparing to the immutable sidecar/manifest; it never silently substitutes a new current derivation. A later parser may change **effective** semantics only through a changed `EventTransformHash` and full replay/rebuild. Missing, ambiguous or conflicting old digest/decoder evidence holds readiness and preserves bytes and old delivery pin; it is never repaired by hashing under current code.

The derived protection record is `HX-EV-PROTECTION-1\0`, codec `01`, u16 count `0006`, tags `01` U state (`Unprotected`, `Protected`, or `ProviderOpaque`), `02` I metadata version, `03` O(U) scheme, `04` O(U) key alias, `05` O(U) content hint, `06` O(M) flags. Its hash is SHA-256 over those complete bytes. For ProviderOpaque fallback, version is exactly 1, optional fields null, and flags contain only the stable classified reason when supplied; it does not use the current parser's version. Null, absent and empty remain distinct even if existing typed helpers normalize them.

When invalid UTF-8, duplicate JSON names or a non-object root prevents StoredDigest, the gateway authenticates the **exact raw source independently**: addressed actor/event key, trusted provider readback, immutable sequence/head and raw-byte SHA-256. It does not fabricate StoredDigest or Apply. Only a complete authenticated permanent corrupt source receives durable AD-31 capture containing raw-byte hash, scope, sequence and reason before subscription acknowledgement. An untrusted or incomplete provider read holds/retries with no acknowledgement. Raw source evidence is retained without exposing bytes in diagnostics.

For retained raw bytes so corrupt that the pinned decoder cannot form `StoredDigest`, a separately reviewed **cutover inventory** may record `RawCorruptDisposition`; it never asserts an origin, encoding, route or digest. Its immutable canonical bytes are `HX-EV-RAW-CORRUPT-1\0 || 01 || 000d` with ordered §4 tags: `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U aggregate type, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` U exact event storage key, `06` N positive sequence, `07` B32 SHA-256 of exact raw provider bytes, `08` N authenticated actor head, `09` U actor ETag, `0a` B32 authenticated provider ReadbackProof hash, `0b` U durable AD-31 capture key, `0c` B32 operator-approved migration-decision evidence hash and `0d` Q decision UTC. The complete disposition record is capped at 64 KiB before allocation. The provider source, capture and independently authenticated operator decision are read back and cross-checked against exact event key/scope/sequence/raw-byte hash before this record can close **inventory** for that one corrupt item. The disposition is immutable under create-once CAS; changed bytes, actor head/proof or decision conflict. It does not make the event readable, authorize a synthetic StoredDigest/V1 origin/route, permit Apply/handler, advance any checkpoint or silently acknowledge any delivery. Replay stops exactly there with typed `RawEnvelopeCorrupt` and last-good nonauthoritative state/checkpoint; subscriber poison acknowledgement still requires the separate AD-31 authenticated capture-before-ack rule. Missing capture, proof or approved decision holds cutover. Every valid retained event still requires full signed manifest, digest sidecar and origin evidence.

Every retained or newly written V1 event has exactly one immutable, event-storage-key-indexed origin record. Its canonical bytes are `HX-EV-V1-ORIGIN-1\0 || 01 || 0007` followed by tags `01` U event storage key, `02` U tenant, `03` U domain, `04` N sequence, `05` B32 raw-event SHA-256, `06` B32 original StoredDigest, `07` B origin branch. Tag `07` is exactly `B(01 || B32 actor-save-certificate hash || B32 exact operation-result/bundle hash)` for `new-writer`, proving that raw event, purpose-06 encoding sidecar, stored-digest sidecar and result were staged and read back in the same actor save. For `retained-offline` it is exactly `B(02 || B32 independently signed migration-manifest digest || B32 reviewer-docket/decision hash || B32 authenticated source-evidence hash || B32 version-2 stored-digest-sidecar hash)`. Any other discriminator or branch length fails. The branch discriminator and fields are immutable under create-once CAS and included in authenticated raw readback. Exactly one branch must verify against the event key, raw bytes, sidecars and StoredDigest before source admission. A new V1 append uses branch `01` and never requires an offline manifest. Historical bytes cannot claim branch `01` without their actor commit certificate; an offline record cannot masquerade as an actor save. Missing, duplicated, swapped or conflicting origin evidence is `LegacyEvidenceConflict` and leaves the event unreadable pending reconciliation without rewriting it. The purpose-06 sidecar remains the exact historical ten-field signed format in §4; this origin record does not alter its claim bytes or the V17 literal fixture.

For new-writer branch `01`, define the immutable **pre-save intent certificate** as `HX-EV-ACTOR-INTENT-1\0 || 01 || 0008`: tags `01` U actor state key, `02` U tenant, `03` U domain, `04` U aggregate type, `05` U aggregate ID, `06` U OperationId, `07` N predeclared checked actor logical version and `08` B ordered intended records. The tag-`08` B is u32 record count and one `(one-byte kind, U exact storage key, B32 SHA-256 of exact staged bytes)` per event, encoding sidecar, stored-digest sidecar, outbox intent and immutable domain/append result in deterministic event order, with one result row; duplicate/missing kinds or keys fail. The branch-`01` second B32 is `PreSaveBundleHash = SHA256("HX-EV-ACTOR-PRESAVE-BUNDLE-1\0" || 01 || B exact ordered event/encoding/digest/outbox/result staged bytes)`, computed before save without origin, certificate, provider ETag or commit token; it is distinct from the post-save `ActorBundleReadbackHash`. This certificate deliberately excludes the origin record, resulting provider ETag and provider commit token; calculate its hash before save, set origin branch-`01` certificate hash to that value, and stage both certificate and origin in the same actor save. The separate authenticated provider receipt is `HX-EV-ACTOR-COMMIT-1\0 || 01 || 0009`: `01` U actor key, `02` U OperationId, `03` N actual logical version, `04` U backend identity, `05` B exact provider fence, `06` U resulting ETag, `07` N committed provider generation, `08` B32 pre-save certificate hash and `09` B ordered actual `(kind, storage key, SHA-256)` rows covering **every** staged record, including certificate and origin. The complete encoded certificate and receipt are each capped at 2 MiB and checked before allocation; inability to represent an admitted batch holds writer readiness before save. Only **after** the actor save commits does the provider issue and sign this purpose-`11` receipt under the §6 backend-bound carrier; the receipt is not one of its own staged records, contains no self-hash, and is read back under the committed save fence/generation. A pre-save signature, guessed ETag or merely co-stored receipt cannot certify the save. Verifier compares every intended hash to actual save/readback, verifies branch-`01` origin's certificate hash and result/bundle hash, and checks actual ETag/generation/fence before trusting the event. A bundle hash alone, separate saves or provider inability to certify same-save atomicity is `ActorCommitEvidenceHold`; old offline branch `02` never claims this certificate. For a zero-event no-op the same intent/receipt pair binds the staged no-op result and command-idempotency checkpoint/completion witness rows under one save, with no event, event-sidecar, outbox or V1 origin row. Both rows appear in the intent certificate and the post-save provider receipt; neither record lists that receipt itself. Preserve the exact purpose-11 backend-bound SPKI bytes, key ID, validity and revocation intervals, signer implementation/options and original binding for every open new-V1 read, backup, rollback and delivery obligation. Rotate verifier-first; missing or changed historic verification bytes produce `ActorCommitEvidenceHold`, not a new receipt or decoder choice.

`MigrationAuthorityDigest = SHA256("HX-EV-MIGRATION-AUTHORITY-1\0" || 01 || u32 rowCount || sorted rows)`. Each row is `HX-EV-MIGRATION-AUTHORITY-ROW-1\0 || 01 || 0009` with ordered tags `01` U domain, `02` one-byte purpose (`0d` signer or `0e` independent reviewer), `03` U key ID, `04` B32 SHA-256 of exact SPKI bytes, `05` Q valid-from UTC, `06` Q valid-to UTC, `07` one-byte revocation state (`00` active, `01` revoked), `08` U stable permitted human/service identity, `09` B32 canonical signer/reviewer identity-policy/configuration hash. Sort rows by separately encoded domain, purpose and key-ID bytes in unsigned UTF-8 order; reject duplicate keys, unknown tags, invalid intervals and a signer/reviewer identity or key reused for both roles on one docket. The actual SPKI bytes and exact identity policy are retained alongside the digest and compared to each row before a manifest is admitted. Every reader and publisher that can validate migrated V1 advertises this 32-byte digest **beside** its active per-domain RegistryFingerprint and requires parity before mixed-reader cutover. It is not folded retroactively into the historical V17 RegistryFingerprint or purpose-06 signature. Rotate migration verification keys before signing under the new map, retain old map bytes and valid original intervals for open event/backup/rollback/delivery obligations, and hold source admission/readiness when the advertised digest, retained map or independently authenticated signer/reviewer identity differs.

## 5. Registry and compatibility fingerprints

Each deployment computes `RegistryFingerprint = SHA256("HX-EV-REGISTRY-1\0" || 01 || u32 row-count || sorted rows)` for one domain.
