Row order is one-byte record tag then separately encoded primary-key components compared as unsigned UTF-8 bytes or big-endian numeric version; duplicate primary keys and tags fail. In the table, comma order assigns **field tags `01`, `02`, ... exactly**, each followed by its declared scalar; `H` means B32 SHA-256 of exact implementation assembly-file bytes or canonical options bytes, never a display version. Primary keys precede u16 field count and are not repeated in that count.

| Row tag / primary key encoding | Exact fields in tag order; count |
| --- | --- |
| `44` D: U domain, U canonical type | `U aggregateRouteId`, `I currentVersion`, `U current CLR assembly-qualified type`, `H current type assembly`, `U Apply implementation ID`, `H Apply assembly`, `I state schema version`, `B state schema descriptor`, `U state serializer ID`, `H state serializer assembly`, `H state serializer options`, `H StateSchemaApplyHash`; 12. All D rows of one aggregate route agree on state/Apply fields. |
| `56` V: U domain, U canonical type, I version | `U schema validator ID`, `H validator assembly`, `H validator options`, `U serializer ID`, `H serializer assembly`, `H serializer options`, `U exact format`, `U identity policy ID`, `H identity extractor assembly`, `H identity options`; 10. |
| `41` A: U domain, U exact alias | `U canonical type`, `I source version`, `U source format`, `U source serializer ID`, `H source serializer assembly`, `H source serializer options`, `U alias validator ID`, `H alias validator assembly`, `H alias validator options`, `U identity policy ID`, `H identity extractor assembly`, `H identity options`, `U V1 unknown-member decoder ID`, `H decoder assembly`, `H decoder options`; 15. An alias with no unknown members uses a registered fixed no-op decoder, never an omitted field. |
| `45` E: U domain, U canonical type, I source version | `I target version` (exactly source+1), `U input format`, `U output format`, `U serializer ID`, `H serializer assembly`, `H serializer options`, `U upcaster ID`, `H upcaster assembly`, `H upcaster options`, `U edge identity policy ID`, `H edge identity assembly`, `H edge identity options`; 12. |
| `46` F: U domain, U canonical type, U write alias | `I current version`, `I source version`, `B current schema descriptor`, `B source schema descriptor`, `U input format`, `U output format`, `U downserializer ID`, `H downserializer assembly`, `H downserializer options`, `U identity validator ID`, `H identity validator assembly`, `H identity validator options`, `U semantic validator ID`, `H semantic validator assembly`, `H semantic validator options`; 15. |
| `53` S: U domain, O(U) empty component | `H TrustMapDigest`, `U raw envelope validator ID`, `H validator assembly`, `H validator options`, `U chain executor ID`, `H executor assembly`, `H executor options`, `U JSON serializer ID`, `H serializer assembly`, `H serializer options`, `U bounded raw reader ID`, `H reader assembly`, `H reader options`, `U protection reader ID`, `H protection reader assembly`, `H protection reader options`, `U attestation verifier ID`, `H verifier assembly`, `H verifier options`, `U signer ID`, `H signer assembly`, `H signer options`, `U publisher ID`, `H publisher assembly`, `H publisher options`, `U marker/reservation protocol ID`, `H protocol assembly`, `H protocol options`, `U raw renderer implementation ID`, `H renderer assembly`, `H renderer options`; 31. The appended renderer fields are tags `1d..1f`; existing S tags remain unchanged. |

Every assembly-file H in D/V/A/E/F/S and every handler, registration-adapter, filter, effect-receipt-provider and readback-codec H in the consumer catalog is the hash of the named direct file. A shared sealed per-domain deployment dependency-closure manifest adds one G record for every transitively loadable managed assembly or native library that can affect those implementations, even when another descriptor also names that file directly; deduplicate by exact `(domain, logical load identity, dependency kind)`. The root set includes D/V/A/E/F/S plus every cataloged handler, verified or legacy registration adapter, filter, effect receipt provider and readback codec. The gateway-owned per-domain deployment capability record pins the same sealed manifest bytes and RegistryFingerprint for all serving peers, even when one process does not locally instantiate a cataloged route. Each process verifies exact bytes and closure of every root it executes; for nonlocal rows it compares the complete canonical row bytes to that pinned capability record, and the endpoint that executes each handler proves its local bytes match those same rows. A missing locally executable dependency, absent manifest attestation or mismatched bytes blocks capability instead of producing a narrower RegistryFingerprint. The same sorted G rows enter RegistryFingerprint and the affected route-specific HandlerCompatibilityHash or EventTransformHash subset; a handler-only G row need not enter an unrelated aggregate transform. G has row tag `47`, primary key `U domain || U logical load identity || U dependency kind` (kind exactly `managed` or `native`), u16 count `0003`, tags `01` U resolved version/ABI identity, `02` B32 SHA-256 of exact dependency file bytes, `03` U deterministic loader context ID. Sort G with other registry rows by tag/key bytes. The manifest is generated from the actual resolved load graph and locked at startup; verify every file's bytes and graph edges, reject duplicate logical identities, unresolved/dynamic reflection loads, unmanifested native loads and any newly loaded implementation dependency until a new fingerprint is published. Runtime and framework dependencies that influence validation, serialization, Apply, protection, signing or marker behavior are included. `EventTransformHash` appends only G rows reachable from its applicable D/V/A/E/S-read/protection implementation roots, and its row count includes them; `HandlerCompatibilityHash` appends only G rows reachable from that route/filter/readback roots after the selected filter rows, with a u32 G-row count even when zero. A fixture whose implementations and all relevant dependencies are the one literal `fixture-assembly\n` has no additional G rows and retains the hashes below. Thus peers with different transitive behavior cannot advertise one registry or semantic hash. S has **31** fields as enumerated; optional empty key is encoded `00`, never an ambiguous concatenated tuple. All hash inputs use exact file bytes/configured canonical option bytes; missing files/options block readiness. `TrustMapDigest` is `SHA256("HX-EV-TRUST-1\0" || 01 || u32 count || rows)`. Sort rows by separately encoded `(domain,purpose,key ID)`; each row is tag `4b`, `U domain`, one-byte purpose, `U key ID`, u16 field count `0006`, then tags `01` B32 SPKI SHA-256, `02` Q valid-from, `03` Q valid-to, `04` byte revocation (00 active/01 revoked), `05` U signer algorithm (`ECDSA-P256-SHA256-P1363`), `06` H signer options. Unknown, duplicate or malformed tags/keys fail. Key ID is ASCII lowercase kebab case 1–64; trust lookup is scoped to domain and purpose. Signer/trust changes alter RegistryFingerprint and require verifier-first transition, but do not change read-state semantic hash by themselves.

A domain registry and consumer-handler catalog are each admitted only after checked preallocation accounting: every encoded row is at most **64 KiB**, one domain manifest has at most **65,536 rows** and **64 MiB total encoded bytes**, including names, options, selected G closure, framing and sort workspace. A row's referenced option/dependency bytes must have immutable, bounded content-addressed storage and count toward that manifest's charged closure; unresolved or dynamic dependency graphs fail readiness. Parse, sort and hash through bounded streaming or charged scratch; never materialize an unbounded whole graph. `RegistryLimit` is the typed startup/readiness result for count, byte or row overflow. The same limits apply to route/filter/capability rows before either RegistryFingerprint or HandlerCatalogFingerprint is advertised. The existing fixture-only zero-additional-G-row V17 manifest remains byte-for-byte unchanged.

`StateSchemaApplyHash = SHA256("HX-EV-STATE-APPLY-1\0" || 01 || U aggregateRouteId || I state schema version || B descriptor || U state serializer ID || H serializer assembly || H serializer options || U Apply ID || H Apply assembly)`. `EventTransformHash = SHA256("HX-EV-TRANSFORM-1\0" || 01 || U domain || U aggregateRouteId || u32 row-count || sorted applicable D/V/A/E rows || B32 StateSchemaApplyHash || S-read || protection-adapter record || sorted selected G rows)`, where row-count is **the applicable D/V/A/E count plus two plus selected G count**. The G rows occur exactly once, immediately after the protection-adapter record, sorted by their separately encoded primary key `(domain, logical load identity, dependency kind)` in unsigned byte order; no G bytes are interleaved with D/V/A/E or placed before the fixed state hash. The state hash is a fixed scalar, not a row. A peer or fixture verifier MUST reject a row count that does not equal the number of D/V/A/E, S-read, protection-adapter and G row byte strings actually concatenated. Applicable rows are selected from the immutable manifest-admitted source types/versions reachable on that aggregate route, independent of each peer's observed corpus or retention state; write-only F is excluded unless historical output is an admitted A alias, in which case A/V/E already bind read behavior. `S-read` is tag `73`, codec `01`, u16 count `0012`, fields `01..0f`: S raw validator ID/assembly/options, chain executor ID/assembly/options, JSON serializer ID/assembly/options, bounded reader ID/assembly/options, protection reader ID/assembly/options, each with the same U/H types as S; appended fields `10..12` are raw renderer implementation ID U, renderer assembly H and renderer options H. Existing S-read tags retain their meanings. Protection adapter record is tag `70`, codec `01`, count `0003`, tags `01` U implementation ID, `02` H assembly, `03` H options. No trust, signer, verifier, publisher, marker or handler-only field enters this semantic hash. Changing a read implementation invalidates compatible folded state; key rotation alone does not.

Consumer `HandlerCatalogFingerprint = SHA256("HX-EV-HANDLERS-1\0" || 01 || u32 count || sorted rows)`, separate from the shared domain registry. Route primary key is **`(domain, HandlerRouteId)`**, unique within domain, stable under CLR rename. Route row tag `52`, keys U domain/U route ID, u16 count `0012`, tags `01..12`: U canonical type, U topic, U tenant-scope rule, U handler implementation ID, H handler assembly, H handler config, U effect mode, U effect contract ID, H effect options, I checkpoint state schema version, B checkpoint descriptor, U state serializer ID, H serializer assembly, H serializer options, U readback codec ID, H readback assembly, H readback options, U state store ID. Filter row tag `4c`, key U domain/U filter ID, count `0007`, tags `01..07`: U exact domain predicate, U exact topic predicate, U exact canonical type predicate, U exact tenant-scope rule, U implementation ID, H assembly, H config. Predicates are catalog literals or the one frozen tenant-scope wildcard rule; reject overlapping or ambiguous selectors, never evaluate arbitrary runtime code for hash selection. A verified handler has exactly one additional capability row tag `43`, primary key U domain/U route ID, u16 count `000c`, tags `01..0c`: U route kind (exact `projection` or `subscription`), U projection type (empty for subscription), U canonical current type, I current payload version, U current CLR handler assembly-qualified type (internal only), U DI registration implementation ID, H registration assembly, H registration options, U effect mode (exact `AtomicStore` or `ProviderIdempotency`), U receipt provider ID, H receipt provider assembly, H receipt provider options. A `43` row without its matching `52` route row, a second `43` key, mismatched canonical type/effect mode, missing receipt probe or a current version other than the D descriptor fails startup. The catalog sort includes `43` by tag/key; a route with no `43` row is legacy-only and cannot claim verified current-event capability. `HandlerCompatibilityHash = SHA256("HX-EV-HANDLER-ROUTE-1\0" || 01 || exact route row || u32 selected filter-count || selected filter rows sorted by filter ID || u32 selected G-row count || selected G rows sorted by primary key || selected capability row if present)`. This preserves the existing no-capability-row preimage, including the signed V17 fixture; a verified route appends its one exact self-delimiting `43` row after G. Select only filters whose literal domain/topic/type and tenant-scope rule name that route, and only the capability row with the same domain/route key; unrelated handler/filter changes cannot invalidate its checkpoint. The selected G closure includes the route handler, verified or legacy registration adapter, selected filter implementation, effect receipt provider, state serializer and readback codec dependencies, plus the capability row's registration and receipt provider dependencies. Select these from the identical shared G rows bound by RegistryFingerprint; duplicate rows are never reencoded with another key or hash. Whole catalog fingerprint remains deployment inventory. Route rename needs explicit marker/effect migration; old key interpretation is retained until proven safe.

Production `52` route tag `06` handler-config bytes and tag `11` readback-codec options MUST each bind the same canonical state-backend identity descriptor `(providerKind, backendInstanceOrClusterId, namespace, endpointConfigurationDigest, storeId)`. Encode it as `HX-EV-STATE-BACKEND-1\0 || 01 || U providerKind || U backendInstanceOrClusterId || U namespace || B32 endpointConfigurationDigest || U storeId`, with nonempty identifiers, strict UTF-8 and no trailing bytes; include those exact bytes in the canonical configuration manifests before their H values are calculated. The provider's authenticated readback reports the same five values and the gateway compares them before checkpoint signing or reuse. Since `HandlerCompatibilityHash` binds the `52` row/config and purpose-04 binds that hash plus state-store ID, an equal friendly name or equal state bytes on another backend cannot authorize reuse. A backend move changes the hash and requires authenticated migration or rebuild. The unchanged V17 literal `store` is a declared **test-local** backend in its existing fixture handler config/options and retains the fixture preimage; production configuration cannot use that shorthand and must provide the full descriptor. No checkpoint claim tag or V17 fixture byte changes.

A projection route declares a nonempty, unique set of current `(canonical event type, PayloadVersion, SerializationFormat)` capabilities. Its existing `52` route row remains the **primary** type; the primary version and format are the current V descriptor for that type in the active domain registry. An extra capability is the self-delimiting row tag `58`, primary key `U domain || U HandlerRouteId || U canonical event type || I current payload version || U current format`, followed by u16 field count `0000`; sort extra rows by those separately encoded unsigned key bytes. Every extra type must resolve to exactly one current D/V descriptor with that version/format, differ from the primary triple and every other extra row, and belong to the route's domain. The `43` verified capability row still describes the primary entry; extra rows share its handler/effect/registration contract. Include each `58` in `HandlerCatalogFingerprint`'s sorted row count and append this route's sorted extra rows after its optional `43` row in `HandlerCompatibilityHash`. With zero extras append **no** count, marker or bytes, preserving the exact V17 no-extra-type preimage. `EventRouteCapability` is an additive immutable record with exactly `EventContractType:string`, `PayloadVersion:int` and `SerializationFormat:string`; its three values are validated against the active D/V current descriptor. The existing single-type `AddVerifiedDomainProjectionHandler` registration delegates to an additive overload accepting a nonempty immutable `IReadOnlyList<EventRouteCapability>`; its primary entry is the old argument and the rest are these extra rows. Registration and discovery attest the whole set and reject overlap/ambiguity. For every event on every live, retry, history, reconciliation and shared-rebuild page, validate its verified effective `(type, version, format)` against the selected route set **before** Apply, handler invocation, history fingerprint or checkpoint. One unmatched event yields `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` with no partial handler result or checkpoint advance. A multi-type route never treats its primary type as permission to skip another page event.

A production named-projection key-space declaration is a catalog capability row tag `59`, primary key `U domain || U HandlerRouteId || U keySpaceId`, followed by u16 field count `0005` and ascending tags: `01` U exact logical-key prefix, `02` U ownership mode (only `aggregate` or `shared`), `03` U state store ID, `04` B exact §5 canonical state-backend descriptor, `05` H canonical declaration/configuration options manifest. The prefix is nonempty strict UTF-8; it selects all and only keys beginning with those exact bytes. Equal or prefix-overlapping declarations in the same `(tenant, domain, store ID)` across any route fail startup; declaration changes cannot silently reinterpret an existing key. Include every `59` row in the sorted `HandlerCatalogFingerprint` count and append that route's sorted `59` rows after its optional `43` and any `58` rows in `HandlerCompatibilityHash`, including the selected declaration implementation/configuration and transitive G dependency rows in the hash's selected closure. A route with no declared key-space appends no row/count/marker and preserves the V17 literal preimage. A changed prefix, key-space ID, ownership mode, backend or dependency requires authenticated migration of all retained roots/checkpoints or a complete fenced rebuild before the route is current; a prior checkpoint hash alone never authorizes the new ownership interpretation.

## 6. Signed route, prefix, checkpoint and command evidence

Signatures use ECDSA P-256/SHA-256, fixed 64-byte IEEE P1363, exact SPKI trust map and no alternate encoding. Signature input is `"HX-EV-SIG-1\0" || 01 || one-byte purpose || 01+U keyID || 02+B claim`; purpose `01` route, `02` delivery, `03` complete prefix, `04` checkpoint, `05` command continuation, `06` immutable provider-encoding evidence, `07` completed command state, `08` broker membership, `09` consumer lease, `0a` broker publication fence, `0b` external route capability manifest, `0c` provider duplicate-probe transcript. Purposes `08..0b` use the separately configured broker/component authority trust map in §7; purpose `0c` uses the separately pinned provider receipt-attestation authority and is valid only for the exact backend/configuration revision in the V3 probe; purposes `0d` and `0e` use separate offline migration and independent reviewer trust maps over the same §4 MigrationDigest; purpose `0f` is the route/key-space scoped named-maintenance authority under the current domain key and fingerprint; purpose `10` is the trusted provider raw-readback claim and purpose `11` is its actor atomic-commit/readback receipt under a separately configured backend-bound provider trust anchor. Neither provider purpose authorizes a caller-constructed source, event route, delivery or command state. Purpose 07 is transient and requires the current active domain key/fingerprint; command-serving domains register its key in `TrustMapDigest`. Claim encoding starts its named separator, codec `01`, u16 field count, then tags `01..N` in table order. Reject missing, extra, duplicate, out-of-order tags. Verify purpose and domain for every claim; route, prefix and continuation require the current active key/fingerprint, while only exact delivery-pin, checkpoint and immutable encoding-sidecar obligations may verify an approved prior fingerprint and original signing interval. Route and complete-prefix claims are transient. They authorize state only while their signature verifies under the **current active domain key and RegistryFingerprint** at verification time; rotation or expiry requires authenticated source replay and new current-key claims, not a historic obligation. Purpose-05 continuation has signed issued/expiry UTC and is similarly invalid under a no-longer-current key/fingerprint, requiring replay from page 1. Purpose-02 delivery pins, purpose-04 checkpoint attestations and purpose-06 encoding sidecars have separate exact durable historic obligations and narrowly scoped expired-key rules; an old sidecar key verifies only its original immutable claim/raw-byte binding, never a new route or prefix. A route claim is capped at **1 MiB encoded** and streamed/charged before allocation, accommodating admitted metadata up to the 512 KiB per-event ceiling; historical larger cases hold readiness for separately approved offline evidence migration, never truncate/poison solely for a new proof cap.

| Claim separator / count | Tags `01..N` and exact scalar types |
| --- | --- |
| `HX-EV-ROUTE-1\0` / 18 | B32 StoredDigest; U tenant; U domain; U aggregate ID; U aggregate type; N sequence; U MessageId; U stored event type name; I stored metadata version; O(U) stored canonical type; O(I) stored payload version; U stored format; U target canonical type; I target version; B32 RegistryFingerprint; B32 effective-payload SHA-256; U effective format; B32 consumed-metadata hash. |
| `HX-EV-DELIVERY-CLAIM-1\0` / 10 | B32 StoredDigest; B32 DeliveryDigest; U tenant; U domain; U aggregate ID; U aggregate type; N sequence; U MessageId; B32 RegistryFingerprint; Q signed issuance UTC. |
| `HX-EV-PREFIX-1\0` / 15 | U tenant; U domain; U aggregate ID; U aggregate type; N start; N end; N admitted head; N target; I count; B32 OrderedDigestListHash; B32 cumulative accumulator; B32 RegistryFingerprint; O(B32 checkpoint anchor hash); O(B32 SnapshotDigest); O(B32 snapshot-prefix accumulator). Tags `0e` and `0f` are appended; existing tags retain their meaning. |
| `HX-EV-CHECKPOINT-1\0` / 18 | U tenant; U domain; U aggregate ID; U aggregate type; U projection route; N completed sequence; B32 accumulator; B32 body hash; B32 StateCheckpointReadbackHash; B32 EventTransformHash; B32 HandlerCompatibilityHash; B32 RegistryFingerprint; U readback ETag for legacy single-state, or the pre-reserved logical state version for production named root; Q signed issuance UTC; U fence coordinator ID; N monotonic fence generation; U state operation ID; U state commit version/ETag (the same pre-reserved logical version for named root). The actual named-root final-pointer ETag is bound only in the provider-issued final-pointer commit receipt after pointer acceptance. |
| `HX-EV-COMMAND-CONT-1\0` / 16 | U tenant; U domain; U aggregate ID; U aggregate type; U stream key; N fixed expected head; N fixed target; N next sequence; B32 RegistryFingerprint; B32 EventTransformHash; B32 cumulative prior-prefix accumulator; U operation ID; Q issued UTC; Q expires UTC; B 16-byte nonce; B32 cumulative effective-event chain. Tag `10` is appended and is mandatory for every newly issued purpose-05 token. |
| `HX-EV-COMMAND-STATE-1\0` / 17 | U tenant; U domain; U aggregate ID; U aggregate type; U stream key; N fixed expected head; N target; B32 RegistryFingerprint; B32 EventTransformHash; B32 StateSchemaApplyHash; B32 final prefix accumulator; B32 SHA-256 of exact final `EventEvolutionProof` container; B32 SHA-256 of canonical final state bytes; O(B32) witnessed SnapshotDigest; B32 effective-event chain hash; O(U) completed replay operation ID; B32 completed page-transcript hash. Tag `11` is appended and binds all prior admitted pages through the durable operation result. |

The command-state proof container is `HX-EV-COMMAND-STATE-PROOF-1\0 || 01 || B(exact purpose-07 command-state claim) || U(key ID) || B(exact 64-byte P1363 signature) || B(exact final EventEvolutionProof container)` with a 2 MiB total cap and no trailing bytes. Its effective-event chain starts `SHA256("HX-EV-COMMAND-EFFECTIVE-1\0" || 01 || U tenant || U domain || U aggregate ID || U aggregate type || N target)` and advances in sequence order as `SHA256("HX-EV-COMMAND-EFFECTIVE-STEP-1\0" || prior B32 || N sequence || B32 StoredDigest || U effective type || I effective version || U effective format || B32 SHA-256(effective payload))`; a witnessed snapshot seed starts after its covered sequence, while full replay starts at 1. An empty chain uses the genesis. The router checks purpose-07 signature/current key, addressed command route and immutable actor head/target, final prefix signature/accumulator and canonical final-state bytes digest. For a one-page request it recomputes the effective chain and page transcript directly from every `verifiedEffectiveEvents` entry and purpose-01 route. For a completed multi-page operation it retrieves the immutable completed ledger/result, verifies every persisted page transition and proof hash under the same operation ID, checks the terminal stored accumulator, effective chain and page transcript against purpose-07 tags `0b`, `0f` and `11`, and checks final page entries directly. A final-page DTO alone never asserts prior pages. A page or continuation token alone is never a completed command state proof. On long streams, `POST /replay-state/pages` produces a completed, privately retained final state and final proof under `operationId`; the command claim binds their hashes and operation ID. The router retrieves that finalized state from the durable operation result, checks completion and hashes, and passes only verified typed state to the processor. A missing or expired result restarts authenticated replay at page 1. On a bounded old request lacking proof, the gateway performs authenticated actor-source lookup and constructs this carrier/proof before invocation; unavailable or conflicting source fails `CommandStateUnproven`. The lookup is keyed by exact scope, stream key, expected head and target; it uses bounded raw actor pages/readback and signs only after a complete prefix reaches target. The domain router can repeat that lookup over its mutually authenticated trusted gateway channel but never accepts free client state. Final state bytes use the registered canonical state serializer and §8 state budget. Empty head=target=0 requires the signed empty prefix and canonical empty-state hash.

`ConsumedMetadataHash = SHA256("HX-EV-CONSUMED-1\0" || 01 || N globalPosition || T timestamp || U correlationId || O(U) causationId || O(U) userId || O(U) DomainServiceVersion || U EventTypeName || U SerializationFormat || I MetadataVersion || O(U) canonical type || O(I) payload version || B32 StoredDigest)`. Route receiver compares **every** consumed DTO scope/target/stored/effective field and this hash to signed claim and authenticated source, and recomputes effective payload hash. A changed/missing attestation triggers source lookup, never direct Apply. Both metadata and payload retain the original timestamp offset; equal instants with different offsets have different evidence.

`OrderedDigestListHash = SHA256("HX-EV-PREFIX-LIST-1\0" || 01 || u32 count || each (N sequence || B32 StoredDigest))`. Check contiguous sequence, count, start/end/head/target and exact per-event route claim order before Apply. `EventEvolutionProof` contains an ordered bounded route claim/signature/key-ID entry **for each event**, one prefix claim/signature/key ID and optional checkpoint claim/signature/key ID, with no unmatched/duplicate entries. Up to 256 events, each route claim at most 1 MiB, page proof at most **64 MiB** charged before allocation; command page proof has stricter **2 MiB** and must use smaller page count when claims are large. Single-event proof is a one-entry list. Empty prefix is start 1/end 0/head 0/target 0/count 0, list hash over count zero, signed scope/fingerprint and no anchor; nonempty head with empty prefix fails except the two exact witnessed zero-tail forms below.

The exact `EventEvolutionProof` container is `HX-EV-PROOF-1\0 || 01 || u32 route-count || route entries || one prefix entry || one-byte checkpoint discriminator || optional checkpoint entry`. Each entry is `B(exact signed claim bytes) || U(key ID) || B(exact 64-byte P1363 signature)` with no extra tags. Route entries are in strictly increasing contiguous sequence order and route-count equals the event count in the associated page; zero routes are valid only for signed empty-stream, witnessed snapshot zero-tail or checkpoint-anchored **projection** zero-tail forms. The prefix entry is mandatory and unique. The discriminator is `00` for no checkpoint and `01` followed by exactly one checkpoint entry; all other values fail. Decode length/count and aggregate 64 MiB proof limit (2 MiB for command pages) **before** allocating claim or entry buffers, then reject trailing bytes, duplicate sequence, unmatched route, wrong purpose/key, invalid signature, scope/head/fingerprint disagreement and a prefix count/order mismatch. Snapshot tags remain only in the prefix claim; they are not checkpoint entries. `/replay-state/pages`, command replay and aggregate reconstruction reject checkpoint discriminator `01` and prefix checkpoint anchor tag `0d`; only projection delivery/completion admits those forms after verifying its projection route and durable state. `CheckpointAnchorHash = SHA256("HX-EV-CHECKPOINT-ANCHOR-1\0" || 01 || B(exact checkpoint claim bytes) || U(key ID) || B(exact 64-byte signature))`. Prefix tag `0d`, when present, must equal this hash of the container's verified checkpoint entry. That entry must name the same scope and projection route, its completed sequence must be exactly page start minus one **only when completed sequence is below `long.MaxValue`**; the terminal count-zero sentinel below compares equal instead. Its accumulator, immutable state-version pointer/readback and active trust/fingerprint must verify before tail Apply. An absent anchor on a projection tail forces full rebuild from sequence 1; an invalid anchor never authorizes partial state. Before a projection page is signed or dispatched, the gateway measures its exact route entries, prefix/anchor carrier, stored DTOs and copied effective-view buffers against both the 64 MiB proof cap and the §8 combined 128 MiB live-scratch cap. If a candidate of at most 256 contiguous events does not fit, it reduces the event count, recomputes the ordered digest list, accumulator, end/count and **new signed complete-prefix claim** for that smaller contiguous range, and retries admission from the same checkpoint without Apply or state write. It continues deterministically down to one event; a single event whose exact signed proof still cannot fit returns `ProofLimit` with source/size evidence and holds route readiness, never retries forever or silently skips it. After a smaller page commits and its checkpoint/readback proves the new end, the next page starts at exactly end+1 under a fresh source-head/target/route-membership check. If source head or route capability changes during shrinking, discard candidate proofs and restart authenticated selection under the new fixed head. Command pages keep their separate 2 MiB outer proof rule. For ordinary stream, Admin, trace, authorized backup and every other authenticated page reader, the gateway likewise premeasures exact proof, readable bytes and simultaneously live scratch before signing or materializing any page of at most 256 contiguous events. A candidate above the 64 MiB proof or 128 MiB live-scratch cap is reduced to a smaller contiguous range; the gateway recomputes and re-signs its route entries, ordered list, prefix range/count/head/accumulator and any applicable anchor under the same fixed source target. It resumes only after the reduced page has been accepted and the next page begins at its end+1, with no skipped event or partial downloadable export. One event whose exact proof cannot fit returns typed `ProofLimit` and holds the reader with source/size evidence; it never loops on the same oversized page. Command pages retain the stricter outer rule. No existing signed fixture claim changes.

The appendable checkpoint accumulator begins `SHA256("HX-EV-CHECKPOINT-CHAIN-1\0" || 01 || U tenant || U domain || U aggregate ID || U aggregate type)` and advances for each contiguous event as `SHA256("HX-EV-CHECKPOINT-STEP-1\0" || previous B32 || N sequence || B32 StoredDigest)`. A page extends the signed previous accumulator; the final complete-prefix claim carries and binds the final value. A token alone is never completion proof. A witnessed snapshot at sequence k requires actor-owned readback of exact folded bytes, SnapshotDigest and cumulative accumulator through k; the signed prefix binds both SnapshotDigest and that accumulator. For k below `long.MaxValue`, its first tail page starts exactly k+1; k at `long.MaxValue` has no tail. Prefix tags `0e` and `0f` are both absent without a witnessed snapshot or both present and verified with one; mixed presence fails. A snapshot zero-tail proof for head=target=k>0 and k below `long.MaxValue` has start k+1/end k/count 0, absent checkpoint anchor, both snapshot tags present and bound to witnessed SnapshotDigest/accumulator, and no Apply. A projection metadata-only completion at checkpoint=head=k>0 and k below `long.MaxValue` has exactly start k+1/end k/count 0, present checkpoint anchor tag `0d` and checkpoint container entry for the same scope/route/sequence k, absent snapshot tags `0e`/`0f`, matching checkpoint accumulator and fenced state readback, and no event read, Apply, handler call, state write or checkpoint advance. **Terminal zero-tail sentinel:** when k=head=target=`long.MaxValue`, either anchored form instead has start=end=`long.MaxValue`, count 0 and zero route entries. Its appropriate snapshot or checkpoint anchor is mandatory, its accumulator/readback must match k, and the equality of start/end is valid only under this exact count-zero terminal rule. It represents no next sequence; the sentinel itself admits no route entry, duplicate event at k, checked increment or continuation to k+1. Separately, a normal one-event page may have start=end=head=target=`long.MaxValue`, count 1, prior accumulator through `long.MaxValue-1` and exactly one verified route at `long.MaxValue`; Apply that event once and complete without next-sequence arithmetic or another token. These forms are distinct from empty-stream start 1/end 0/head 0 with no checkpoint or snapshot tags. Any other zero-route nonempty-head form fails. Missing or incompatible anchor forces authenticated replay from 1. A projection tail starts only at signed checkpoint+1 when the checkpoint is below `long.MaxValue`; absent/invalid anchor forces full rebuild from sequence 1. `StateCheckpointReadbackHash = SHA256("HX-EV-STATE-READBACK-1\0" || 01 || U tenant || U domain || U state store ID || U durable state key || U route || N source sequence || U operation ID || U state version/ETag || U serializer ID || H serializer options || B bounded canonical state bytes)`. Require handler state source sequence **equal** completed sequence and matching operation/version before signing.

Checkpoint body is unsigned and acyclic: `HX-EV-CHECKPOINT-BODY-1\0`, codec `01`, count `000b`, tags `01..0b` tenant U, domain U, aggregate ID U, aggregate type U, route U, state bytes B, serializer ID U, completed sequence N, accumulator B32, source head N, expected CAS token O(U). It excludes readback hash, signature and resulting ETag. For the legacy single-state/V17 mode, hash body, CAS, read exact body/ETag and actual durable handler state, then sign purpose 04. For a production named row-set root, reserve the operation-scoped logical state version under the coordinator fence, compute the root/body/readback hash and sign purpose 04 **before** the one final root/checkpoint pointer CAS; claim tags `0d` and `12` both carry that reserved logical version. The prepared readback certificate binds only the prepared-record ETag; the actual final-pointer ETag enters only the separately authenticated provider-issued receipt after CAS acceptance, never a precommit claim or hash. If stores differ, use a linearizable `IProjectionStateCheckpointFence` coordinator for `(tenant, domain, aggregate ID, aggregate type, projection route)`. It owns one durable per-scope generation record under a coordinator ID. Its required provider operation is `TryWriteWithFence(scope, expectedGeneration, operationId, versionedStateOrCheckpoint)`: under a linearizable transaction/CAS, the participating state or checkpoint store compares the coordinator's **persisted current generation** with `expectedGeneration` and rejects a stale token before writing any state or checkpoint bytes. A coordinator generation advance serializes with these compare-and-write operations; an already issued token never authorizes a write after the advance. Each store persists operation ID, generation and immutable version together with the write and exposes their fenced readback. The coordinator must furnish a shared serializable fence that covers both stores' compare-and-write operations; independently sampled generations or readback after an unfenced write do not qualify. Persist state/effect under one stable operation/version, read it under that fence, CAS checkpoint referencing it, reread both before releasing the fence, and bind coordinator ID, generation, state operation/version and checkpoint ETag in the signed purpose-04 claim. If either backend cannot implement the compare-and-write guarantee, `CheckpointFenceUnavailable` holds readiness and the previous checkpoint/state pointer stays authoritative. Concurrent advances serialize on this fence; a mismatched row, stale generation or provider unable to enforce the shared fence returns `CheckpointFenceUnavailable` and leaves the prior checkpoint authoritative. CAS and independent readbacks alone do not qualify. State bytes/readback ceiling is 64 MiB, streaming hash buffer at most 1 MiB plus metadata; `CheckpointStateLimit` leaves last good checkpoint. Historic checkpoint obligations are immutable records keyed by scope/route/sequence/body hash/state readback hash/signature/key ID with tag `0e` signed issuance UTC matching obligation time and purpose-04 key interval, prior fingerprint and pending rebuild/rollback obligations, separate from delivery-pin obligations. Expired key use is only for the exact still-open historic obligation, original signing interval and durable readbacks; revocation requires explicit incident disposition. Trust-only transition verifies old obligation, current semantic/handler hashes, state/readback and accumulator, then signs current-fingerprint attestation **before** tail Apply at an actor boundary.

The handler state write above is an **immutable version**, never an in-place replacement of the key read by the old checkpoint. Its durable key is `projection-state-version:` plus lowercase SHA-256 of `U tenant || U domain || U aggregate ID || U aggregate type || U HandlerRouteId || N completed source sequence || U operation ID || N fence generation` under the §4 scalar codec; create-once CAS rejects a different value at that key. The checkpoint's durable record atomically carries a `HX-EV-CHECKPOINT-STATE-POINTER-1\0 || 01 || 0006` sidecar with tags `01..06`: U immutable state-version key, U state store ID, U state operation ID, U state version/ETag, N fence generation, B32 checkpoint body hash. This pointer is committed with the checkpoint CAS and its exact state key/version is read back under the same fence; the signed purpose-04 claim tags `0f..12`, the body hash and `StateCheckpointReadbackHash` bind it without changing the existing signed claim/body codecs or V17 fixture bytes. Until checkpoint body **and pointer** CAS/readback prove one committed transition, the old checkpoint still points to its old immutable state version. On ambiguous CAS, retain both versions and the fence generation, reconcile checkpoint/pointer/state by operation ID, and never report a new `Current` from a state write alone. In a single transactional store the equivalent is one serializable transaction that creates the immutable state version and changes the checkpoint body/pointer together, followed by readback; no in-place overwrite is allowed there either. Garbage collection of an old state version requires durable proof that no current checkpoint, retry, rebuild or rollback obligation references it. The approved V17 claim bytes remain a codec fixture; production acceptance also requires the corresponding immutable state-version key and pointer readback.

The V17 historical/test-local named-batch codec uses `BatchStateReadbackHash = SHA256("HX-EV-BATCH-READBACK-1\0" || 01 || u32 row-count || sorted rows)`. Each unique row is `U stateStoreId || U durableStateKey || U operationKind || N sourceSequence || U operationId || one-byte resultState || U resultingETagOrVersion || B32 canonicalReadbackBytesDigest`; sort by the separately encoded state-store ID and durable key, then operation kind/sequence/ID, reject duplicate keys or rows and unknown operation kinds. `resultState=01` is a present write (`operationKind=upsert`) whose resulting ETag/version and exact canonical readback digest match durable state. `resultState=00` is a successful delete (`operationKind=delete`): the same write fence supplies an authenticated absent-state readback and durable delete operation receipt/version in `resultingETagOrVersion`; the B32 digest is SHA-256 of the exact canonical delete receipt bytes, and the receipt binds key, operation ID, source sequence and fence. Absence alone, a missing receipt, a missing write row, or a present deleted row fails. After every `ReadModelBatchOperation` effect, read back **every** durable row under one write fence and compare operation ID, source sequence, version and canonical bytes or delete receipt. A missing/mismatched row prevents checkpoint advance. A delete receipt is the provider-authenticated, durable bytes `HX-EV-DELETE-RECEIPT-1\0 || 01 || 0007`, tags `01..07`: U state store ID, U durable state key, U operation ID, N source sequence, U write-fence ID, U resulting delete version/ETag, B exact provider delete-ack bytes. The same fence authenticates an absent-state readback for that key; the receipt is not constructed from absence alone. `canonicalReadbackBytesDigest` for a delete is SHA-256 of this complete receipt, and `resultingETagOrVersion` equals tag `06`. Missing durable ack, incomparable fence or provider inability to prove absence holds the checkpoint without advancement. The present-write V17 fixture uses discriminator `01`; its checkpoint signature binds that batch hash and the shared-fence fields in §11.

Named-projection writes use immutable shadow rows, never an in-place key visible to an ordinary reader. A startup-frozen catalog assigns each `(tenant, domain, HandlerRouteId, declared logical key-space)` **one** ownership mode: `aggregate` (one addressed stream) or `shared` (many streams), using the §5 key-space capability rows; overlapping declarations fail readiness. Each source stream retains its own authenticated contiguous sequence checkpoint and prefix accumulator. A shared route/key-space has one durable linearizable generation coordinator and commit-order log. Under its CAS fence the coordinator reserves the next checked **global commit ordinal** for one prepared operation, binds that candidate ordinal to immutable operation ID, original authenticated source-proof hash, precommit transition-intent hash and previous root hash, and commits the ordinal log entry **atomically with** the new route-global root and source checkpoint visibility. A failed uncommitted candidate consumes no ordinal; an ambiguous result is read back by operation ID before any replacement candidate. The ordinal is exactly previous ordinal plus one (genesis zero); gaps, reused ordinals, changed operation bytes, conflicting source progress or an unavailable coordinator hold. An exact retry of the same operation reuses its assigned ordinal/result after readback, including ambiguous CAS. A late source event whose per-stream sequence or aggregate ID sorts before an already committed source gets the next ordinal; it cannot change prior visible history. Authenticated replay follows this durable ordinal log, not arrival-time sorting or a retrospectively sorted `(sequence, aggregate)` tuple. The root binds the ordinal and source proof through its progress intent and operation witness, while every contributing stream remains independently sequence-contiguous. A per-aggregate pointer never publishes a shared key; queries select the route-global committed root and certificate. Aggregate-owned keys use the same root/progress codec with one source and need no cross-stream ordinal. Provider inability to supply the fence, ordinal CAS and exact readback blocks route readiness.

For each candidate operation, under that fence read the **currently committed logical** value, logical ETag/version and expiry from the old root, then apply the existing public `ReadModelBatchOperation` policy. `ExpectedETag` with an empty ETag means create-only and requires proven logical absence; a nonempty ETag must match the present visible logical ETag. `IdempotentAbsent` is **delete-only**: a present logical key is deleted under the fence, and an already absent key is a proved idempotent no-op. It never rejects a valid present-key delete merely because absence is false. Unconditional writes remain serialized by the fence. A fresh absent shadow physical key is not proof of logical absence. The operation witness records observed and resulting logical versions/ETags, exact intended canonical bytes, `ReadModelBatchOperation.ValueTypeName`, policy, TTL and the assigned global ordinal/source-proof intent for shared ownership. Exact retry compares OperationId and all those intended fields; an intervening committed logical version conflicts unless the original operation/result is read back as already committed. `BatchStateReadbackHash` is the changed-row/delete witness, distinct from full membership. V17's signed batch witness remains a historical/test-local codec vector. The production logical-operation witness is `HX-EV-BATCH-LOGICAL-1\0 || 01 || I operationCount ||` entries in original batch ordinal order. Each entry is `HX-EV-BATCH-LOGICAL-ENTRY-1\0 || 01 || 000d`, ascending tags: `01` I ordinal, `02` U logical key, `03` U OperationId (for a `ReadModelBatchOperation`, exactly its enclosing `ReadModelBatchScope.BatchId`, never a nonexistent per-operation member), `04` U kind (`upsert`/`delete`), `05` U policy (`Unconditional`/`ExpectedETag`/`IdempotentAbsent`), `06` O(U) caller expected ETag (empty means create-only), `07` O(U) observed committed logical ETag (absent means proved logical absence), `08` N observed logical version (zero for proved absence), `09` O(U) resulting logical ETag (absent for tombstone/no-op), `0a` N resulting logical version, `0b` B32 SHA-256 of exact intended canonical value bytes (empty bytes for delete), `0c` O(N) positive TTL ticks, `0d` U exact `ValueTypeName` (nonempty for upsert, empty for delete). Ordinals start at zero and are contiguous. A present-key `IdempotentAbsent` creates the durable delete receipt/absence readback; an already absent no-op retains its version and carries a fenced absence witness without inventing a delete receipt. The shared commit ordinal and original authenticated source-proof hash are bound by the §6 source-transition intent included in ProgressHash; a batch cannot substitute an operation from a different source/ordinal. The production `NamedBatchScopeBytes` are exactly `U ReadModelBatchScope.StoreName || U TenantId || U Domain || U AggregateId || U ProjectionType || U BatchId || U HandlerRouteId || U keySpaceId || U ownershipMode` in that order. All operations in one batch carry the same BatchId; an exact retry compares the complete scope tuple, BatchId, ordinal, source-transition intent and logical operations before reuse. Production `BatchStateReadbackHash = SHA256("HX-EV-BATCH-READBACK-2\0" || 02 || B(NamedBatchScopeBytes) || B32 SHA-256("HX-EV-BATCH-READBACK-1\0" || 01 || u32 row-count || sorted changed-row/delete rows) || B(exact logical-operation witness))`. The complete scope tuple and BatchId therefore enter this batch fingerprint and the root through tag `0b`; the root also carries tenant/domain/route/key-space/ownership and its tag `07` OperationId equals the same BatchId. A mismatch in any field is a conflicting batch/root, never an exact retry; V17's signed V1 hash and literal bytes remain historical framing evidence only.

The production complete row-set is a persistent immutable **keyed sparse Merkle tree**, with the existing root record's tag `09` binding its root and tag `08` its checked member count. Its key tuple is the exact separately encoded §4 `U tenant || U domain || U HandlerRouteId || U keySpaceId || U logicalKey`; path bits are the 256 bits of SHA-256 over that tuple, most-significant bit of byte zero first. The leaf at depth 256 is `HX-EV-NAMED-LEAF-2\0 || 02 || 000b`, ascending tags `01..05` the five U key components, `06` U immutable physical row key, `07` U physical row version/ETag, `08` one-byte state (`00` proved delete/tombstone, `01` present), `09` B32 SHA-256 of exact authenticated row content/readback bytes or exact durable delete receipt, `0a` O(N) positive logical TTL ticks and `0b` N original visibility generation. Its hash is `SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-LEAF-HASH-2\0" || 02 || B(exact leaf record))`. At depth 256 the empty hash is `E256 = SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-EMPTY-2\0" || 02 || u16(256))` with count zero; a leaf has count one. At each depth `d=255..0`, `NodeHash = SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-NODE-2\0" || 02 || u16(d) || I leftCount || B32 leftHash || I rightCount || B32 rightHash)` with checked parent count equal to leftCount+rightCount. `Ed` is that formula with both child counts zero and both child hashes E(d+1). The root of a zero-member set is E0 with count zero. Present and proved tombstone leaves both count as one retained member until certified compaction. The exact row proof is `HX-EV-NAMED-ROW-PATH-2\0 || 02 || B(exact key tuple) || one-byte terminalKind || terminal || u16(256) || siblings`: terminalKind `00` has no terminal bytes and denotes E256/count zero; `01` is followed by `B(exact leaf record)` with count one. Each sibling is `I checked count || B32 hash` in ascending depth `0..255`; no default sibling is omitted. A membership proof uses `01`, including a tombstone, and an absence proof uses `00`. There are no extra entries or trailing bytes. Recompute bottom-up using the key bits, require both reconstructed root hash and count to equal root tags `09` and `08` under the authenticated certificate, and read/hash the selected physical row or delete receipt before returning value or absence. A different exact leaf key on the same 256-bit path is a hash collision and fails closed; it never silently replaces a member. A changed key recomputes only its verified 256-level logical path by immutable path copying under the route fence; unchanged subtrees and their counts are reused from the previously certified root. Physical storage is a deterministic content-addressed compressed sparse trie: materialize leaves and two-child branch records, and encode each maximal single-child run as `HX-EV-NAMED-SPAN-2\0 || 02 || u16 startDepth || u16 endDepth || I descendantCount || B32 descendantHash || B(ordered path bits)`; the decoder expands every skipped logical level with its fixed Ed empty sibling and the declared key bits before comparing the root. The bit string has exactly `endDepth-startDepth` bits, packed most-significant-bit first into the minimal byte count with unused low bits zero; a span with startDepth >= endDepth or a branch hidden inside a span is invalid. Spans are maximal, nonempty and uniquely normalized; their exact content hash and referenced child bytes are authenticated. This compression preserves the full 256-level proof codec while allowing the 1 GiB retained-index bound to be met for a million small leaves; an oversized encoded index still fails `NamedIndexLimit`. Complete membership/absence comes from the path and certified root, never a linear row scan or adjacent-member inference. The older sorted-leaf/V17 literal is historical framing evidence only and cannot certify a production named root.

The logical source-progress set is keyed by `(aggregate type, aggregate ID)` and holds each contributing source's checked sequence (the proposed sequence only for the addressed transition, otherwise its committed sequence), **previous committed** source-checkpoint hash, prefix accumulator, last operation ID and `O(B32)` precommit transition-intent hash. The currently building checkpoint hash is never an input to its own candidate progress/root hash. For production, encode each source entry as `HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-ENTRY-2\0 || 02 || 0007` with ascending tags: `01` U aggregate type, `02` U aggregate ID, `03` N sequence, `04` B32 previous committed checkpoint hash, `05` B32 accumulator, `06` U operation ID and `07` O(B32) transition-intent hash. A new source with no prior checkpoint uses exactly 32 zero bytes only at sequence zero; unchanged entries have absent intent and retain the currently committed checkpoint. The addressed source has exactly one present intent. That intent remains `HX-EV-NAMED-SOURCE-INTENT-1\0 || 01 || 0008`, tags `01` U aggregate type, `02` U aggregate ID, `03` N proposed source sequence, `04` B32 proposed accumulator, `05` B32 original authenticated actor/source proof hash, `06` U operation ID, `07` N coordinator-assigned global commit ordinal (zero for aggregate-owned mode), `08` B32 previous committed checkpoint hash. Path key is SHA-256 of exact §4 `U aggregateType || U aggregateId`, with the same most-significant-bit-first 256-level path and collision fail-closed rule as the row tree. Progress leaves use `SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-LEAF-2\0" || 02 || B(exact entry))` with count one. Empty and node hashes use distinct `HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-EMPTY-2\0` and `HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-NODE-2\0` separators, the same depth and checked child-count codecs as the row tree, with zero-count empty leaves. `ProgressHash = SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-ROOT-2\0" || 02 || I checkedSourceCount || B32 sparseProgressRoot)`; this commits both complete source count and sparse tree root in tag `0a` of the named root. The exact source proof is `HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-PATH-2\0 || 02 || B(exact U aggregateType || U aggregateId tuple) || one-byte terminalKind || terminal || u16(256) || siblings`, with terminalKind `00` for the empty terminal and no bytes, or `01` followed by `B(exact progress entry)`; each of the 256 ascending-depth `0..255` siblings is `I checked count || B32 hash`. No default sibling is omitted and no extra bytes are allowed. Its reconstructed checked count and hash must equal the certified ProgressHash, and each source update reads/verifies the previous certified path, changes only that path and retains all unchanged subtrees. Progress branches and maximal single-child runs use the same authenticated compressed storage rule with distinct `HX-EV-NAMED-PROGRESS-SPAN-2\0` separator; proof verification always expands the full 256 logical levels. No complete million-entry vector or O(total sources) SHA-256 is reconstructed per event. Missing prior source evidence, an altered transition intent or a count/root mismatch holds before publication. From the prior signed checkpoint and original source proof, build candidate rows, intent, ProgressHash and root **first**. The new source checkpoint then signs that candidate root/intent; the final fenced pointer CAS publishes the prepared root and checkpoint only together with their authenticated certificate linking hashes, source intent, ordinal and previous checkpoint. Readback verifies the certificate and new signature against the already constructed root without any forward hash reference. A query verifies only selected source leaves/index paths and the coordinator certificate, never the entire vector. Missing prior checkpoint, altered intent, cyclic forward reference, changed operation or absent certified sparse-progress path/proof prevents root commit/readiness.

The authoritative checkpoint **state bytes** for a named batch are the exact canonical root/pointer record `HX-EV-NAMED-ROWSET-ROOT-1\0 || 01 || 000d` with ascending tags: `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U HandlerRouteId, `04` U keySpaceId, `05` U ownership mode (`aggregate` or `shared`), `06` N generation (equal to the assigned global commit ordinal for shared ownership), `07` U OperationId (exact enclosing `ReadModelBatchScope.BatchId` for a named batch), `08` I complete row count, `09` B32 Merkle root hash, `0a` B32 ProgressHash, `0b` B32 BatchStateReadbackHash, `0c` Q provider-certified atomic pointer visibility UTC, `0d` B exact canonical `HX-EV-STATE-BACKEND-1\0` descriptor bytes from §5. The fence provider reserves tag `0c` as its certified prospective pointer-visibility UTC **inside the same held linearizable commit operation before the candidate root is hashed and checkpoint signed**. The final root/checkpoint CAS must certify and use that exact reserved UTC as its visibility time or abort without publishing either record; a caller clock, unfenced pre-CAS estimate or post-sign substitution cannot supply it. A provider unable to reserve and atomically certify that time and root holds route readiness. Its reserved serializer ID is exactly `hexalith.eventstore.named-rowset-root.v1`; its canonical §4 LF options manifest is exactly `{"codec":"hx-ev-named-rowset-root-1","version":1}\n`, expanded with no optional fields and hashed by the §4 OptionsHash rule. No handler-defined object occupies named-batch state bytes. The named checkpoint state readback hash uses the exact §6 `HX-EV-STATE-READBACK-1\0` formula with this root record as `B bounded canonical state bytes`, the reserved serializer ID/options hash, route/key-space root durable state key, source sequence, operation ID and a **deterministic operation-scoped logical state version reserved under the coordinator fence before signing** in the `U state version/ETag` position. This is a logical version, never the provider ETag first returned after CAS. The provider compares that reserved version while staging root and signed checkpoint as invisible prepared records, reports the exact prepared readback and actual provider ETag under the fence, and certifies that the final pointer CAS can publish precisely those bytes and that logical version together with the postcommit certificate. A provider unable to reserve and compare it holds route readiness. The changed-row `BatchStateReadbackHash` remains tag `0b`, a separate operation witness. Stage and read back every changed shadow row/delete receipt, affected sparse-tree paths, previous addressed source checkpoint and source transition intent first; authenticate untouched subtrees against the previous certified roots without rereading every member. Construct the candidate root from those prior records; only afterward sign the addressed source checkpoint over that exact root and intent. Root and newly signed addressed source checkpoint are staged as **prepared and invisible** under the operation fence; other source checkpoints in the progress vector remain unchanged. The coordinator reads back both prepared byte strings, their reserved logical version and **prepared-record ETag**, and authenticates the separate prepared readback certificate below. It then performs **one** final fenced pointer CAS that selects their exact hashes and root generation, atomically makes the checkpoint transition visible, and causes the provider to issue the separate final-pointer commit receipt below. The actual final-pointer ETag is known only after this CAS accepts and never enters a prepared hash or signature. No reader selects a prepared root or checkpoint before that CAS. Neither root nor progress leaf contains the newly signed checkpoint hash, so construction is acyclic. A separate store stages the source checkpoint invisibly under the same fence until root commit and exposes it only through the committed root. Read back the exact root, selected sparse progress-path validation, source checkpoint, prepared certificate, final-pointer provider receipt and state hash before `Current`. Before the final visibility CAS, the authenticated **prepared readback certificate** is `HX-EV-NAMED-PREPARED-1\0 || 01 || 000a`, with ordered tags `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U HandlerRouteId, `04` U keySpaceId, `05` U OperationId, `06` B32 exact prepared root-record hash, `07` B32 exact prepared signed checkpoint-carrier hash, `08` U reserved logical state version, `09` U **prepared-record ETag**, `0a` B exact authenticated prepared-provider readback/fence evidence. It contains no final-pointer ETag, final visibility generation or post-CAS timestamp. The final pointer CAS verifies those prepared hashes/version/ETag and creates a provider-issued **final-pointer commit receipt**, `HX-EV-NAMED-POINTER-COMMIT-1\0 || 01 || 000c`, ordered tags `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U HandlerRouteId, `04` U keySpaceId, `05` U OperationId, `06` B32 prepared-certificate hash, `07` B32 selected root-record hash, `08` B32 selected signed checkpoint-carrier hash, `09` U reserved logical state version, `0a` U **actual final-pointer ETag**, `0b` N actual visibility generation and `0c` Q actual provider-certified visibility UTC. Its authenticated provider binding to the atomic pointer CAS is verified through the configured backend trust/fence and independently read back before `Current` or query; receipt tag `0b` must equal selected root tag `06`, and receipt tag `0c` must equal the provider-reserved root tag `0c` or the CAS is rejected as uncommitted. The receipt is not included in its own pointer hash. A missing, changed or unprovable final-pointer receipt holds the previous certified root or the candidate for same-OperationId reconciliation. If a crash occurs after preparation but before final pointer CAS, readers keep the previous certified pointer. Recovery reads exact prepared root/checkpoint bytes, prepared ETag, logical version and prepared certificate under the original fence, then retries that **same** pointer CAS without inventing a final ETag or time. A lost final CAS acknowledgement reads back pointer and provider receipt before reporting visibility. Missing or changed evidence holds without publication; a lost CAS acknowledgement is reconciled by exact pointer/provider-receipt readback before retry. A provider exposing prepared root bytes as current before certificate/pointer publication fails readiness. The legacy single-state/V17 claim and hash codec stay unchanged. Partial rows or uncertain CAS keep the old root and last-good state. A same-store serializable transaction may stage rows plus checkpoint and publish their certified pointer atomically with the exact prepared readback certificate and provider-issued final-pointer receipt; independent stores require the shared coordinator/fence and retain all old generations until obligations close. V17's fixed signed checkpoint is a historical batch-hash framing vector and is not rewritten; production named-batch root mode uses this reserved state codec.

Named row and progress indexes are separately capacity-bound **before** root publication: each separately encoded logical key is at most 512 UTF-8 bytes, each complete leaf/progress entry at most 4 KiB, each retained complete index at most 1 GiB encoded, each row/source count at most 1,000,000 and all live construction scratch at most 128 MiB. Checked byte counters charge keys, records, framing and Merkle nodes before each allocation. For each event, at most **4,096 changed logical keys** are admitted. Read and verify the prior committed row and source roots, then copy only the affected 256-node paths into authenticated encrypted bounded storage, sharing untouched immutable nodes. Charge all changed keys, 256 path nodes per changed key, encoded bytes, proof buffers and 128 MiB live scratch before construction; stage every chunk under one OperationId, verify readback, then publish one root and certificate. An oversized change is `NamedIndexLimit` before any visible pointer. Corpus/readiness preflight proves required routes fit this bound or requires a separately approved migration. Initial migration may stream a bounded complete index once; ordinary event updates never scan all rows or sources. `NamedIndexLimit` rejects an overlarge candidate before root publication while retaining the previous committed root. A tombstone and its exact delete/version evidence remain while any rollback, retry, backup, checkpoint or older-root obligation refers to them. Once all such obligations close with durable proof, a fenced compaction root at a new global ordinal may omit the tombstone, retaining immutable archived delete/version evidence for ABA-safe later creates; absence is then proved by the authenticated empty sparse path against the compacted root. Compaction advances no source sequence, signs no fabricated source event, and requires exact root/certificate readback. If safe pruning cannot maintain all bounds, route readiness holds rather than dropping evidence or silently rejecting valid history.

The additive public `INamedProjectionVersionedReadModelStore` query seam has `GetAsync<TValue>(string storeName, string logicalKey, NamedProjectionReadScope scope, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<ReadModelEntry<TValue>>` and `GetManyAsync<TValue>(string storeName, IReadOnlyList<string> logicalKeys, NamedProjectionReadScope scope, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<IReadOnlyList<ReadModelBulkEntry<TValue>>>`, with the old `TValue : class` constraint. `NamedProjectionReadScope` is immutable descriptive route input and names tenant, domain, HandlerRouteId, declared key-space, ownership mode, optional addressed aggregate for aggregate ownership and state-backend ID; the separately verified capability below grants access, and caller JSON alone cannot grant it. Under the shared fence the router reads the **last committed route-global root for shared keys** or the aggregate root for aggregate-owned keys, verifies its checkpoint/state hash, backend, progress root, operation and catalog declaration plus the authenticated coordinator commit certificate that the changed source path and unchanged certified subtrees yield the complete ProgressHash at root CAS, then releases the coordinator lock. It reads only requested logical keys through authenticated leaf membership/absence index paths and checked physical row/delete readbacks, not a full row-set scan. Before returning **any** route-aware value, it reacquires the coordinator fence and rechecks both the exact root pointer/generation **and authoritative coordinator UTC against every selected leaf expiry**. If the root changed it retries at most three times; if UTC reached an expiry even with an unchanged root, it triggers or observes maintenance and returns `ReadModelExpiryPending` with no expired bytes until a tombstone root is certified. A fourth root change returns `CheckpointFenceUnavailable` with no partial values. Cancellation releases proof buffers, rows and locks and returns no mixed generation. The route-aware `GetManyAsync` proves all keys against one root, preserves request order and rejects duplicate keys.

Pre-admission caps are **1,000,000 complete rows per root**, **256 requested keys**, **8 MiB total encoded query proof**, **64 MiB total result bytes**, and **128 MiB combined simultaneously live proof/row/result/scratch bytes**. Charge conservative encoded sizes and allocator capacity before each allocation or lock hold; a request that cannot fit returns typed `ReadModelQueryLimit` with no partial result or held fence. The query proof includes the exact root record, selected leaf/absence paths and selected source-progress paths, each under its own index hash; all hash/readback comparisons precede deserialization. Corpus/readiness preflight holds a route whose key count, retained row sizes or source-vector/index proof cannot satisfy these limits; it never raises a cap silently.

`DaprReadModelStore` implements the route-aware seam additively. A startup-frozen catalog discriminator recognizes every versioned logical key and shadow generation before **all** public legacy reads and writes: `GetAsync`, `GetManyAsync`, `SaveAsync`, `TrySaveAsync`, time-to-live writes, delete/erase, batch, bulk and staging entry points. A legacy read either goes through a registered authenticated scoped query adapter or returns `ReadModelRouteContextRequired` before touching a versioned key; a legacy write to that key returns the typed context-required failure and cannot create a shadow row. Only the authorized route-aware fenced writer/query seam may touch versioned generations. During 6.6 migration, `SharedProjectionEpochCoordinator.ReadGenerationAsync`, `BuildBatch` and every physical-generation read/write use an additive authorized route-aware store adapter carrying the same authenticated route/key-space scope and fence. Existing physical rows are readable only with authenticated legacy generation/marker evidence; new writes enter the new root protocol. A direct old `IReadModelStore` call from the coordinator is neither an unguarded bypass nor a blocked production path: readiness requires that migrated adapter and a test proving the generic legacy guard rejects the same physical key without its authority. Non-versioned keys retain existing APIs and coordinated-batch marker behavior. Admin projection views use the route-aware seam; missing/ambiguous root is `ProjectionStateUnproven`, never a free shadow read.

`TimeToLive` starts at tag `0c` atomic root visibility UTC, as the public batch contract specifies. The leaf binds its TTL duration and original write visibility generation; checked addition to that generation's provider-certified CAS UTC yields expiry, checked against authoritative coordinator UTC. An unchanged leaf retains its original generation and expiry across later root commits. The coordinator retains an authenticated `(generation, visibility UTC, root hash, operation ID)` ledger for every still-referenced generation; a missing or changed old time holds the query rather than resetting TTL. Shadow and prior physical rows, delete receipts, index nodes and source proofs have independently renewed storage retention while any committed pointer, retry, rollback or backup obligation references them. Renewal uses separate lease/metadata keys or a provider operation proved to preserve the exact physical row bytes, version and ETag bound by its Merkle leaf. If renewal mutates any bound field, the coordinator copies the row into a new immutable version and certifies a new root under the fence before old retention lapses. An unrenewable referenced row holds readiness; no maintenance silently invalidates an already certified root. TTL expiry is a **route/key-space scoped maintenance transition** with no source event, arbitrarily selected contributing aggregate, fabricated source checkpoint, source-sequence advance or accumulator change. Its immutable OperationId is `SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-TTL-OP-1\0" || 01 || B32 old root hash || U logical key || N original visibility generation || Q checked expiry UTC)` rendered as lowercase hex. Under the route/key-space fence it creates one proved tombstone leaf and root at the next checked global commit ordinal; **ProgressHash and every contributing source's signed checkpoint, sequence and accumulator remain unchanged**. An aggregate-owned key may use the same scoped maintenance claim and unchanged-progress rule. The maintenance intent is `HX-EV-NAMED-TTL-INTENT-2\0 || 02 || 0008`, tags `01` B32 old root hash, `02` U logical key, `03` N original visibility generation, `04` Q checked expiry UTC, `05` U OperationId, `06` N assigned global ordinal, `07` B32 unchanged ProgressHash, `08` B32 exact authenticated tombstone leaf/readback hash. Maintenance root tag `0b` is `SHA256("HX-EV-NAMED-MAINT-READBACK-1\0" || 01 || B(exact maintenance intent))`, so it is constructible before the root hash; the claim then signs that completed new root hash. No root, intent, claim or checkpoint hash refers forward to itself. A distinct **purpose `0f`** signed `HX-EV-NAMED-MAINTENANCE-1\0 || 01 || 000d` claim has tags `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U HandlerRouteId, `04` U keySpaceId, `05` B exact canonical backend descriptor, `06` B32 old root hash, `07` U logical key, `08` Q expiry UTC, `09` B32 new tombstone-root hash, `0a` N global commit ordinal, `0b` B32 unchanged ProgressHash, `0c` U exact maintenance OperationId, `0d` B32 SHA-256 of the exact maintenance intent. It uses the current active domain signing key and never verifies as an event-progress purpose-04 checkpoint. The coordinator atomically publishes the new root and this maintenance claim, then reads back an authenticated commit certificate binding both hashes, ordinal, backend, operation ID, exact logical version and provider ETag before queries select it. Exact retries reuse the pinned operation ID, ordinal, root and claim; ambiguous CAS reconciles by OperationId before another attempt.

At each committed root, a durable scheduler registers only changed/new finite-leaf expiry jobs or a successor explicitly rebased under that root, retaining unchanged jobs by reference with their original generation, expiry UTC and maintenance OperationId. Before executing a job, it reads the current certified root under the route/key-space fence and authenticates that leaf. If the exact logical leaf is absent, replaced, or has a different original visibility generation or expiry, it durably marks the old job superseded and never tombstones the replacement. If the same expiring leaf persists under a newer root, it derives a successor maintenance OperationId by the §6 formula using that current root and unchanged key/generation/expiry, CAS-links the old job to the successor and drives the successor against the current root. An ambiguous old attempt is reconciled under its original OperationId before rebasing. Concurrent expiry jobs for one key serialize under the fence; a scheduler or query trigger drives the linked successor or returns typed `ReadModelExpiryPending`, never spins on a stale root. It retries after crash and reconciles ambiguous CAS before another transition. Every route-aware query compares the leaf expiry to authenticated coordinator UTC. At `now >= expiry`, it triggers or observes that scheduled transition and returns typed `ReadModelExpiryPending`/`ProjectionStateUnproven` until a committed tombstone root and signed maintenance certificate prove absence. It never returns expired bytes or infers absence from clock time alone. Scheduler outage or late first query therefore holds the value as unproven until repair; a still-live value is returned only while authenticated UTC is strictly before expiry. Physical row expiry never substitutes for this transition. Missing, altered or early-retired evidence holds readiness.

An **event-progress** checkpoint's `StateCheckpointReadbackHash` is the exact precommit logical-version root-state readback hash for a production named batch, and the existing single-state hash for a single-state operation. A scoped purpose-`0f` TTL transition instead binds its root and unchanged ProgressHash through its distinct maintenance claim and final-pointer provider receipt; fenced compaction binds those values through its own authenticated root certificate. Neither invents a source checkpoint. `BatchStateReadbackHash` is separately bound inside the named root. The historical V17 batch-hash checkpoint vector retains its original codec bytes and is not a production root-state substitute. Every signed production checkpoint binds its selected state bytes/hash under the shared fence, never an unfenced copy of intended writes.

The optional folded-snapshot witness is an additive actor-owned side record, never a rewrite of the approved Story 6.1 v1 envelope. `SnapshotDigest = SHA256("HX-EV-SNAPSHOT-1\0" || 01 || U tenant || U domain || U aggregate ID || U aggregate type || N covered sequence || I snapshot envelope version || U snapshot state serializer ID || B canonical verified readable folded-state bytes || B32 StateSchemaApplyHash || B32 EventTransformHash || B32 derived protection record hash || B32 cumulative prefix accumulator through covered sequence)`. The protection record includes state, metadata version, scheme, key alias, content hint and `O(M)` flags under §4; snapshot state obeys the approved Story 6.1 bound and is streamed/checked before witness hashing. The proof carries those exact folded bytes, digest and accumulator or authenticated actor retrieval of all three. The signed prefix and receiver recomputation tie `DomainServiceCurrentState.SnapshotState` to the verified bytes; a free typed/JSON `CurrentState` is never command authority. Gateway obtains signed source/snapshot proof for it or rejects it, and the domain router rejects unproven state before calling even the old processor. `DomainServiceCurrentState.SnapshotState` is deserialized **from the verified bytes** under fingerprinted serializer; receiver recomputes witness and never trusts a free object to assert it. Actor stages snapshot and side record in one batch, saves once and reads both back; absent, partial, stale or incompatible witness safely bypasses to full replay. If Story 6.2 runtime is absent, no witness is read/written and full replay is used. Changed state/Apply, transform or relevant handler compatibility causes replay/rebuild; verifier-only key rotation does not.

The persisted witness at `AggregateIdentity.SnapshotKey + ":evolution-witness"` has exact binary schema `HX-EV-SNAPSHOT-WITNESS-1\0 || 01 || 0011`, followed by tags `01..11` in order: U tenant, U domain, U aggregate ID, U aggregate type, U snapshot key, N covered sequence, I approved v1 envelope version, U state serializer ID, B32 SHA-256 of canonical verified readable folded-state bytes, B32 SHA-256 of the complete exact original v1 snapshot storage/envelope/ciphertext bytes, B32 SnapshotDigest, B32 StateSchemaApplyHash, B32 EventTransformHash, B32 protection-record hash, B32 cumulative prefix accumulator, U actor batch operation ID, N actor logical version chosen before save with checked increment. The actor pre-renders the exact provider snapshot storage bytes with a bounded deterministic serializer before staging, and both snapshot hashes bind those separately preserved approved v1 bytes and key; no field changes that envelope. Stage snapshot plus side record under one actor batch operation and predeclared logical version, then read back their exact bytes, key, sequence, equal operation/logical version and provider ETag before treating either as authority; the post-save ETag is readback evidence outside the prewrite side record, so no digest is circular. A partial or mismatched pair is a safe full-replay bypass. The existing v1 snapshot may be re-folded only after full authenticated replay and one actor-owned paired save/readback.
