Domain receivers obtain snapshot authority through `IAuthenticatedSnapshotSource.ReadAsync(tenant, domain, aggregateType, aggregateId, snapshotKey, coveredSequence, CancellationToken)` over the mutually authenticated gateway channel. The actor-owned response contains exact original v1 snapshot-storage bytes, exact side-record bytes, verified readable folded-state bytes obtained through the existing typed snapshot unprotection/readability outcome, batch operation ID, predeclared logical version and provider ETag, all under one linearizable provider read fence. The gateway verifies provider key/scope/ETag and exact bytes under the actor identity before returning them; request-carried `snapshotWitness`/`snapshotState` are only bounded hints. The receiver independently hashes original storage bytes and folded bytes, decodes the side record, compares its two byte hashes, batch ID, logical version, key, sequence, serializer, protection, StateSchemaApplyHash, EventTransformHash and accumulator to the source response and signed prefix, then deserializes only authenticated, verified readable folded bytes. The lookup has a 128 MiB raw-storage and 64 MiB folded-byte cap, streams before materialization, and never returns a free typed state. Unavailable, partial, mismatched or unfenced readback bypasses the snapshot and forces authenticated replay from sequence 1; it cannot authorize tail Apply.

Protected approved-v1 snapshots have **two distinct byte domains**. First authenticate the exact original actor storage/envelope/ciphertext bytes, key, ETag, batch/version and protection metadata under the 128 MiB raw cap, with bounded provider readback before materialization. Then invoke the existing typed snapshot unprotection/readability outcome using the registered provider, key and occurrence context, bounding the readable result at 64 MiB before materialization. Only a successful readable outcome supplies the canonical folded-state bytes for tag `09`, `SnapshotDigest`, deserialization and signed state proof; ciphertext is hashed only by tag `0a` and the actor storage witness. Recheck the derived protection record and original storage hash independently. `ProtectedUnreadable`, `ProviderOpaque`, missing key/provider, ambiguous unprotection or a cap failure preserves the stored snapshot and safely bypasses to authenticated replay from sequence 1 under existing retention/erasure policy, without deleting the snapshot, treating ciphertext as state or falling back to guessed plaintext. A verified `Unprotected`/legacy no-op outcome still passes these separate storage and readable-byte checks.

The additive cumulative-prefix side record at `AggregateIdentity.MetadataKey + ":evolution-prefix"` is `HX-EV-PREFIX-STATE-1\0 || 01 || 0008` with tags `01..08`: U tenant, U domain, U aggregate ID, U aggregate type, N covered sequence, B32 accumulator, U actor batch operation ID, N actor logical version chosen before save with checked increment. Its genesis is the §6 scope genesis. After a fully authenticated seed, each actor append computes the §6 step once per newly staged contiguous event, saves the final accumulator with the event batch under the same operation, and reads both back under the same predeclared logical version and provider ETag before publishing it. A snapshot copies the verified accumulator at its covered sequence. For old streams with no side record, authenticated full replay from sequence 1 establishes a seed and stages it with the next actor-owned save or paired refold; no missing-history shortcut is allowed. A missing, conflicting or ambiguous update forces full replay and prevents a tail-cost claim. Each normal append advances from the last readback value with O(1) work per event, without rereading the entire stream at each fold.

Paged command-state request/response carries addressed scope, fixed expected head/target, page start/count/max bytes, ordered EventEvolutionProof, continuation token and final signed complete-prefix proof. Each page has at most 256 events, 64 MiB readable bytes and 2 MiB proof. Before admitting a **final** page, compute the exact encoded size of its final `EventEvolutionProof` plus purpose-07 claim, key ID, 64-byte signature, command-state-proof framing and Base64 carrier, and reserve the entire result within the outer 2 MiB `commandStateProof` cap. If the page is too large, shrink and re-sign it **before** admission or return `ProofLimit` with no Apply or authoritative completion; a signed admitted final page is never silently over cap. This proof budget is distinct from the 128 MiB actual encoded response cap. Continuation uses purpose 05 claim above plus key ID/signature codec; one stable operation ID, head, target, fingerprints, stored accumulator, effective chain and nonce span pages. For each admitted page, the receiver starts with the signed prior chain, verifies every route/effective payload and applies the §6 effective-step hash in sequence order. Its signed next token tag `10` equals that resulting chain; a next request whose token chain differs from the durable prior ledger chain is `CommandStateUnproven` before Apply. The durable operation ledger persists both accumulators and a page transcript. Transcript genesis is `SHA256("HX-EV-COMMAND-PAGES-1\0" || 01 || U operation ID || U tenant || U domain || U aggregate ID || U aggregate type || N fixed head || N target)`; each completed page advances as `SHA256("HX-EV-COMMAND-PAGE-STEP-1\0" || prior B32 || N page start || I page count || B32 SHA-256(exact EventEvolutionProof container) || B32 resulting stored accumulator || B32 resulting effective chain)`. Persist the page start/count, exact proof hash, prior/resulting values and transcript under the ledger CAS/readback; no transition may skip or repeat a sequence. Purpose-07 tag `11` signs the terminal transcript from that ledger. A zero-page empty or witnessed-snapshot completion uses the genesis and its signed anchor. The gateway stores a durable bounded spent-token ledger keyed by token digest and operation. It holds at most 65,536 entries and 64 MiB encoded bytes, retains entries at least 24 hours and issues tokens valid for 15 minutes. Capacity is reserved as described below before Apply, and full capacity returns `ContinuationCapacityHold` without authoritative progress. The old full-array adapter fills one array from successive verified pages only within §8 limits, with no extra full-stream upcast array.

The operation ledger stores the complete request fingerprint, exact original signed-request-blob key/hash, consumed-token digest, pinned response-blob key/hash, owner instance/generation/lease, next token and committed transition under CAS. An exact retry of a pinned incomplete page returns its byte-identical response without another Apply only while matching live domain scratch remains available for continuation. If scratch is lost after a receiver crash, the old operation is invalidated; the gateway returns `ReplayRestartRequired` and begins new authenticated replay from page 1. A pinned **final** response is different: its complete final state/result/proof are durable under the same ledger transition, so an exact retry returns the pinned blob without volatile scratch or Apply while the blob, **current** proof key/fingerprint, complete final prefix and 24-hour durable pin obligation remain valid. Its exact retry lookup by operation ID, request fingerprint and consumed-token digest precedes the 15-minute token-expiry check; expiry bars new Apply or continuation, not return of an already committed final response within its retained obligation. An invalid/stale final proof or changed active key/fingerprint requires source-authenticated page-1 restart even when a blob exists. Incomplete pinned pages still require a live scratch match; expired token, missing blob or lost scratch invalidates continuation and restarts from page 1. Altered requests fail even when a blob exists. A previously consumed token cannot run Apply again under its old operation. Cancellation before a proven absent pin discards private scratch; cancellation after a committed pin reports its committed truth; ambiguous outcomes use the reconciliation protocol below.

The existing `AggregateReconstructionRequest` positional constructor and bounded `POST /replay-state` whole-array route remain source/wire compatible. Add `POST /replay-state/pages` with records `PagedAggregateReconstructionRequest` and `PagedAggregateReconstructionResponse`; property names below are exact lower-camel JSON names under the pinned Web serializer, no unknown or duplicate members, and `byte[]` is canonical padded RFC 4648 Base64. Request fields are: `tenantId:string`, `domain:string`, `aggregateType:string`, `aggregateId:string`, `expectedHead:long`, `targetSequence:long`, `pageStart:long`, `pageCount:int` (0..256), `maxPageBytes:int` (1..67,108,864), `events:ReplayEventEnvelope[]` (only this verified page, with count equal to `pageCount`), `eventEvolutionProof:byte[]` (the §6 binary container, at most 2 MiB), `snapshotWitness:byte[]?` (exact §6 side-record bytes), `snapshotState:byte[]?` (exact original folded-state bytes), `continuationToken:byte[]?`, `operationId:string`, `includeTimeline:bool`, `requestId:string?`. The two snapshot fields are both absent without prefix snapshot tags `0e`/`0f` and both present together only on the first page after an actor-authenticated witness readback; the signed prefix binds their SnapshotDigest and accumulator. The receiver obtains §6 `IAuthenticatedSnapshotSource.ReadAsync` actor evidence and checks side-record scope/key/covered sequence, original v1 storage-byte hash, folded-state byte hash, state/transform/protection hashes, actor batch/version and provider ETag against its source readback and signed prefix before deserializing the looked-up folded bytes into `DomainServiceCurrentState.SnapshotState`; request-carried bytes alone never prove these values. A missing, mixed, failed or unreadable snapshot witness causes the receiver to reject and discard the post-snapshot page **before Apply**. It asks the gateway for a fresh actor-authenticated page-1 request and newly signed proof with no snapshot authority, then starts a new replay operation; it never treats a tail page as complete history or locally changes its start to 1. The same rule applies after a protection outcome or readback failure, preserving the stored snapshot. The first page has null continuation and start 1 or witnessed snapshot sequence+1 when that sequence is below `long.MaxValue`; later pages have one current signed token. `targetSequence <= expectedHead`, all values are checked before allocation and the page range cannot exceed target/head. `/replay-state/pages` admits a zero-event request **only** for the signed empty-stream or witnessed snapshot zero-tail form, including §6's terminal max-sequence sentinel; it rejects a checkpoint anchor, checkpoint proof entry or projection metadata-only form even if its signature is valid. A count-zero terminal request has `pageStart=expectedHead=targetSequence=long.MaxValue`, no continuation and no event, and it cannot issue a next token. No `target+1` arithmetic is attempted at that value.

Response fields are `operationId:string`, `returnedPageStart:long`, `returnedPageEnd:long`, `returnedPageCount:int`, `nextContinuationToken:byte[]?`, `isComplete:bool`, `finalResult:AggregateReconstructionResult?`, `finalProof:byte[]?`. For an incomplete accepted page, the next token is non-null and both final fields are null; for complete success, the next token is null, `finalResult.StateJson` is the final state and `finalProof` is the exact signed complete-prefix container reaching target. A typed failure/cancellation has no authoritative final state or completion proof. The response's range/count must match the accepted request page, including start=target+1/end=target/count=0 for an ordinary witnessed zero tail, or start=end=target=`long.MaxValue`/count=0 for the terminal sentinel. A page ending at `long.MaxValue` is final and has no next token. Add nullable `PagedContext` properties to `AggregateReconstructionRequest` without changing its positional constructor; the new endpoint maps request scope, target, one-page `Events`, `IncludeTimeline`, `RequestId` and verified proof/continuation context into the existing `IAsyncAggregateReplay.ReplayAsync(AggregateReconstructionRequest, CancellationToken)` signature. On this route `Events` never means complete history; old processors and the synchronous adapter reject non-null `PagedContext`. Gateway verifies `finalProof` against original actor head, target and accumulated pages, then durably pins the exact final result/proof/response before exposing `finalResult`. The new endpoint and DTOs are additive; old `/replay-state` remains subject to §8 full-array bounds.


`PagedRequestFingerprint = SHA256("HX-EV-PAGED-REQUEST-1\0" || 01 || taggedRequestRecord)`, where the quoted separator ends in **one NUL byte** (`00`), never the two literal characters backslash and zero. `taggedRequestRecord` begins u16 field count `0011` (17) and then ordered one-byte tags with these exact §4 encodings: `01` U tenantId, `02` U domain, `03` U aggregateType, `04` U aggregateId, `05` N expectedHead, `06` N targetSequence, `07` N pageStart, `08` I pageCount, `09` I maxPageBytes, `0a` u32 bounded event count followed by one B for each canonical **stored** `ReplayEventEnvelope` value in page order, `0b` B exact EventEvolutionProof bytes, `0c` O(B) snapshotWitness, `0d` O(B) snapshotState, `0e` O(B) continuationToken, `0f` U operationId, `10` one byte includeTimeline (`00` false, `01` true), `11` O(U) requestId. The event count equals pageCount and is at most 256; every B/O length and the complete record are checked before allocation. The stored event value uses the §4 recursive canonical JSON renderer over the exact lower-camel stored DTO names (`sequenceNumber`, `eventTypeName`, `payload`, `serializationFormat`, `metadataVersion`, `messageId`, `correlationId`, `causationId`, and present additive `storedEventContractType`, `storedPayloadVersion`, `storedSerializationFormat`, `storedEventTypeName`, `storedDigest`, `registryFingerprint`), with byte arrays as canonical padded Base64. Its raw parsed member-presence map distinguishes absent from explicit null and includes only stored provenance, never an effective payload/view or caller-declared adaptation. Unknown, duplicate or wrong-case V2 member names fail. Preserve the original stored evidence and page order; a typed-object reserialization cannot erase presence or substitute bytes for this fingerprint. The endpoint computes it before lookup and compares every retry's exact fingerprint, source evidence, **original signed proof bytes**, token and operation to the CAS-pinned request blob and ledger; changed bytes or meaning cannot receive a pinned response.

For page 1, null `continuationToken` has digest `SHA256("HX-EV-ABSENT-CONT-1\0" || 01 || 00)`; a **present empty** token is rejected at raw-presence validation before fingerprint or lease lookup. Every later token is nonempty signed exact bytes, and its digest is SHA-256 of those exact bytes, including signature. ReplayOwnerLease, ledger, pinned request/response blob keys and exact retry checks use this same null-versus-present rule; no empty-byte array aliases absence. The `/replay-state/pages` request parser rejects, by raw member presence before the stored-only PagedRequestFingerprint, any caller supplied `effectivePayload`, `effectiveEventContractType`, `effectivePayloadVersion`, `effectiveSerializationFormat`, `isAdapted`, `verifiedEffectiveEvent`, `verifiedEffectiveEvents` or any other effective/adaptation/verified-view member at any event or request level. Unknown members, including wrong-case variants, fail before lookup. Only the authenticated private reader derives effective views; a changed or unexpected member cannot retrieve a pinned response.

`AggregateReconstructionRequest` gains an additive nullable **public sealed immutable** `PagedContext` record containing public immutable `IReadOnlyList<VerifiedEffectiveEventView>` verified page views, copied exact `byte[]` EventEvolutionProof bytes, four addressed scope `string` fields, `long` expected head/target/page start/page end, `int` page count, `string` operation ID, opaque bounded `byte[]` scratch-handle token, copied exact signed `byte[]` continuation token, two copied 32-byte stored/effective accumulators and `bool` completion flag. The endpoint builds it only after actor source/proof validation. Its public constructor or factory lets an external `IAsyncAggregateReplay` implementer receive the verified page/proof, immutable scope/range and prior accumulators, plus an opaque bounded `byte[]` scratch-handle token. Constructors and getters defensively copy the token; only the gateway/domain ledger can authenticate its bytes and associate them with live private scratch. Constructors snapshot the view list and its byte fields, proof and accumulator arrays; a later caller mutation cannot change the admitted page. The gateway/domain operation ledger authenticates the token and its owner/generation and rechecks all proof/view hashes at the call boundary; no public member exposes an internal CLR type, raw mutable scratch or unverified state. The context grants the one async call a borrowed opaque handle under the operation/page fence **and a public `IPagedReplayStateSession StateSession`**. This session is supplied in process, never JSON-bound, and has `ReadPriorAsync(byte[] handle, CancellationToken): ValueTask<ReadOnlyMemory<byte>>` and `WriteSuccessorAsync(byte[] priorHandle, ReadOnlyMemory<byte> canonicalState, string serializerId, CancellationToken): ValueTask<byte[]>`. `ReadPriorAsync` authenticates the handle's scope, operation, owner instance, scratch generation and current page lease and returns a defensively copied, charged canonical prior state; the external async replay implementation can deserialize and Apply its verified page. `WriteSuccessorAsync` validates the registered serializer and canonical bytes, checks the 64 MiB state and combined 128 MiB live-scratch budgets before allocation, and CAS-creates **one** private successor under that same owner/generation/page lease. It returns only a defensively copied opaque successor handle, never a mutable reference to stored state. Both calls propagate cancellation and reject another operation, stale handle, second successor, prior-state mutation or publication of intermediate state. `PagedProgress` names exactly this successor handle; the ledger validates proof/range, resulting state hash and CAS readback, commits it once, invalidates the prior handle and clears its full old capacity. Ambiguous successor writes reconcile by operation ID and handle generation before retry, without another Apply; a failed or cancelled transition preserves the prior committed private state until no obligation refers to it. The old handle is invalidated and its full capacity cleared on a committed transition; on failure, cancellation or restart the ledger retains or clears it according to the reconciliation state, never exposes it to a caller or a second operation. Only the async `IAsyncAggregateReplay` implementation may consume `PagedContext`; every old/synchronous adapter rejects a non-null value before Apply. `AggregateReconstructionResult` gains an additive nullable **public sealed immutable** `PagedProgress` record, with a public constructor or factory, containing an opaque bounded defensively copied `byte[]` resulting scratch-handle token, resulting stored/effective accumulators, exact processed start/end/count and final-state flag. These public immutable fields have only public types and are transport-neutral; the gateway alone can commit or invalidate a handle under the operation ledger. The async implementation returns this progress after a verified page without publishing intermediate state or timeline as authority. The endpoint matches its range/chains to the signed proof and durable ledger, maps it to `PagedAggregateReconstructionResponse`, and exposes `FinalResult` only after final proof and durable pin verification. A missing, mismatched or cancelled progress returns no authoritative completion.

The signed purpose-05 token already binds operation ID and nonce. A durable `ReplayOwnerLease` keyed by `(tenant, domain, aggregate type, aggregate ID, operation ID, exact token digest)` has `HX-EV-REPLAY-OWNER-1\0 || 01 || 000e` and ordered tags `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U aggregate type, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` U operation ID, `06` B32 exact token digest, `07` U stable domain-service instance ID, `08` U authenticated endpoint ID, `09` N private scratch generation, `0a` B32 current RegistryFingerprint, `0b` B32 EventTransformHash, `0c` Q issued UTC, `0d` Q expiry UTC no earlier than token expiry, `0e` N monotonic CAS generation. Its actor/gateway-owned linearizable readback, not a caller field, is authority. The gateway selects that exact live instance for every later page; the receiver compares owner ID and scratch generation under the operation fence **before Apply**, and renews the lease/next generation before issuing the next token. Authentication of the endpoint and lease readback prevents a load balancer from routing a continuation to a different instance. If instance, lease or scratch is lost, invalidate the operation and restart authenticated replay at page 1; a deployment without enforceable affinity cannot advertise paged replay readiness. A durably committed final response is still returned from its pin without instance or scratch, subject to the current proof and retained obligation checks above.

Before any page Apply, CAS-pin an encrypted, authenticated **exact original request blob** keyed by `(tenant, scope, operation ID, page start, consumed-token digest)` containing the canonical 17-field request record, exact original signed `EventEvolutionProof`/continuation bytes, exact request-mode proof carrier bytes and their SHA-256/fingerprint. Preserve signature byte identity; a newly signed semantically equivalent claim is a different request, never an exact retry. The request blob uses the same scope authorization, 24-hour minimum retention, obligation-based CAS deletion, 128 MiB per-blob maximum and shared 1 GiB request-plus-response blob quota as response pins; reserve its capacity before Apply and return `ContinuationCapacityHold` if unavailable. Ambiguous pin creation is reconciled by exact blob readback before Apply. On retry, retrieve and compare the caller's fingerprint **and exact signed bytes** with this pinned blob before checking a previously committed response; return pinned bytes without re-signing or re-Applying. Missing/corrupt original proof bytes holds or restarts from authenticated page 1, never fabricates equivalent signatures. A pinned final response remains retrievable through this durable request blob even after owner scratch is gone.

The **gateway also pins before sending** the exact fully encoded, signed `/replay-state/pages` request bytes and exact proof carrier under that same `(tenant, scope, OperationId, pageStart, consumed-token digest)` identity. It reserves the checked request-blob quota, writes the authenticated encrypted blob, and reads back the byte-for-byte payload/hash, signature, scope and retention obligation **before** transport send; receiver-side pinning before Apply remains a separate check. A lost response or gateway restart first reads this outbound pin and resends its bytes unchanged, including the same signatures and JSON/Base64 spelling. An ambiguous outbound pin creation reconciles by operation/page and exact hash without sending a new encoding. If the pin cannot be recovered or authenticated, the gateway returns `ReplayRestartRequired`, invalidates that operation and begins a fresh page-1 operation with a new identity; it never re-signs the old operation or asks the receiver to accept semantically equivalent bytes as an exact retry. Caller cancellation after the send starts retains the pin and follows the same recovery path.

The 65,536-entry/64 MiB durable continuation ledger is a metadata index, not the response-byte store. Keep exact encoded retry responses in a separate durable CAS blob store keyed by operation ID, page start and consumed-token digest, with a **128 MiB actual encoded per-response** and **1 GiB shared request-plus-response** hard bound, expiry no earlier than the ledger's 24-hour retention. Each blob is keyed by tenant, addressed scope and operation/page/token digest, encrypted at rest with authenticated readback, and readable only by the gateway/domain principal authorized for that exact operation; cross-tenant lookup and payload logging are forbidden. After every retry, rollback and proof obligation closes (never earlier than ledger retention), CAS-delete the encrypted blob and revoke its operation access/key, then read back absence. A failed deletion or revocation holds quota/capacity for reconciliation rather than silently leaking the state copy. Startup recovery performs the same bounded cleanup; cancellation does not bypass it. The cap includes the complete Web JSON response: final state, optional timeline, proof, Base64 expansion, JSON escaping, metadata and token. The separately allowed 64 MiB raw state and 64 MiB timeline do **not** imply their combined encoded response fits. Before invoking Apply, atomically reserve a conservative checked upper bound for the requested page response and ledger transition. A final page reserves enough for its maximum admitted encoded state/timeline/proof or returns `FinalResponseLimit` before Apply; an ordinary page that cannot fit may be shrunk and re-signed **before** admission or returns `ContinuationCapacityHold`. A signed admitted page is immutable, so no post-admission shrink is allowed. If optional timeline causes `FinalResponseLimit`, a fresh authenticated **state-only** replay with `includeTimeline=false` may be started from page 1 under a new operation; no failed oversized response claims completion. State-only output remains subject to the same actual encoded cap.

Compute the page and any final state/proof in private charged scratch **after** reservation. Serialize the exact `PagedAggregateReconstructionResponse` once under the pinned Web JSON/Base64 codec and check actual length against its reservation and the 128 MiB cap. If it exceeds the cap, return `FinalResponseLimit` for a final page or `ContinuationCapacityHold` for an incomplete page, without authoritative transition. Commit by one linearizable operation: create the immutable exact response blob and, for a final page, immutable final result/proof under the same operation ID; CAS the ledger pointer to their exact hashes, request fingerprint, consumed-token digest and next-token/completion state. A backend with a serializable multi-key transaction may commit all three together. Otherwise the blob/final objects are prepared immutable first and **only** the ledger-pointer CAS is the authoritative transition; they are invisible until that pointer commits. Read back blob, ledger pointer and final result/proof under the same durable operation identity before returning response bytes or exposing/consuming a next token. The prior token remains authoritative until the pointer's commit is proven.

After **any** blob create, final-result create, ledger CAS or readback error, including a lost acknowledgement, retain reserved quota, operation ID, token and private scratch; classify the outcome as `ReplayCommitAmbiguous`. Under the same linearizable operation fence, read back **all three** objects and exact blob bytes/hash, token transition, request fingerprint and final proof/result; for an incomplete page the final-result object must be proven absent. If the pointer, blob and required final-result presence/absence exactly match the proposed commit, return the pinned bytes and committed truth without another Apply. If the old pointer and token remain unchanged and the backend proves no committed transition or in-flight CAS can later appear, release quota, discard prepared orphans and scratch, and permit a new source-authenticated page-1 replay. Any mixed, missing, stale or inconclusive evidence holds for reconciliation with quota and operation identity retained; it never releases capacity, retries Apply under the uncertain token or reports completion. A crash before commit uses the same readback classification on recovery. A crash after proven commit returns the exact pinned blob on identical retry. Incomplete committed pages require matching live scratch for **continued** Apply, otherwise `ReplayRestartRequired`; a final committed pin needs no scratch. Final retry precedes token-expiry rejection while its 24-hour obligation, current proof key/fingerprint and complete prefix remain valid; expiration still forbids new Apply or continuation. A missing/invalid final blob/proof restarts from authenticated page 1. Test a capacity failure after computation, lost CAS acknowledgement, mixed readback, lost incomplete response and lost final response after token expiry.

`NamedProjectionReadScope` is descriptive routing input, **never authorization**. The trusted query router issues one unforgeable store-verified `NamedProjectionReadCapability` after authenticating the principal and current catalog/access policy. Add nullable init-only `byte[]? ReadCapability` (`readCapability`) to the public `NamedProjectionReadScope`, preserving its constructor; the router sets a private copy of exactly 32 CSPRNG bytes, while the store looks up only SHA-256 of that token in a tenant-scoped durable CAS/readback capability index. The bound record contains authenticated principal identity, tenant, domain, HandlerRouteId, key-space, backend identity, ownership mode, optional aggregate, allowed exact logical keys or constrained prefix, allowed read operation (`Get` or `GetMany`), authoritative expiry UTC no later than five minutes after issuance, nonce and catalog/authorization revision. Raw token bytes are never logged or stored, and a caller-created scope without a live matching index record conveys no authority. On every route-aware `GetAsync` and `GetManyAsync`, `DaprReadModelStore` verifies the capability index and tenant scope under the trusted store, checks the authenticated current principal from request context and catalog/authorization revision, requires the requested store, route, keys and operation to be within its grant, and checks expiry against authoritative time **before** root or row lookup. A public constructor, copied capability for another principal, stale/revoked revision, wrong route/key or expired grant yields typed `ReadModelAccessDenied` with no existence signal, read-model bytes or partial bulk result. Root/row proof and final fence/UTC checks remain mandatory after this access check. The legacy store/key-only API cannot be used to inspect protected versioned key spaces.

An exact-key grant never broadens to a prefix when the inline 1 KiB capability record cannot hold its keys. For 1..256 requested exact logical keys, store a separate tenant-scoped immutable authenticated `HX-EV-QUERY-KEYSET-1\0 || 01 || u16 count || sorted unique U key` blob of at most 256 KiB; each key is at most 512 UTF-8 bytes under the named-index rule. The inline record stores only the blob's immutable key, B32 exact blob hash and u16 count, still within 1 KiB. Issuance reserves record **and** blob quota, stages both under one CAS transaction or a recoverable token-hash operation, reads back exact bytes and publishes the token only after both are proven durable. Every requested key is checked against the authenticated exact set before any row/root read; a changed, missing or extra key denies without existence leakage. Expiry/revocation CAS cleanup of token and blob is atomic or reconciled by token hash, blob hash and record versions; ambiguous deletion retains both quota charges until authenticated readback. A prefix grant is legal only when the caller explicitly requested that prefix and policy authorized it, never as a substitute for an exact set.

The exact-key blob has its **own** tenant-scoped active quota: at most **100,000 live key-set blobs** and **1 GiB encoded blob bytes including storage/index overhead** per tenant, separate from the capability-record limit of 100,000 live records and 128 MiB encoded index. Issuance reserves both charges under one token-hash operation before either record or blob is made active; saturation of either returns `ReadModelAccessUnavailable` before token issue or row lookup. A token and blob are retired together by authenticated expiry/revocation, CAS deletion and readback; ambiguous deletion retains **both** charges until exact record version and blob hash/absence reconcile. An orphaned blob cannot be counted free, an inline token cannot name a missing blob, and a caller never receives a partial grant.

Add `AggregateReconstructionStatus.InProgress = 3` after the existing `Succeeded = 0`, `Partial = 1` and `Failed = 2` values, and add nullable init-only `PagedProgress` to `AggregateReconstructionResult` without changing its existing eight-field positional constructor or deconstruction. For an incomplete accepted `/replay-state/pages` call, that result is exactly `Status=InProgress`, `StateJson=null`, `LastAppliedSequenceNumber` equal to the **verified completed page end only**, `FailedSequenceNumber=null`, `FailedEventType=null`, `ErrorCategory=None`, `Message=null`, `Timeline=null`, and a non-null verified `PagedProgress` bound to the ledger CAS/readback. It is neither `Succeeded` nor `Partial` and conveys no authoritative final state. The pages endpoint maps this status to `isComplete=false`, null `finalResult` and `finalProof`, and exactly one pinned non-null next token; it never publishes the intermediate result as a final result. Only a signed, completed whole prefix reaching the fixed target may return `Succeeded` with authoritative `StateJson`, `FinalResult`, no next token and a verified final proof. The old `/replay-state` route rejects `InProgress` before serializing its old response, so an old consumer cannot mistake a page for a complete reconstruction. A failed or cancelled page carries no `PagedProgress` that authorizes continuation and preserves last-good state.

For production named-projection mutations add `IVersionedNamedProjectionBatchWriter.TryCommitAsync(VerifiedNamedProjectionBatch request, CancellationToken cancellationToken): Task<ReadModelBatchResult>`. `VerifiedNamedProjectionBatch` is an immutable, defensively copied request with exact addressed `StoreName`, `TenantId`, `Domain`, `HandlerRouteId`, declared `KeySpaceId` and prefix, `OwnershipMode` (`aggregate` or `shared`), optional `AggregateType`/`AggregateId` (required for aggregate ownership and absent for shared ownership), authenticated event source proof and current effective-view digest, stable `OperationId` and enclosing `ReadModelBatchScope.BatchId`, expected certified root hash/generation, coordinator ID/fence generation, and ordered `ReadModelBatchOperation` values including kind, `ValueTypeName`, policy, expected logical ETag, TTL and bounded canonical value bytes. The trusted dispatcher creates a `VerifiedNamedProjectionBatch` only from a validated route/page and a store-verified write capability bound to the current catalog, source proof, root and fence; a caller-created instance is transport data, not authority. The writer reauthenticates that capability, catalog route/key-space/backend, source and operation identity, current root and shared fence before staging. It applies concurrency to the committed logical value, stages immutable shadow rows, computes exact batch/readback and complete-row-set proofs, then publishes only a certified root/checkpoint pointer under §6. It returns `ReadModelBatchResult` only after readback, with conflict, retryable and indeterminate outcomes distinct. Both `IVerifiedDomainProjectionHandler` and migrated `SharedProjectionEpochCoordinator` use this one seam. The existing six-field `ReadModelBatchScope(StoreName,TenantId,Domain,AggregateId,ProjectionType,BatchId)` remains unchanged: map its store/tenant/domain, stable BatchId and projection type explicitly into the new request, verify that its aggregate ID equals the source for aggregate ownership, and for shared ownership treat its aggregate ID only as original source-delivery provenance rather than the shared key owner. Its old opaque marker hash never substitutes for HandlerRouteId, key-space, root or fence authority. Generic `IReadModelBatchStore.ExecuteAsync` remains source-compatible for ordinary keys but is denied write access to protected versioned generations without this authenticated adapter.

Finite-leaf expiry scheduling is incremental: at a certified root transition register a job only for a new/changed finite leaf, or for a successor explicitly rebased under the current root; unchanged jobs retain their original visibility generation and expiry by reference and are never re-enqueued for every root. At most one active job exists per finite logical leaf. A superseded job is CAS tombstoned and compacted only after its successor/root obligation is read back; ambiguous deletion is reconciled by original job ID before releasing capacity. Cap each encoded job at 1 KiB, active jobs at 1,000,000, and the active scheduler index at 1 GiB per route/key-space. Reserve checked capacity before root publication; saturation is a typed capacity hold with prior certified root still authoritative. The §6 stale-root rebase, exact maintenance OperationId and fail-closed query expiry checks remain mandatory. The shared global-ordinal log has at most 1 GiB hot bytes. Seal contiguous content-addressed segments of at most 64 MiB into a tenant-scoped authenticated archive; hot plus retained archive is at most 64 GiB per route/key-space. Each archival transition commits exact segment start/end ordinals, content hash, predecessor/successor anchor and current root under the same fence, then reads it back. Replay proves a gapless ordinal chain, every source proof/root hash and every archive link before Apply. Segments referenced by replay, backup, rollback, retry or source-history obligations remain; compact only after a durable certified checkpoint/retention decision proves no reference. Reaching either quota holds the next transition before its ordinal/root commit; authoritative history is never dropped to make room.

The §6 `NamedProjectionReadCapability` index is tenant-scoped and quota reserved **before** token issuance: at most 100,000 live records and 128 MiB encoded index bytes per tenant, with each record at most 1 KiB and lifetime at most five minutes. The issuer charges exact encoded record and index overhead using checked arithmetic under the capability-store CAS fence. `ReadModelAccessUnavailable` on saturation grants no token and no fallback read. Expired records are CAS-deleted only after authenticated expiry and exact record-version readback; an ambiguous delete is reconciled by token hash and version before releasing quota, and a changed/revoked record is never recycled as an active grant. This quota is independent of the 256-key, 8 MiB proof, 64 MiB result and 128 MiB scratch query limits.

## 7. Publication, transport and side effects

The single delivery body is binary: ASCII `HX-EV-DELIVERY-BODY-1\0`, codec byte `01`, u16 field count `0025` (37), then the following **tag `01` through `25` in this exact order**, using §4 scalar types. The body contains no digest, signature, key ID or attestation field. `DeliveryDigest = SHA256("HX-EV-DELIVERY-1\0" || 01 || B(exact body bytes))`. Publisher pins original readable bytes and their format, not current-version upcast output; retries send the same pinned body and attestation bytes for a MessageId.

| Tag | Body field and encoding | Tag | Body field and encoding |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `01` | StoredDigest B32 | `02` | MessageId U |
| `03` | tenant U | `04` | domain U |
| `05` | aggregate ID U | `06` | aggregate type U |
| `07` | sequence N | `08` | global position N |
| `09` | timestamp T | `0a` | correlation ID U |
| `0b` | causation ID O(U) | `0c` | user ID O(U) |
| `0d` | DomainServiceVersion O(U) | `0e` | stored EventTypeName U |
| `0f` | stored MetadataVersion I | `10` | stored EventContractType O(U) |
| `11` | stored PayloadVersion O(I) | `12` | stored SerializationFormat U |
| `13` | readable SerializationFormat U | `14` | original readable payload B |
| `15` | ReadableExtensions O(M) | `16` | protection state U |
| `17` | protection metadata version I | `18` | protection scheme O(U) |
| `19` | protection key alias O(U) | `1a` | protection content hint O(U) |
| `1b` | protection flags O(M) | `1c` | CloudEvent specversion U (`1.0`) |
| `1d` | CloudEvent id U | `1e` | CloudEvent type U |
| `1f` | CloudEvent source U | `20` | CloudEvent subject O(U) |
| `21` | CloudEvent time O(T) | `22` | CloudEvent datacontenttype U |
| `23` | pubsub component U | `24` | topic U |
| `25` | application routing headers M |  |  |

`ReadableExtensions` is truly `O(M)`: absent, JSON null and empty map hash differently. Keep stored raw extensions, including original `eventstore.protection` bytes and presence, separate from readable publication extensions. For the first pin, copy the stored map, replace `eventstore.protection` with the canonical serialized provider-returned unprotect metadata exactly as the current carrier `Write` does, preserve every other entry, and pin that resulting readable map (necessarily present), original readable bytes and readable format. Retries use pinned bytes and never rerun a variable restamp. Compare stored map only with source evidence and readable map only with delivery bytes. The codec still preserves null/empty/absent for authenticated legacy forms. Protection flags use `O(M)` and preserve null/empty/absent where raw evidence distinguishes them. `eventstore.protection` raw bytes remain in StoredDigest; derived protection fields in delivery are compared to authenticated source. Extension bounds are at most 64 entries, each key 256 UTF-8 bytes, each value 4096 UTF-8 bytes and total canonical extension bytes 256 KiB; full encoded per-event metadata at most 512 KiB. Corpus/provider preflight measures admitted history and blocks readiness if a valid source exceeds a newly chosen capability bound; no silent truncation.

For retained V1 source, a raw extension key that is a case-insensitive variant of `eventstore.protection` retains its exact original spelling/bytes and historical exact-key interpretation. The publisher MUST NOT add the exact key beside that variant during readable restamping, delete the variant, or reinterpret it as the reserved key. A source containing both spellings is malformed duplicate-name evidence. A variant-only source holds publication/readiness for an approved alias mapping or separately approved offline evidence migration; source read can retain and diagnose it without changing bytes. This exception overrides the ordinary first-pin restamp rule above only by holding, never by emitting a silently normalized delivery.

The delivery claim is signed with purpose 02; tag `0a` is immutable trusted-signer issuance UTC, copied into its exact durable pin obligation. Verification compares the signed time to that purpose-02 key's valid-from/valid-to interval and the obligation's stored time before permitting historic-key use; a separately stored earlier time cannot extend validity. A separate binary CloudEvents extension **`hyevattestation`** carries `HX-EV-ATTEST-1\0`, codec `01`, u16 count `0003`, tag `01` B delivery-claim bytes, tag `02` U key ID, tag `03` B exactly 64-byte signature, with no extra bytes. Decoded attestation is at most 8 KiB. The canonical CloudEvent `data` is the exact binary delivery body, content type **`application/vnd.hexalith.eventstore.v2+octet-stream`**. JSON structured mode uses `data_base64` with padded RFC 4648 Base64 and represents Binary `hyevattestation` by padded RFC 4648 Base64; reject simultaneous `data`/`data_base64`, noncanonical padding/alphabet and duplicate attributes. HTTP binary mode carries raw body bytes and `ce-hyevattestation` as the binding's canonical padded Base64 Binary string. Decode both modes to the **same body and extension bytes before hashing/signature**. Pin canonical bytes plus the exact mode-specific transport mapping. A provider unable to expose/send exact decoded bytes, preserve raw headers or cap ingress **before** allocation fails readiness; generic object publication is insufficient. Raw HTTP/DAPR ingress caps whole received body at 128 MiB and attestation at 8 KiB before parsing.

CloudEvent `id` equals persisted MessageId, `type` equals pinned stored-form `EventTypeName` (V2 canonical; V1 exact registered alias), `source` equals the exact registered publisher source URI, `specversion` is `1.0`, and optional subject/time, content type, component and topic equal the signed body/pin before dedup or routing. New application routing headers are exactly `hx-tenant-id`, `hx-domain`, `hx-aggregate-id`, `hx-aggregate-type`, `hx-event-contract-type`, `hx-payload-version`. The publisher renders exact lowercase ASCII application HTTP header names in binary mode; the receiver folds received HTTP header names to lowercase ASCII before duplicate detection and comparison with the signed map, so legal header-name case variation alone is accepted while changed values, duplicate case variants and unknown `hx-*` fail. In DAPR they are metadata keys `metadata.hx-tenant-id`, `metadata.hx-domain`, `metadata.hx-aggregate-id`, `metadata.hx-aggregate-type`, `metadata.hx-event-contract-type`, `metadata.hx-payload-version` surfaced by authenticated raw ingress. They are **not** CloudEvents extensions. Each is required on new delivery, lowercase-ASCII named, unique even by case variant and sorted by unsigned UTF-8 name into M. For V2, contract type and version headers compare to the stored pair. For V1 stored pair absent/null, derive those two header values from the **exact registered domain-scoped alias/source-version descriptor**, keep the stored pair absent/null in the delivery body, and bind the derived values in this signed header map and active RegistryFingerprint. The receiver compares them to that alias resolution, never to nonexistent stored pair fields. The other four headers compare to signed addressed scope. Unknown `hx-*` fails; broker-added non-`hx-*` headers are ignored and never influence route. `hx-payload-version` is canonical invariant ASCII unsigned decimal 1..1024, with no sign, space or leading zero. Parse and re-emit before comparing signed version. `MapEventStoreDomainEvents` builds a `VerifiedDeliveryContext` from raw authenticated transport containing exact core attributes, component/topic/body/headers; DTO attestation properties are read-only derived views, not independent authority.

`MapEventStoreDomainEvents` selects ingress by authenticated transport content type and bounded raw bytes **before** deserializing any DTO. The legacy flat-JSON route remains for queued V1 messages. It parses under the same raw size/depth/duplicate-name bounds, authenticates source component/topic and legacy addressed metadata, and performs trusted gateway lookup by scoped MessageId. From the authenticated original publication/configuration revision for that physical subscription, it resolves the **complete immutable set** of addressed logical HandlerRouteIds before processing or acknowledging one old broker delivery; current membership or a broker-attempt ID cannot add, omit or rename routes. The batch handoff operation ID is deterministically derived from separately encoded §4 U fields `(component, topic, physical subscription ID, MessageId, original configuration revision)`, and its durable manifest stores the sorted complete route-ID set, original event key/scope/StoredDigest, original JSON identity and exact shared binary pin/send intent. Each route record retains the unique key `(component, topic, physical subscription ID, HandlerRouteId, MessageId)` with separately encoded U fields; scope and digest are compared as values, never used to create a second key for changed evidence. Under the global MessageId reservation and one-pin CAS, a linearizable batch transaction creates or returns **all** route records, the one shared attested binary pin and shared send intent. If the backend lacks a multi-record transaction, a batch-wide CAS manifest owns staged records and an atomic committed pointer; no individual record or send is active until the manifest, all records and pin read back as one exact complete set. A partial or ambiguous batch remains unacknowledged and is reconciled by the same operation ID, without effect or a second pin. Redelivery reauthenticates source/configuration, recomputes the complete set, and compares every route record, shared pin and manifest bytes; changed source, route set or pin is `LegacyHandoffConflict` and never creates a second effect. The old JSON broker delivery is acknowledged **only after the complete manifest, every required route record and shared send intent/pin are durable and read back**. A crash before that point leaves it unacknowledged; a crash after it but before ack makes redelivery find and reuse the full set. The outbox sends the one exact pinned binary delivery until durable broker acceptance; every logical route independently uses its marker and effect receipt, so one completed route cannot suppress another. A source lookup, batch readback or broker failure keeps the old JSON unacknowledged. Legacy JSON cannot assert `isAdapted` or bypass reservation/effect checks. The new `application/vnd.hexalith.eventstore.v2+octet-stream` route decodes the exact body and `hyevattestation`, builds `VerifiedDeliveryContext`, and verifies body, transport, signature and effective route before DTO binding/handler selection. JSON and binary binders never reinterpret each other's bytes. Reconcile old broker unacked counts, full handoff batches, outbox states and target acceptance before removing JSON ingress.

The durable legacy handoff manifest has exact canonical bytes `HX-EV-LEGACY-HANDOFF-1\0 || 01 || 0017` with ascending §4 tags: `01` U original broker component, `02` U topic, `03` U physical subscription ID, `04` N authenticated configuration revision, `05` U exact MessageId, `06` U tenant, `07` U domain, `08` U aggregate type, `09` U aggregate ID, `0a` U event storage key, `0b` N sequence, `0c` B32 StoredDigest, `0d` N exact original JSON body length, `0e` B32 SHA-256 of its **exact received bytes**, `0f` B exact authenticated original CloudEvent core/header identity under the §7 transport codec, `10` B32 canonical decoded stored-event identity/payload/protection hash, `11` B sorted unique complete logical HandlerRouteId set encoded as u32 count then U IDs, `12` U deterministic handoff OperationId, `13` B32 exact shared binary pin hash, `14` B32 exact decoded binary body hash, `15` B32 exact binary attestation hash, `16` B exact immutable shared send-intent bytes, `17` B32 exact original-JSON side-record hash. Tag `10` is exactly `SHA256("HX-EV-LEGACY-DECODED-1\0" || 01 || U tenant || U domain || U aggregate type || U aggregate ID || U event key || N sequence || U MessageId || B32 StoredDigest || B(§4 canonical decoded original stored-event JSON value) || B(exact decoded readable payload bytes) || B(exact derived protection-record bytes) || B(exact tag-`0f` core/header identity bytes))`; the canonical renderer retains all known/unknown legacy members and null/absent distinctions, so raw whitespace is not an identity input. The complete handoff manifest is capped at 2 MiB and its route set at the authenticated 4,096-member physical-subscription bound before nested allocation; the send-intent B is capped at 1 MiB. The side record is tenant-scoped authenticated encrypted bounded storage keyed by that OperationId; it contains the exact original JSON body and core/header bytes, is capped by the admitted 128 MiB raw-carrier bound, and is read back byte-for-byte before handoff commit or old-message acknowledgement. Before constructing the binary pin, parse those original bytes with the frozen legacy codec and compare **every** stored event field, readable payload and protection state with authenticated actor source and the newly rendered binary body under the frozen binary codec. Different whitespace can produce equivalent decoded content only after exact original broker pin/body bytes and decoded fields authenticate; changed content, scope, route set, side-record bytes or configuration revision is `LegacyHandoffConflict`. The manifest, original-body side record, all route records and send intent share one committed handoff pointer; partial readback cannot acknowledge the original JSON delivery. Handoff/side-record retention lasts through broker retry, rollback, route-effect and late-delivery obligations.

A queued original JSON delivery may perform **one explicit JSON-to-binary pin migration** only when authenticated actor source, original broker component/topic/physical-subscription/configuration-revision evidence and old publication/outbox record prove the same MessageId, scope, StoredDigest, original JSON identity and **complete addressed logical route set**, and independently prove no prior binary pin or send. Under the global MessageId CAS, install one durable migration obligation and the exact new attested binary pin plus the batch-wide handoff manifest and **every** route record above. This is a one-time transition from proved JSON publication, not a second global pin. Read back obligation, shared pin/send intent, manifest and every route record before acknowledging the old JSON delivery. A repeated JSON delivery resolves to those same bytes and complete set. Ambiguous prior binary send, missing historical route-set/configuration proof, incomplete batch or conflicting pin holds `LegacyHandoffConflict`/reconciliation without acknowledgement or effect. Current publisher configuration and broker attempt ID cannot supply missing historical route identity.

Binary readiness uses three exact version-1 claims, independently of the immutable event delivery pin. Each starts with its ASCII separator ending NUL, codec `01`, a big-endian u16 field count and ascending one-byte tags under §4; unknown, missing, repeated, out-of-order or trailing fields fail. `RegistryFingerprint` is the raw 32-byte current **domain-scoped** value, not its hex text. One claim names exactly one domain; a multi-domain subscription supplies one independent entry and lease per domain. All U strings use strict UTF-8 and the existing domain, route and key grammars where applicable.

| Claim | Exact separator/count and tagged fields in order |
| --- | --- |
| MembershipClaim | `HX-EV-MEMBERSHIP-1\0`, `0008`: `01` U domain, `02` U authenticated component, `03` U topic, `04` N positive monotonic revision, `05` B32 RegistryFingerprint, `06` B member set, `07` Q issued UTC, `08` Q expires UTC. |
| ConsumerConfiguration | `HX-EV-CONSUMER-CONFIG-1\0`, `000e`: `01` U consumer identity, `02` U domain, `03` U component, `04` U topic, `05` U stable broker subscription ID, `06` U stable HandlerRouteId (a stable external route ID for external members), `07` U canonical event-type selector, `08` U tenant-scope rule, `09` B32 binary-carrier capability hash, `0a` U duplicate-safety mode, `0b` U dedup/receipt-provider ID, `0c` B32 provider-backed duplicate-probe receipt digest, `0d` U exact provider backend identity, `0e` N configuration revision equal to MembershipClaim revision. |
| ConsumerLeaseClaim | `HX-EV-CONSUMER-LEASE-1\0`, `000f`: `01` U consumer identity, `02` U domain, `03` U component, `04` U topic, `05` N revision, `06` B32 SHA-256 of exact MembershipClaim, `07` B32 configuration hash from its member row, `08` B32 RegistryFingerprint, `09` B32 binary-carrier capability hash, `0a` U duplicate-safety mode (`DurableDedup` or `ProviderIdempotency`), `0b` U provider ID, `0c` B32 duplicate-probe receipt digest, `0d` U provider backend identity, `0e` Q issued UTC, `0f` Q expires UTC. |

| PublicationFenceClaim | `HX-EV-PUBLICATION-FENCE-1\0`, `0009`: `01` U domain, `02` U component, `03` U topic, `04` N revision, `05` B32 SHA-256 of exact MembershipClaim, `06` B32 RegistryFingerprint, `07` U publisher operation ID, `08` Q expires UTC, `09` B exactly 16-byte broker nonce. |

The `ConsumerLeaseClaim` row above is the historical `000f` lease codec and remains byte-for-byte unchanged, including V23 fixture literals. Its tag `07` hashes an exact ConsumerConfiguration row. For production effect-mode admission use `HX-EV-CONSUMER-CONFIG-2\0 || 02 || 000f`, preserving tags `01..0a` with their declared types/meaning, then `0b` U effect mode (`AtomicStore` or `ProviderIdempotency`), `0c` U dedup/receipt-provider ID, `0d` B32 provider-backed V3 duplicate-probe receipt digest, `0e` U exact provider backend identity and `0f` N active configuration revision. Its hash occupies the same lease tag-`07` slot; a V23 legacy configuration row remains historical fixture evidence, not production effect-mode readiness. Broker/subscriber follow the lease hash to this authenticated V2 row and verify effect mode at **row tag `0b`**; compare V2 row tags `0a`/`0c`/`0d`/`0e` to lease tags `0a`/`0b`/`0c`/`0d` respectively, and compare V2 row tag `0f` to the active lease/membership revision and current catalog before send or effect. No lease field is invented or its historical count changed.

The member set is u32 count followed by each `U consumerIdentity || B32 configurationHash`, sorted by unsigned UTF-8 consumer identity; duplicate identities fail.
For a physical subscription with multiple handlers, the broker enumerates one stable logical consumer identity per `(physical subscription ID, HandlerRouteId)`, encoded `U physical subscription ID || U HandlerRouteId` and rendered as canonical lowercase hex in ConsumerConfiguration tag `01` and the membership set. Every logical member has its own configuration row and route-specific lease, though the physical subscription receives one pinned message. Tag `07` is the canonical type for one type or exact `*` for an attested nonempty multi-type set. **Production tag `09` for both single-type and multi-type routes** is `SHA256("HX-EV-BINARY-CAP-2\0" || 01 || B32 base binary-carrier capability hash || B32 current HandlerCompatibilityHash || B32 EffectProviderConfigurationHash || B(exact route-capability evidence))`. `EffectProviderConfigurationHash = SHA256("HX-EV-EFFECT-CONFIG-1\0" || 01 || U effect mode || U receipt-provider ID || U backend ID || B32 canonical provider-options hash)`. Internal route evidence is `B(exact 52 primary row) || O(B(exact 43 verified row)) || u32 extra-row count || each B(exact 58 row) sorted as §5`; a single-type route uses zero extras and still binds its handler/filter/state compatibility and effect provider. External evidence is the exact signed carrier below. Changing any route, handler, filter, state or effect configuration advances membership revision and invalidates old leases. The broker checks all logical members behind a physical subscription under one revision before the one pinned send. The unchanged V23 single-member fixture and older multi-type formula are historical codec examples only; its literal `consumer` identity is insufficient for production.

`ExternalRouteCapabilityManifest = "HX-EV-EXTERNAL-ROUTE-1\0" || 01 || 000d` has ordered tags `01` U domain, `02` U physical subscription ID, `03` U stable external HandlerRouteId, `04` B route set, `05` B32 filter/config digest, `06` B32 handler implementation digest, `07` U effect mode, `08` U receipt-provider ID, `09` U provider backend ID, `0a` B32 EffectProviderConfigurationHash, `0b` B32 HandlerCompatibilityHash, `0c` B32 ExternalDependencyClosureHash, `0d` B exact external dependency-closure record bytes. Route set B contains u32 positive count and sorted unique `(U canonical type, I current version, U format)` rows. The closure record is `HX-EV-EXTERNAL-CLOSURE-1\0 || 01 || u32 positive row count || sorted G-format §5 dependency rows || u32 positive root count || sorted root records || u32 edge count || sorted edge records`, capped at 1 MiB. A root record is `U role || U logical load identity || U dependency kind || B32 expanded-options/configuration hash`, with role exactly `handler`, `validator`, `filter`, `serializer`, `registration`, `effect-provider` or `receipt-provider`; every executable role used by the route has a root. An edge record is `U parent logical load identity || U parent kind || U child logical load identity || U child kind`, sorted by those four separately encoded keys. Every root and edge endpoint resolves to exactly one G row, every reachable managed/native load has a row, and no row is unreferenced by a root or transitive edge. G rows bind exact file bytes/ABI/loader-context hashes; roots bind exact options/configuration. Duplicate roots/edges, cycles not present in the actual loader graph, missing edges or a different loaded graph fail. A named root with no dependency still has its own G row and root record. `ExternalDependencyClosureHash = SHA256(exact complete closure record bytes)`; tag `0c` must equal this hash of tag `0d`, and both are covered by the purpose-`0b` signature. Its exact §6 carrier is signed for purpose `0b` by a predeclared broker-trusted external capability authority; consumer self-signing is insufficient. Manifest tag `0b` MUST equal `SHA256("HX-EV-EXTERNAL-HANDLER-1\0" || 01 || U domain || U external HandlerRouteId || B exact route set || B32 filter/config digest || B32 handler implementation digest || B32 EffectProviderConfigurationHash || B32 ExternalDependencyClosureHash)`; the broker recomputes it. Before lease issue and at active membership revision, the broker authority authenticates the endpoint's sealed loader graph and exact file/config hashes against every row, rejects duplicate/missing/dynamic loads and requires a new signed manifest/revision after any change. A self-reported closure without independent broker-authority verification is insufficient. The broker verifies signature, scope, route set, compatibility hash, closure and version-3 provider probe before using those exact carrier bytes in production tag-`09`. External multi-type uses `*` only with this manifest; unknown types are never admitted by wildcard.

Production duplicate-safety readiness requires **probe receipt version 3**, exact bytes `HX-EV-DEDUP-PROBE-3\0 || 03 || 0015`, then ordered tags `01..15`: `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U aggregate type, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` U test MessageId, `06` B32 test DeliveryDigest, `07` U HandlerRouteId A, `08` U distinct HandlerRouteId B, `09` U effect mode, `0a` U provider backend identity, `0b` N configuration revision, `0c` I A attempts (3: injected attempt, retry, duplicate), `0d` I B attempts (2: first and duplicate), `0e` U crash point (exact `effect-before-receipt-commit`), `0f` I A committed-effect count after injected crash (0 or 1), `10` I A durable-receipt count then (equal to `0f`), `11` I final A committed-effect count (1), `12` I final B committed-effect count (1), `13` B32 final A receipt digest, `14` B32 final B receipt digest, `15` B32 authenticated provider query/commit transcript digest. Both routes receive the same scoped MessageId and DeliveryDigest; only HandlerRouteId differs in their seven-part production effect keys. Inject a crash between intended effect and receipt commit on A, restart, query its durable effect/receipt, retry A, deliver B and duplicate both. If A committed before crash, tags `0f`/`10` are both 1; otherwise both are 0 and retry commits once. Final route-specific effect and receipt counts are exactly one each, never MessageId-only dedup. The authenticated transcript binds both keys, crash boundary, pre/post queries, commits and duplicate attempts; broker independently queries and checks both route receipts. Its digest in tag `15` is SHA-256 of the exact provider-signed `HX-EV-DEDUP-TRANSCRIPT-3\0 || 03 || U provider backend identity || N configuration revision || u32 6 || rows` carrier, capped at 8 KiB before signing or decoding. The six ordered rows are A injected attempt, A durable query after restart, A retry, B first attempt, A duplicate and B duplicate. Each row is `B(exact seven-part §4 U/U/U/U/U/B32/U effect key) || I attempt ordinal || one-byte operation` (`01` call or `02` read-only query) `|| one-byte observed commit` (`00` absent or `01` committed) `|| I cumulative committed-effect count || I cumulative durable-receipt count || U provider commit version/ETag` (empty only when absent) `|| O(B32 receipt digest)`. The provider signs the complete transcript through the exact §6 signed carrier with purpose `0c` and a separately trusted receipt-attestation key; tag `15` hashes that complete carrier including its key ID and signature. The broker verifies the signature, exact backend/configuration revision, both receipt queries and all six rows; a caller-supplied transcript is not evidence. For a committed row, its `U provider commit version/ETag` is the canonical ASCII decimal of receipt tag `0d` (no sign or leading zeros) followed by `:` and the exact nonempty receipt tag `0e` ETag; split only at the first colon. Its present receipt digest is SHA-256 of the complete canonical `EventEffectReceipt` record in §7, and tags `13`/`14` equal the final A/B row digests respectively. The broker/provider reads back that complete record and compares its seven-part key, mode, backend, result digest and actual commit fields; an unknown record field, version disagreement or digest without its record fails readiness. Production ConsumerConfiguration V2 tag `0d` and the unchanged ConsumerLeaseClaim tag `0c` bind SHA-256 of these exact V3 bytes under the current revision/backend; the historical V23 ConsumerConfiguration V1 tag `0c` remains fixture-only. Missing route, same-route pair, another route's receipt or unauthenticated transcript holds readiness. V23 V1 and earlier V2 framing are historical examples, insufficient for production. Route B in this V3 probe is an isolated provider-local test route on the **same effect mode, backend identity, provider options and configuration revision** as route A; it has a distinct HandlerRouteId and exact seven-part effect key, and cannot execute a live business effect. The provider provisions and tears down B under its authenticated test namespace while retaining the signed receipt/transcript. A live route's readiness requires a separate V3 probe for **each** effect mode/backend/configuration revision it uses; the probe for backend `store-a` never certifies another backend or option set. The broker compares each live route's effect configuration hash to that probe's authenticated backend/revision and requires both route-specific durable receipts; no test-route receipt is accepted as a live event effect receipt.
`configurationHash = SHA256(exact ConsumerConfiguration bytes)`. Broker authority verifies active rows, domain/fingerprint/revision, current HandlerCompatibilityHash and effect configuration, the V3 crash/two-route transcript and two independent durable receipts before signing membership/leases. The historical ConsumerLeaseClaim `000f` codec is unchanged and does not directly repeat an effect-mode field. At admission, follow its tag `07` exact ConsumerConfiguration hash to the authenticated active configuration row, verify that row's tag `0b` effect mode, provider/backend/probe, current membership revision, tag `09` carrier capability and lease signature/expiry. A changed mode with unchanged lease bytes changes the configuration hash and fails before publish or acknowledgement. A changed registry, handler, filter, state codec or effect provider/configuration advances membership revision and invalidates old leases. The capability hash binds exact binary/structured CloudEvents codecs, content type, attestation extension, routing-header verifier and route/effect evidence above. A configured provider name, V23 probe-V1 or single-route V2 receipt cannot substitute. Probe evidence remains durable and support-safe.

Each signed carrier is exactly `B(claim bytes) || U(purpose-specific key ID) || B(exact 64-byte IEEE P1363 signature)` with no trailing bytes, using §6 `HX-EV-SIG-1\0` framing and purposes `08` membership, `09` consumer lease, `0a` publication fence. The broker/component authority signs membership and fence; it signs leases or delegates them to an authenticated predeclared per-domain/component/topic authority. Authority keys and purposes are pinned separately from event route/delivery trust, with exact SPKI, validity and revocation checks. Neither event key nor consumer self-signing authorizes these claims. Reject noncanonical member order, nonpositive or reused revision, issued-after-expiry, expired claim, wrong purpose/key, mismatched domain/fingerprint/configuration, or a fence expiry later than either membership expiry or the **earliest active member lease expiry**.

The authoritative broker persists membership revision, exact signed member set/configuration hashes, matching leases and publication fences under one linearizable serialization boundary. `TryPublishAtRevision(domain, component, topic, expectedRevision, fenceNonce, operationId, exactCanonicalPinDigest, exactModeTransportBytes)` atomically checks trusted broker UTC, membership and every active matching lease's issued/expiry interval, domain/fingerprint/configuration/probe hashes, fence expiry and nonce, exact active revision/member set and operation ID **at acceptance**; a membership activation or lease expiry before acceptance rejects without bytes. A joined/changed consumer cannot become active until its new probe and lease pass. A broker lacking this compare-and-accept primitive holds binary publishing with `ConsumerMembershipFenceUnavailable`. One global MessageId pin and one operation ID per shared component/topic serve all subscribers; no per-subscriber binary resend is created. Publisher obtains and prechecks fresh claims on every send, but its precheck never substitutes for broker acceptance-time checks.

`ExactCanonicalPinDigest = SHA256("HX-EV-BROKER-PIN-1\0" || 01 || B(exact decoded delivery body) || B(exact decoded hyevattestation))`; the body already contains the signed §7 CloudEvent core and routing-header fields (tags `1c..25`), so no unsigned transport-only broker header enters this digest. A global pin stores those canonical bytes and the deterministic renderer version for each permitted transport mode. It also retains exact original outbound bytes for same-mode retries. Binary mode uses the raw body and canonical padded Base64 extension; structured mode uses exact canonical padded Base64 `data_base64` and Binary extension. A broker mode conversion is accepted only if strict decoding gives the same pinned body, attestation, signed core attributes and routing metadata **and** exact canonical rendering for the received mode. Unknown attributes, noncanonical Base64 or changed decoded content/destination are `DeliveryPinConflict`; arbitrary byte equality between two different modes is not required. A same-mode retry must use its retained exact outbound bytes. No delivery/marker identity depends on unsigned transport-only headers.

The deterministic **structured** renderer emits one UTF-8 JSON CloudEvent object with keys sorted by unsigned UTF-8 name bytes, no insignificant whitespace and the §4 options-text string escaping rule. Required keys are `specversion`, `id`, `source`, `type`, `datacontenttype`, `data_base64` and `hyevattestation`; optional `subject` and `time` appear only when signed as present. `data_base64` and `hyevattestation` use canonical padded RFC 4648 Base64 of the exact pinned binary body/attestation. No `data` member or unknown attribute is allowed. Event `datacontenttype` is the signed `application/vnd.hexalith.eventstore.v2+octet-stream`; outer HTTP content type is `application/cloudevents+json`. The deterministic **binary** renderer uses the exact pinned raw body as HTTP/DAPR data, event content type as outer content type, and exact lowercase `ce-specversion`, `ce-id`, `ce-source`, `ce-type`, optional `ce-subject`, optional `ce-time`, and `ce-hyevattestation` with canonical padded Base64. Both renderers use the same canonical signed `time` text when present: seven fractional digits, original signed T offset, `Z` for zero offset and `+HH:MM`/`-HH:MM` otherwise. Application routing headers/metadata are the exact six signed lower-case `hx-*` keys already defined above, sorted as M; they remain outside the structured JSON CloudEvent object. Publisher pins the renderer version, exact canonical event object/body and application header map, plus actual outbound mode bytes. Ingress rejects duplicate/unknown event attributes, alternate Base64, changed signed fields, changed routing metadata or noncanonical received-mode rendering before comparing the decoded canonical pin. Hop-by-hop transport headers remain unsigned and cannot affect route or identity.

The broker's `TryPublishAtRevision` is idempotent for `(domain, component, topic, operationId)` and the exact canonical pin digest: an identical retry after an accepted send returns the same durable acceptance receipt with no second publication; any changed digest or same-mode outbound bytes conflicts. A durable rejected attempt proves no bytes were accepted and may use a renewed fence/nonce under the **same** operation and pin; accepted is terminal, and an unknown attempt cannot be renewed until receipt reconciliation proves rejection. A durable authenticated `GetPublicationReceipt(domain, component, topic, operationId, exactCanonicalPinDigest)` query returns only `Accepted`, `Rejected` or `Unknown`, with persisted revision, 16-byte nonce, accepted transport mode, SHA-256 of exact accepted mode transport bytes and SHA-256 of the exact original request mode bytes. Its response is read under the broker authority and scoped to that operation/digest; `Accepted` must match the pinned digest, original request-mode hash, accepted mode's deterministic renderer hash and revision. The broker stores both request and accepted modes/bytes under the same operation; an identical same-mode request returns its existing receipt even if the broker converted the accepted carrier, while any changed request bytes conflict. An ambiguous send/ack **first queries this receipt** and, if accepted, records that truth without another send; if rejected, obtains a fresh current fence and retries the same pin/operation ID under the broker's idempotency rule; if unknown or lookup unavailable, it holds with no new nonce/send. Changed bytes under an operation ID are always conflict. Durable receipt retention covers all event pin, retry, rollback and handoff obligations; expiry cannot turn an unresolved send into a fresh operation. §11.3 gives admitted signed broker claim vectors, distinct from event trust.

Publisher cutover is scoped to each affected domain/component/topic. Enumerate every internal and external active consumer from the authoritative membership claim and verify its exact signed lease, provider-backed duplicate probe and current RegistryFingerprint before any V2 binary send; unknown/unready members hold. A join, leave, configuration or fingerprint change increments the revision and invalidates existing fences/leases as described above. The publisher keeps one global pinned send per shared topic and retains old JSON ingress until queued legacy traffic, handoff outboxes and broker acceptance have drained with durable evidence. Binary decoding alone is never a cutover proof.

For an existing `CommandEnvelope`, preparation `OperationId` is **exactly the original command `MessageId`**, validated for the addressed scope before the first prepare CAS and carried unchanged through gateway, actor retry and result lookup. Event MessageIds allocated later are separate. A non-command batch append caller supplies an additive required stable client OperationId; missing, changed or freshly generated-on-retry values fail before volatile generation. The same rule covers a zero-event no-op. An immutable client/gateway `OperationId` and the **canonical original command or batch input** define append preparation identity before any timestamp, MessageId, global position, protected payload, sidecar signature or result is generated. `AppendPreparationIdentity = SHA256("HX-EV-APPEND-PREP-1\0" || 01 || U OperationId || U tenant || U domain || U aggregateType || U aggregateId || B(canonical original request value under the §4 strict JSON renderer))`. The raw request and parsed presence map are bounded, authenticated and frozen before hashing; volatile generated fields are absent from this identity. The global linearizable preparation CAS key is `append-preparation:` plus lowercase hex `SHA256("HX-EV-APPEND-PREP-KEY-1\0" || 01 || U tenant || U domain || U aggregateType || U aggregateId || U OperationId)`. The record retains and compares that exact five-part tuple as well as `AppendPreparationIdentity`, so a hash collision fails closed. It creates `Preparing(tuple, identity, inputHash, ownerFence)` before generation. Equal command MessageIds in different scopes use different preparation keys; a changed original input in one scope conflicts. This namespace is separate from the global event-MessageId uniqueness index. Only its fenced owner may generate once and CAS an immutable `Prepared` capsule containing identity, original input hash, the selected writer mode/approved fingerprint, all generated IDs/timestamps/allocated positions, exact prepared raw/protected bytes and original protection record, original StoredDigest/decoder identity, encoding/digest sidecars, outbox intent, original bounded result bytes and full AppendOperationFingerprint. Preparation is **durable before** any MessageId reservation or actor save. An exact retry first reads and authenticates that capsule, compares the original input/identity and reuses every pinned value and byte; it never reruns a clock, ID allocator, protector, serializer or signer to compare against Committed. Different original input under one operation ID is `AppendPreparationConflict`. A missing, incomplete or ambiguous capsule is `AppendPreparationHold` before actor save until its CAS and no-save state are reconciled; a proven never-started preparation may be released under durable no-commit proof. Neither timeout nor caller cancellation guesses that proof. Generated global positions are allocated under the same fence and held by the capsule; retries neither skip nor reallocate them. The actor reads back capsule identity/fingerprint with the committed bundle.

Before generating any volatile append value or allocating a `Prepared` capsule, calculate a checked conservative aggregate **encoded** batch bound from the original input, declared count, per-provider maximum raw/protected output, sidecars, outbox intents, operation result, identifiers, signatures, field lengths and framing. A provider without a verified output bound cannot serve append readiness. The exact encoded capsule is then measured as it is streamed, still before reservation/actor save; any bound violation is `AppendPreparationLimit` with no partial capsule or regenerated values. One durable preparation capsule is at most **512 MiB** per operation, and all simultaneously live capsule plus request/response reservations share the existing **1 GiB** operation quota.
