Build and read the immutable capsule as authenticated encrypted bounded chunks, each with checked exact length and SHA-256 and a whole-capsule hash/root and ordinal/count; reserve bytes before each chunk, use at most **128 MiB live scratch**, and never materialize the full capsule in memory. Readback verifies every chunk, order, exact whole bytes/hash, preparation identity and owner fence before any MessageId reservation or actor save. A lost, altered or ambiguous chunk holds `AppendPreparationHold` and retry under the same operation ID without regenerating IDs, time, protected bytes or signatures. No partial capsule is durable append authority.

A `Prepared` capsule remains retained while its reservation/save is Pending, Ready or ambiguous, or any in-flight retry still needs its original volatile bytes. Only after Committed actor/root/result/full-bundle readback and durable closure of retry, rollback and publication obligations may a fenced CAS publish `HX-EV-APPEND-COMPLETION-1\0 || 01 || 000f`: `01` B32 preparation identity, `02` B32 original-input hash, `03` U exact OperationId, `04..07` U tenant/domain/aggregate type/aggregate ID, `08` B32 AppendOperationFingerprint, `09` B32 committed batch-root hash, `0a` B32 committed member-root hash, `0b` B32 actor intent-certificate hash, `0c` U original result key, `0d` B32 exact result hash, `0e` I event count and `0f` U authenticated retention-decision ID. The record and supporting actor/provider evidence are immutable and independently read back before capsule chunk reclamation. Reclaim encrypted chunks only with authenticated absence/readback; ambiguous deletion retains quota until reconciled. An exact late retry authenticates this compact record, original-input identity, committed root/member proofs, actor certificate, sidecars and result; it returns the pinned result without regenerating ID, time, signature or payload. Missing required evidence is `AppendPreparationHold`, never a fresh append. Pre-reserve capacity for both retained capsules and compact records under the operation quota; saturation returns `AppendPreparationHold` **before** a new save, with no unbounded or unsafe fallback.

One global strongly consistent `IEventMessageReservationStore` spans tenants, routes and instances. Key is `event-id-reservation:` plus lowercase SHA-256 of exact UTF-8 MessageId, with original MessageId stored to detect a hash collision; each per-ID index points to one authenticated batch root. Domain readiness proves the same backend identity, namespace and atomic ETag/CAS semantics across instances with persisted cross-instance probe. Prepare exact stored raw bytes/digest, reject duplicate MessageIds within the proposed actor batch, and reserve durable capacity for the complete index before save. A root first enters `Pending`; CAS-create every per-ID entry in deterministic order under that root's backend/fence and read back the exact ordered IDs, root fingerprint and each index pointer. **Actor save is forbidden until one CAS changes the root to `Ready` after that full readback.** A crash during partial Pending indexing resumes only by the same OperationId and exact durable Prepared capsule when actor-save absence is proven, or releases the whole root plus all entries under durable no-commit proof. Missing, ambiguous or conflicting capsule/index/save evidence holds the entire root; a different append cannot reuse any of its IDs. Only `Ready` may enter actor save/readback. After exact actor-owned bundle/result readback, one linearizable root CAS to `Committed` grants publication, subscriber and retry authority for every ID simultaneously. Per-ID status is always derived from the root, never committed independently. A failed reservation or proven no-save cleanup releases all entries under the same fence; timeout or cancellation alone is no-commit proof. Historical collision, capacity and retention rules below remain in force.

Batch reservation is one atomic decision after a recoverable indexing phase. Before per-ID index creation, CAS-create one durable root `HX-EV-APPEND-BATCH-1\0 || 02 || 000c` with ordered tags `01` U OperationId, `02..05` U tenant/domain/aggregate type/aggregate ID, `06` B32 AppendOperationFingerprint, `07` u32 ordered ID count followed by each U MessageId, `08` B32 proposed event/outbox/sidecar bundle hash (result-record tag `08`), `09` one-byte status (`00` Pending, `01` Ready, `02` Committed, `03` Released), `0a` O(B32) actual actor bundle readback hash, `0b` O(U) result-record key and `0c` O(B32) result-record hash. Root key is `append-batch:` plus the same scoped preparation-key digest; it retains and compares the exact five-part tuple from the preparation record and is CAS-fenced against a second root for that tuple. A distinct scope with the same OperationId has a distinct root key; every per-ID reference retains its own exact globally unique event MessageId. Each per-ID record contains exact MessageId, root key, root fingerprint, ordinal, scope and owner fence; readback must recover **exactly** the ordered root list, with no missing, extra or foreign entry, before Pending→Ready. A per-ID reference alone conveys no commit. Tags `0a..0c` are absent through Ready and populated only in the single Ready→Committed CAS after exact actor event/outbox/sidecar/result readback. `ActorBundleReadbackHash = SHA256("HX-EV-ACTOR-BUNDLE-1\0" || 01 || B(exact ordered event/outbox/encoding-and-stored-digest-sidecar bundle bytes) || B(exact operation-result side-record bytes) || U actor ETag || N actor logical version)` under §4 checked lengths. At actor commit or ambiguous-save reconciliation, recompute that full hash once and require the same committed root for every batch member. Afterward publication, subscriber and duplicate checks verify the addressed member through the bounded member-root inclusion proof and authenticated committed root, without rereading the full bundle on each delivery. A crash before commit reconciles all IDs together; Pending partial indexing with any actor-save evidence is ambiguous and holds rather than completing Ready. Released is a tombstone under durable no-commit evidence, retaining the operation/ID conflict record for its obligation. Root and references share one strongly consistent backend/fence; no separate per-ID Committed transitions or partial-batch visibility exist.

At actor commit, stage an immutable outbox **routing intent** and the bounded pre-rendered operation-result side record in the same actor-owned operation as the event, purpose-06 encoding sidecar and stored-digest sidecar. The exact bounded intent record is `HX-EV-OUTBOX-INTENT-1\0 || 01 || 0012` with ascending tags: `01` U event key, `02` U tenant, `03` U domain, `04` U aggregate type, `05` U aggregate ID, `06` U MessageId, `07` B32 StoredDigest, `08` U operation ID, `09` U exact publisher source URI, `0a` U pubsub component, `0b` U topic, `0c` M canonical routing-header name/value map, `0d` U routing policy version, `0e` B32 destination-configuration digest, `0f` O(U) CloudEvent subject, `10` O(T) CloudEvent time preserving original offset, `11` B nested record `HX-EV-OUTBOX-CORE-1\0 || 01 || U content type || U stored event type name || U source-derivation ID || B exact source/type derivation inputs`, and `12` B exact immutable configuration bytes or `HX-EV-OUTBOX-CONFIG-REF-1\0 || 01 || B32 content hash || U immutable address`. Tag `12` is exactly one `B` whose length includes its first payload byte: `00 || exact immutable configuration bytes` or `01 || complete HX-EV-OUTBOX-CONFIG-REF-1 record bytes`. There is no discriminator outside the B length and no second nested B around the variant. Hashing, pinning and readback use those exact tag-12 B bytes; resolve either form to exact configuration bytes whose SHA-256 equals tag `0e`. Absent and present optional subject/time values are distinct. The entire intent is at most 1 MiB encoded and is charged before allocation; an overlimit or unresolved configuration reference holds append before reservation. All configuration bytes and derived core attributes are fixed before actor save; the content-addressed reference and its exact resolved bytes remain retained for every pin/retry obligation. Save and read back exact intent, event, both sidecars **and operation-result record** bytes under the same operation/ETag before marking the global reservation Committed or allowing first publication. The publisher derives the first delivery pin solely from this readback intent and authenticated original event, never from mutable current routing configuration. A retry compares source URI, destination, header values/policy and configuration digest to the pin; changes are conflict. An old outbox without authenticated intended-route evidence holds for separately approved offline evidence migration; guessed destinations and current defaults are forbidden.

`AppendOperationFingerprint = SHA256("HX-EV-APPEND-OP-1\0" || 01 || B32 AppendPreparationIdentity || U operation ID || U tenant || U domain || U aggregate type || U aggregate ID || u32 ordered batch count || for each event in original batch order: U MessageId || U event key || N sequence || B exact prepared raw storage bytes || B32 StoredDigest || U selected historic provider encoding ID || B exact immutable outbox routing-intent bytes; then B32 SHA-256(exact pre-rendered actor result bytes))`. All lengths/counts use §4 checked bounds. The intent bytes include commit-selected CloudEvent subject/time presence and values, content type, exact type/source derivation inputs, source URI, component/topic, headers/policy and immutable destination configuration bytes/digest and are deterministically prepared before reservation. The fingerprint is computed once from the exact values atomically committed in the `Prepared` capsule and stored in that capsule before reservation; a retry compares original input with its preparation identity and then uses the pinned fingerprint. Volatile sidecar signature/issuance bytes are **not** recomputed for a retry. The global Committed **batch root** stores the fingerprint and result-record key/hash; the exact original actor result bytes live in the immutable actor-owned operation-result side record. The event, outbox, purpose-06 encoding sidecar, stored-digest sidecar and result record are read back under one actor fence and compared before the reservation becomes Committed. A repeated append with an already `Committed` MessageId may return the original actor result only after comparing this exact **entire proposed batch and operation** fingerprint against the committed record and reading back all corresponding immutable events/outboxes/sidecars **and original result record** under the actor fence. Same ID with a different batch, order, route or any evidence is `MessageIdConflict`; missing, partial or uncertain readback holds reconciliation without staging or returning a new result. This rule applies even if one event's scope and StoredDigest happen to match.
The pre-rendered domain/append result is encoded once by the selected bounded V1/V2 writer **before** MessageId reservation and actor save; its exact byte size obeys the mode's §8 total result cap. Stage an immutable side record at `aggregate-operation-result:` plus lowercase SHA-256 of `U tenant || U domain || U aggregate ID || U operation ID`, with `HX-EV-APPEND-RESULT-1\0 || 01 || 0008` and ordered tags: `01` U operation ID, `02` B32 full AppendOperationFingerprint, `03` U actor state key, `04` N predeclared actor logical version, `05` B exact original bounded result bytes, `06` B32 SHA-256 of tag-`05` bytes, `07` I event batch count, `08` B32 SHA-256 of the complete exact event/outbox/encoding-and-stored-digest-sidecar bundle staged in the same actor batch. After deriving all event sequences and original result bytes, and before global reservation, the actor predeclares its checked logical version, stages result, event, both sidecars and routing intent together, saves once, and reads back their exact bytes, operation/version and ETag under the actor fence. The result record is never first invented at the later global `Committed` transition. If save commits but acknowledgement is lost, `Ready` reconciliation finds this record and the full bundle, compares result hash/fingerprint/operation and readback, then completes the reservation and returns **the original recorded result bytes**. Missing, partial, mismatched or uncertain evidence holds Ready for bounded reconciliation with no restaging or newly computed result; proven no-commit releases it under the existing rule. A repeated Committed append follows the same full-batch/readback comparison and returns that recorded result, never a new serialization.

A zero-event `DomainResult.IsNoOp` follows the same original command MessageId/OperationId and immutable preparation identity but uses a separate **actor-owned no-event completion CAS**, not a zero-member event batch root. Pre-render the exact existing public no-op result shape and payload with the selected bounded writer before the CAS. The durable `HX-EV-NOOP-RESULT-1\0 || 01 || 0009` side record has ascending tags: `01` U OperationId, `02` B32 AppendPreparationIdentity, `03` B32 canonical original input hash, `04` U selected writer mode, `05` O(B32) selected registry fingerprint (absent for authenticated old V1), `06` B exact pre-rendered result bytes, `07` B32 SHA-256 of tag `06`, `08` U actor command-idempotency checkpoint key and `09` U checked actor logical version. The actor commits this record atomically with a no-op completion witness at that checkpoint under the same operation fence, then reads back exact identity, input hash, mode/fingerprint, result bytes/hash and version before returning. No per-event MessageId index/root, event stream append, outbox intent or publication is created. An exact retry resolves this side record and returns its pinned bytes without re-running domain work or serialization; changed original input under the same command MessageId is `AppendPreparationConflict`. An ambiguous no-op save is `AppendPreparationHold` until actor readback proves committed or never-started; caller cancellation follows the same actor commit witness as an event append and never invents a new result. The no-op record and checkpoint survive for the command idempotency/retry obligation.

A no-op `Prepared` capsule obeys the same retention and quota rule as an event batch, but its compact completion is **not** a zero-member batch-root claim. After authenticated readback of the committed no-op result/checkpoint witness, same-save actor intent certificate, post-save provider commit receipt and closure of retry/rollback obligations, CAS-publish `HX-EV-NOOP-COMPLETION-1\0 || 01 || 000f` with ordered §4 tags: `01` B32 preparation identity, `02` B32 original-input hash, `03` U OperationId, `04..07` U tenant/domain/aggregate type/aggregate ID, `08` U selected writer mode, `09` O(B32) selected RegistryFingerprint, `0a` U no-op result storage key, `0b` B32 exact no-op result-record hash, `0c` B32 authenticated no-op checkpoint-witness hash, `0d` B32 actor intent-certificate hash, `0e` B32 purpose-11 provider commit-receipt hash and `0f` U authenticated retention-decision ID. The complete compact no-op record is capped at 4 KiB before allocation. It has no event count, StoredDigest, member root, batch root or fabricated event ID. Read back the compact record and all named witnesses before deleting encrypted capsule chunks; ambiguous deletion retains quota. An exact late retry compares original input and this compact record, authenticates the still-retained no-op result/checkpoint/intent/receipt evidence, and returns the pinned original no-op result without generating IDs, clocks, signatures or payload again. Missing required evidence yields `AppendPreparationHold` and never reexecutes the no-op CAS.

Before each append, the store reserves durable capacity for the preparation capsule, batch root, all per-ID references and reconciliation headroom under its configured scalable quota; inability to guarantee that capacity returns `ReservationCapacityHold` before save. A capacity failure never falls back to tenant-local uniqueness. Pending entries have bounded reconciliation, but Committed entries are not evicted merely because the store is full.

Per-route marker key includes scoped MessageId, canonical type and stable HandlerRouteId, while immutable duplicate identity compares scope, sequence, StoredDigest and DeliveryDigest; RegistryFingerprint is audit/readiness metadata, not duplicate identity. Same MessageId may reach several cataloged routes only for identical event evidence. A changed body, destination, header or reused ID is conflict, never duplicate. Migrate legacy Completed/Dispatched marker under CAS only with independent scoped actor evidence **and** route-specific receipt/outbox/catalog proof; Completed remains terminal, Dispatched only finalizes. Old InProgress may retry only under proven effect idempotency. Unique-ID inventory alone is insufficient. Ambiguity holds for reconciliation.

There is exactly **one durable global publication pin keyed by the exact MessageId**, CAS-created only after comparing scope, StoredDigest, DeliveryDigest and all pinned decoded body/attestation/core/header bytes and the exact outbound bytes for the same transport mode. A changed digest cannot create a second pin even if a secondary index includes that digest. The separate durable historic delivery-pin obligation is keyed by scoped MessageId and DeliveryDigest and stores purpose 02, scope, StoredDigest, exact body and extension bytes, signature/key ID, signed issuance UTC from claim tag `0a` and approved fingerprint, source head, pending queue/quarantine/rollback needs and drain status. Recovery may derive the first pin from durable original event/readability only when actor commit/outbox witness proves no prior send; otherwise it holds for operator reconciliation. The exact global MessageId publication pin and its body/attestation bytes remain durable until every queue, broker retry, quarantine replay, rollback target and old-key verification obligation for that event has closed with durable evidence; a reclamation worker may then remove bulky pin bytes under CAS only after installing and reading back an immutable authenticated **global-MessageId pin tombstone**. That tombstone retains exact SHA-256 hashes and lengths of the pinned canonical body, attestation, signed core/header set and each permitted transport-mode byte rendering, original signature/key ID/trust interval, scope, StoredDigest, DeliveryDigest and all completed route receipts. It retains the necessary verification key and actor-source evidence for at least the enforceable maximum broker redelivery horizon plus every queued, rollback and replay obligation. If no enforceable finite redelivery horizon exists, full pin bytes remain or reclamation readiness fails. A late delivery is authenticated against tombstone hashes/lengths and original actor source before route-receipt deduplication; changed bytes conflict, and missing trust/evidence holds without a new send, effect or false acknowledgement. The global Committed ID index and immutable event digest remain for lifetime uniqueness. Pin storage reserves quota before first send, otherwise returns `PublicationPinCapacityHold` and keeps the committed outbox pending. A retired key may verify only its exact still-open pin obligation after proving signature validity in the key's original interval and exact byte/scope equality. On a queued approved pin after registry rotation, first validate the historic purpose-02 signature, prior RegistryFingerprint, signed issuance time/key interval and **exact durable pin obligation**; do not compare that prior fingerprint to the active route fingerprint. Independently authenticate the immutable actor source under current trust, compute a fresh purpose-01 route and verified effective view under the **current** key/fingerprint, compare StoredDigest, MessageId, scope and sequence across old pin, actor source and new route, and compare the received exact delivery body/attestation/transport bytes to the old pin before handler selection. The current view retains the authenticated source digest and original delivery digest as provenance; a newly computed effective payload need not equal the old pinned readable payload. Missing old obligation, unavailable source or missing current route holds without effect or acknowledgement. Historic purpose-02 acceptance never authorizes old effective bytes to bypass current evolution. Revocation or compromise follows an explicit incident decision; age alone never grants generic old-key acceptance or drops an open pin. Before activating a new signer, every affected verifier advertises the new trust map/fingerprint and old obligations remain readable.

The existing public `EventStoreDomainEventContext` remains an **unsealed** positional record with its exact six-argument constructor `(TenantId, AggregateId, MessageId, SequenceNumber, Timestamp, CorrelationId)`, generated six-field deconstruction, nullable `long? GlobalPosition`, and optional init properties `Domain`, `CausationId`, `UserId`. No member is redeclared, sealed, made required, or changed in type or accessibility; old source and compiled callers retain their API. Add a separate immutable `VerifiedEventStoreDomainEventContext` wrapper for the two new effect callables. Its public `LegacyContext: EventStoreDomainEventContext` is a detached copy with those original fields, and its verified-only members are addressed `AggregateType`, non-null verified global position, `DeliveryDigest`, `HandlerRouteId`, stored metadata/type/version/format/digest, effective type/version/format/payload digest, current `RegistryFingerprint`, and exact purpose-01/02 claim/key/signature values. Binary members are immutable copies; public mutable transport arrays never back signed authority. The router constructs the wrapper only after source, consumed-metadata, current-view and both signed claims validate, compares every wrapper and legacy member to those claims and actor readback, and binds scope, MessageId and DeliveryDigest to `EventEffectKey`. Production admission requires a verified global position when the signed claim requires one; this does not narrow the old nullable member. A mismatch is `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` before effect or marker. Both new callable interfaces receive the verified wrapper; an old-handler adapter receives its `LegacyContext` and preserves its six-argument metadata semantics.

The additive effect contract uses immutable `EventEffectKey(tenant, domain, aggregateType, aggregateId, MessageId, DeliveryDigest, HandlerRouteId)` with that exact seven-part order and a provider-backed durable `EventEffectReceipt` containing the key, provider operation ID, committed effect result digest, commit version/ETag and provider identity. Add independent `ITransactionalEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>.HandleAndCommitAsync(TEvent eventPayload, VerifiedEventStoreDomainEventContext context, EventEffectKey key, IEventEffectTransaction transaction, CancellationToken cancellationToken): ValueTask<EventEffectReceipt>` and `IEventEffectReceiptReader.GetReceiptAsync(EventEffectKey key, CancellationToken cancellationToken): ValueTask<EventEffectReceipt?>`. The router builds `VerifiedEventStoreDomainEventContext` only after source, route and delivery proof validation and compares every context field to the signed consumed metadata and authenticated actor source. It first queries the receipt, then obtains a provider transaction bound to the exact key under a unique constraint/linearizable CAS, invokes the new handler **only within that context**, and accepts success only after effect and receipt commit in one serializable transaction and authenticated readback. `IEventEffectTransaction` exposes a provider-enlisted `IEventEffectWriteSession EffectWrites` and `CommitEffectAndReceiptAsync(EventEffectReceipt proposedReceipt, CancellationToken cancellationToken): ValueTask<EventEffectReceipt>`; that call atomically commits every enlisted effect mutation and the unique-key receipt, and authenticated readback must match the returned receipt. A second commit or a write after commit fails. Any handler path that can perform an effect outside this session cannot claim `AtomicStore`. A crash before commit yields neither effect nor receipt; a crash after commit yields both; an ambiguous commit is resolved by receipt/effect readback before another invocation. `ProviderIdempotency` uses `IProviderIdempotentEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>.HandleIdempotentlyAsync(TEvent eventPayload, VerifiedEventStoreDomainEventContext context, EventEffectKey key, IEventIdempotentEffectProvider provider, CancellationToken cancellationToken): ValueTask<EventEffectReceipt>` and the same read-only receipt lookup. The provider enforces the exact key on its effect call and returns/queries the same result and receipt across crash/retry; an adapter cannot emulate this by writing a receipt after an arbitrary side effect. The router selects the cataloged transactional or provider-idempotent implementation ahead of the unchanged `IEventStoreDomainEventHandler<TEvent>` `Task` API, with duplicate route registration rejected. Consumer lease mode `DurableDedup` denotes the proved `AtomicStore` transaction-and-receipt contract; `ProviderIdempotency` denotes the external exact-key contract. Existing handlers stay source-compatible and production-ready only through a verified adapter satisfying one of those callable contracts. Production route activation requires the §7 version-3 crash/two-route probe and authenticated durable receipt for every route; a configured provider name or a later marker write is insufficient. `HandlerCapabilityHold` occurs before handler invocation, acknowledgement or completed marker when a route lacks a proven contract. Production activation freezes and audits the transitive DI/service graph and implementation dependencies of each effect-capable route. `AtomicStore` may reach effect sinks only through its `IEventEffectWriteSession`; `ProviderIdempotency` may reach them only through the exact-key provider. A service locator, dynamically resolved or injected alternate effect sink, or effect write outside those channels fails registration/readiness. Per-route crash and concurrency probes plus code/configuration review verify this restriction; a fixture alone cannot prove arbitrary handler code pure. A crash after effect but before `Dispatched` queries the receipt and finalizes the same key without rerunning effect. Valid addressed event with no current-type handler is `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` and retries; `PredeclaredRouteFilter` may acknowledge only authenticated out-of-scope delivery under its frozen predicate. AD-31 quarantine is evidence capture, never permission to advance a checkpoint past bad history.

`EventEffectReceipt` is a bounded canonical provider record, not a free DTO. Its exact bytes are `HX-EV-EFFECT-RECEIPT-1\0 || 01 || 000e` with ordered §4 fields: `01` U tenant, `02` U domain, `03` U aggregate type, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` U exact MessageId, `06` B32 DeliveryDigest, `07` U HandlerRouteId, `08` U provider operation ID, `09` B32 committed effect-result digest, `0a` U provider identity, `0b` B exact canonical backend identity, `0c` U mode (`AtomicStore` or `ProviderIdempotency`), `0d` N actual commit version and `0e` U actual commit ETag. The seven fields `01..07` are exactly the production `EventEffectKey`; each U is strict UTF-8 under §4 and nonempty, the complete record is capped at 4 KiB before allocation, and unknown/duplicate/out-of-order fields or inconsistent provider version/ETag fail readiness. `ReceiptDigest = SHA256(exact complete EventEffectReceipt record bytes)` with no DTO reserialization. The provider signs or authenticates those exact committed bytes under its configured backend trust/fence and returns the same bytes on receipt readback and duplicate query. V3 probe transcript tags `13`/`14`, broker/provider readback and route dedup compare precisely this `ReceiptDigest` and the complete record against the effect result; a changed mode, key, backend, commit version or ETag is conflict, not an already completed effect. Existing V17/V20/V22/V23 signed fixture literals remain historical and unchanged.

The 6.6 binary cutover includes `Operations/Capture/DeadLetterEnvelopeParser` and `Operations/Endpoints/DeadLetterOperationsEndpointExtensions`. The parser recognizes the exact §7 authenticated Binary CloudEvent body **and headers** and structured `data_base64` mapping, in addition to legacy structured JSON `data`. Before assigning replay-safe scope or MessageId it verifies the attestation, pinned body/core/header identity and transport-to-canonical decoding; it retains exact transport bytes, headers and hashes. Malformed or unauthenticated bytes may receive a support-safe unidentified hash for evidence capture but can never be replayed as an identified event. The capture endpoint streams a bounded admitted body up to the §8 128 MiB raw-carrier cap into tenant-scoped authenticated encrypted durable blob storage, recording exact bytes/hash/headers in an actor CAS index with readback; a broker/publisher durable reference is equivalent only when it proves the identical pinned bytes and finite retention obligation. HTTP 200/ack occurs **only after** exact durable capture and readback. The former 1 MiB default cannot acknowledge an uncaptured valid binary delivery. Capacity, provider or readback failure is retryable non-2xx and holds binary readiness unless a broker dead-letter path independently proves retention beyond its finite retry budget; finite retries alone are insufficient. Above-cap bytes are classified with safe hash evidence and never claimed captured, while publication/subscription bounds prevent treating them as valid deliveries. AD-31 durable capture still precedes poison acknowledgement; no-handler valid addressed events retain their §9 retry/hold rule.

Binary ingress resolves the **complete addressed logical HandlerRouteId set** from the authenticated pinned physical-subscription membership revision and the verified current event route set, even though the broker delivers one body. Before any physical acknowledgement, CAS-stage/read back one immutable `HX-EV-BINARY-HANDOFF-1\0` delivery/route-set manifest keyed by the exact global MessageId, physical subscription ID, membership revision and DeliveryDigest; it binds the sorted unique route IDs, canonical pin/body/attestation hashes, source scope and one stable delivery OperationId. Each addressed route has its own durable effect key and Completed/Filtered receipt under that operation. The handoff's committed pointer is the sole authority that all required route entries exist; partial/ambiguous staging is reconciled by OperationId and leaves the physical message unacknowledged. Invoke only routes lacking an authenticated terminal receipt, at most once per exact effect key. A predeclared authenticated filter may terminalize only its proven out-of-scope route. Acknowledge the physical delivery only when **every** addressed route has durable Completed or permitted Filtered status and the complete manifest/receipts read back. Any missing handler, retryable, failed, ambiguous, changed route set or changed bytes leaves the physical message unacknowledged, resumes only incomplete routes and never converts another route's receipt into global completion. An empty addressed set is not success for a valid addressed event unless the signed predeclared physical route filter proves it out of scope. This binary rule is independent of, and consistent with, the batch-wide legacy JSON handoff above.

The immutable actor-save result in §7 is the **domain/append result**, not the later public command/publication outcome. Pre-render and stage that exact bounded domain result (including rejection) with the original event/outbox/sidecar bundle; the zero-event no-op uses its separate actor-owned no-event result record. Actor commit and an exact append retry return only the pinned domain result bytes at the append layer. After commit, the command endpoint derives its final public status from durable outbox/publication receipt, pins `HX-EV-COMMAND-OUTCOME-1\0 || 01` under the original scoped command OperationId with the append-result hash, committed batch/no-op witness, publication state/receipt hash, exact public response bytes/hash and endpoint outcome revision, then reads it back before replying. `PublishFailed` or pending publication after save may affect that public status but **cannot overwrite** the committed append result or alter its original fingerprint. An exact command retry first reads append and command-outcome records: if publication is proven complete it returns the exact pinned published outcome; if pending/failed it returns the exact pinned truthful pending/failure outcome or reconciles against a later durable publication receipt under a monotonic outcome revision before issuing a new pinned response. It never reports published from append commitment alone, re-renders a different result, restages events or emits a second no-op/publication. A lost public-response acknowledgement is resolved by outcome readback; cancellation before actor save requires proven no commit, while cancellation after save preserves append truth and leaves publication/outcome reconciliation on a bounded recovery token. A zero-event no-op has no publication receipt and pins the existing no-op public shape from its actor completion witness without an outbox send.

At actor commit/reconciliation, authenticate and hash the **entire** prepared event/outbox/encoding/stored-digest/result bundle once under the actor fence. Add a versioned committed per-member Merkle commitment as an actor-owned sidecar within the exact bundle whose hash is batch-root tag `08` and whose readback hash is the actor-save certificate. The separately defined AppendOperationFingerprint remains the original complete-batch input comparison and does not change its historical codec. For ordered batch index `i` of count `n`, define `MemberLeaf_i = SHA256("HX-EV-BATCH-MEMBER-1\0" || 01 || I i || I n || U MessageId || U exact event key || B32 raw event hash || B32 StoredDigest || B32 outbox-intent hash || B32 encoding-sidecar hash || B32 stored-digest-sidecar hash || B32 domain/append-result hash)`. Build a left-to-right binary tree with `Parent = SHA256("HX-EV-BATCH-NODE-1\0" || 01 || B32 left || B32 right)`; duplicate the last hash at an odd level, record `n`, leaf order and final B32 root in the authenticated member-root sidecar bound through batch-root tag `08` and `ActorBundleReadbackHash`. The actor's one full-bundle readback verifies every leaf before the global root becomes Committed. A per-member proof contains version `01`, checked `i`, `n`, exact leaf fields, and one ordered sibling B32 per level with left/right derived from index; its depth is exactly `ceil(log2 n)` (zero for one member), at most 10 for the admitted 1,000-event V1 batch, and its entire encoded carrier is at most 16 KiB before allocation. Publication, subscriber and duplicate checks authenticate the committed batch root/certificate and verify only the addressed immutable member's raw event, outbox, sidecars and result binding with that proof; they do not reread every other member on each delivery. A missing member, changed sidecar/result, proof count/index mismatch or uncommitted root holds. An exact append retry still compares the full original batch fingerprint and actor readback, never merely one member. This additive production commitment leaves historical V17/V20/V22/V23 signed fixture literals untouched.

Before parsing, allocation or signature verification, cap the complete signed MembershipClaim carrier (claim, member set, key ID and signature) at **1 MiB encoded**, with at most **4,096** sorted unique logical members and at most **4 KiB encoded per member**. The external route-capability route set is at most **256 KiB** and **1,024** unique type/version/format rows; the complete external manifest plus claim/signature/carrier is at most **2 MiB**, while its dependency closure retains the existing **1 MiB** cap. Length and count prefixes are checked against these caps before copying any nested B value; sorting scratch and duplicate detection are charged. Overflow is typed `ConsumerMembershipFenceUnavailable` or `ExternalCapabilityLimit` and prevents publish/ack, not a truncated set or an inferred handler. A smaller signed closure cannot authorize an overlarge external route set.

The binary handoff manifest's exact bytes are `HX-EV-BINARY-HANDOFF-1\0 || 01 || 0011`, then ascending tags: `01` U MessageId, `02` U tenant, `03` U domain, `04` U aggregate type, `05` U aggregate ID, `06` U component, `07` U topic, `08` U physical subscription ID, `09` N membership revision, `0a` B32 StoredDigest, `0b` B32 DeliveryDigest, `0c` B32 pinned body hash, `0d` B32 pinned attestation hash, `0e` B32 route-set hash, `0f` B route set, `10` U stable delivery OperationId, `11` B32 pinned core/header/transport-rule hash. The route set is `u32 count || U HandlerRouteId` repeated in unsigned UTF-8 sorted unique order; count is at most 4,096 and the complete manifest is at most 1 MiB before allocation. Tag `0e` equals SHA-256 of exactly tag `0f`'s decoded route-set bytes. The durable handoff key is `binary-handoff:` plus lowercase SHA-256 of `"HX-EV-BINARY-HANDOFF-KEY-1\0" || 01 || U component || U topic || U physical subscription ID || U MessageId`; revision, route set and digests are compared as **values** at that one key so a changed delivery cannot create a parallel completed handoff. A route receipt key adds `U HandlerRouteId` under that handoff and retains the complete seven-part `EventEffectKey`; readback verifies every route in tag `0f` before acknowledging one physical message. This manifest is a new production record and does not alter any historical signed fixture.

The public command-outcome record is `HX-EV-COMMAND-OUTCOME-1\0 || 01 || 000c` with ordered tags `01` U original scoped Command OperationId, `02` U tenant, `03` U domain, `04` U aggregate ID, `05` B32 immutable domain/append-result hash, `06` B32 committed batch-root or no-op witness hash, `07` U publication state (`pending`, `published` or `failed`), `08` O(B32) durable broker/publication receipt hash, `09` B exact encoded public response bytes, `0a` N monotonic outcome revision, `0b` B32 SHA-256 of tag `09` bytes and `0c` N actor logical version. Its key is `command-outcome:` plus lowercase SHA-256 of separately encoded tenant/domain/aggregate ID/OperationId. Tags `0b` and `0c` are authenticated under the same operation fence and compared to tag `09` and actor readback; a mismatch is no outcome. Revision zero is created after actor commit/no-op readback; a later `published` revision is CAS-linked only to an authenticated publication receipt. Earlier immutable versions remain for exact lost-response reconciliation. One revision cannot change its bytes, receipt hash or public status, and a `failed` status never changes the actor domain/append result. The outcome record is bounded by the selected §8 response cap before allocation.

The committed per-member proof carrier is `HX-EV-BATCH-MEMBER-PROOF-1\0 || 01 || I index || I memberCount || B(exact ordered MemberLeaf fields from §7) || I depth || depth × B32 sibling`, with no trailing bytes. `index` is zero-based, `memberCount` is 1..1,000, `depth` is the checked `ceil(log2 memberCount)`, siblings are ordered leaf-to-root, and left/right position is derived from each index bit; an odd duplicated right child must equal the current left hash. Compare the reconstructed root to the committed member-root sidecar B32 authenticated by batch-root tag `08` and the actor readback certificate and compare every supplied member field to exact actor-owned readback before effect/publication. A proof over 16 KiB or with a count/depth/index mismatch fails before allocation or hashing. This is a proof of one committed member, never a replacement for full-batch comparison on append retry.

The member-root sidecar is `HX-EV-BATCH-MEMBER-ROOT-1\0 || 01 || 0006` with ordered tags `01` U scoped append OperationId, `02` U actor state key, `03` I member count, `04` B32 final Merkle root, `05` B32 SHA-256 of exact pre-rendered domain/append result bytes, and `06` B32 original AppendOperationFingerprint. Stage it in the same actor save as events, outbox intents, encoding/stored-digest sidecars and operation-result record. The complete ordered bundle hash in `HX-EV-APPEND-BATCH-1` tag `08` and result-record tag `08` includes this exact sidecar after the event members, before final actor readback. Its bytes are precomputed from prepared values and never include the bundle hash or actor ETag, so no hash is circular. Actor commit/reconciliation checks tag `03` against root tag `07`, recomputes every leaf/root once from the full bundle, compares tag `04`, verifies tags `05`/`06` against result/fingerprint and binds the sidecar in `ActorBundleReadbackHash`; only then may the root become Committed. Thereafter a publisher or consumer needs the authenticated committed root, sidecar and addressed member proof, not a repeated full-batch scan. The original batch-root codec, V17 historical fixture and full-batch append-retry comparison remain unchanged.

## 8. Numeric budgets and deterministic outcomes

All sizes are byte counts with checked arithmetic, charged **before** serializer, protection, allocator, staging or response materialization. For projection admission, one shared 128 MiB pipeline-scratch charge covers the **sum of simultaneously live** stored DTO/event bytes, verified effective payload and view buffers, duplicated route-claim bytes, `EventEvolutionProof` container, deserialization state and scratch owners. Retain no uncharged alias/copy; release prior owners or select smaller pages/streaming views before allocation. The separate 64 MiB readable-page and 64 MiB proof maxima are individual ceilings, not additive permission above 128 MiB. An oversized combination returns typed `ScratchLimit` before Apply/handler and leaves state/checkpoint unchanged. Provider raw reads must offer streaming/size-limited bytes or enforced transport maximum; bounded unprotect **and bounded protect output** need streaming APIs or provider-enforced preallocation bounds. The old whole-array protection API cannot establish these ceilings. Negative head or `currentSequence > long.MaxValue - eventCount`, and independent global-position overflow, return `SequenceExhausted` before staging.

| Boundary | Hard rule and typed result |
| --- | --- |
| New V2 event/hop | At most 1 MiB serialized readable payload and each hop output (`PayloadLimit`); V2 result at most 256 events and 16 MiB total wire JSON/base64/metadata/ResultPayload (`ResultLimit`). |
| V1 producer/read | At most 1,000 events per result, 64 MiB per readable event, 128 MiB full serialized result and 128 MiB raw protected envelope; bounded writer precedes full allocation. A zero-hop, validated current-version V1 source may be larger than 1 MiB but within measured legacy ceiling. |
| Source/page/chain | Raw envelope at most 128 MiB in its separately charged immutable source budget; copied validation/canonicalization/deserialization/hop buffers use a distinct at-most-128 MiB pipeline scratch budget. Decode in bounded windows or non-authoritative spool without a second full raw in-memory copy; release stage buffers before the next hop. Readable page at most 256 events/64 MiB; page proof at most 64 MiB, command proof at most 2 MiB, route claim at most 1 MiB. Typed `RawEnvelopeLimit`, `ReadableLimit`, `ScratchLimit`, `ProofLimit`. |
| Legacy complete array | At most 100,000 events, 64 MiB cumulative readable payload and **256 MiB conservative accounting**, whichever first; charge stored payload buffer, separately allocated readable/effective buffer(s), encoded metadata/extensions and 8192 bytes/event before each addition. If buffers alias, charge actual distinct capacity once with documented ownership. At most 128 MiB active scratch apart from that array. `LegacyArrayLimit` returns no partial array. |
| Append preparation | Checked full encoded batch estimate before volatile generation; 512 MiB maximum durable capsule, 1 GiB combined live capsule/request/response reservations, 128 MiB live chunk scratch. `AppendPreparationLimit` before reservation/save, with no partial capsule. |
| Command continuation | Ledger index at most 65,536 entries/64 MiB; exact signed-request and retry-response blobs at most 128 MiB each and **1 GiB combined**, CAS/readback/retention at least 24 hours; owner lease/endpoint affinity is required while scratch is live. Reserve conservative blob quota before Apply; serialize after private computation, and enforce the 128 MiB cap on **actual encoded Web JSON including state, optional timeline, proof, Base64 and escaping**. `FinalResponseLimit` leaves no authoritative final completion; when only timeline causes excess, start a fresh authenticated page-1 state-only replay with `includeTimeline=false`. An ambiguous blob/final-result/ledger CAS or readback retains quota and operation ID until all three reconcile; `ReplayCommitAmbiguous` never permits a second Apply. Missing blob returns `ReplayRestartRequired`; missing scratch only invalidates incomplete pages. Exact final-pin retry within its 24-hour obligation precedes 15-minute token-expiry rejection when current proof key/fingerprint and complete prefix still verify. |
| Timeline/checkpoint | Timeline at most 1,000 entries/64 MiB cumulative serialized state (`TimelineLimit`); checkpoint state/readback at most 64 MiB (`CheckpointStateLimit`), streamed with at most 1 MiB hash buffer plus canonical metadata. Snapshot uses separately approved Story 6.1 bound and streamed witness. |
| Named read-model query | At most 1,000,000 members/root, 256 requested keys, 8 MiB encoded authenticated index proof, 64 MiB result and 128 MiB combined live scratch. Charge before allocation and return `ReadModelQueryLimit` with no partial result. |
| Named index construction | Each encoded logical key at most 512 UTF-8 bytes, leaf/progress entry at most 4 KiB, each complete retained row or progress index at most 1 GiB, at most 1,000,000 rows/sources, at most 4,096 changed logical keys per source event and 128 MiB live construction scratch. Verify and copy only changed sparse-tree paths, with 256 nodes per path and charged scratch; `NamedIndexLimit` blocks publication. |
| Named maintenance/archive | At most one active job per finite leaf; each job at most 1 KiB, 1,000,000 active jobs and 1 GiB active scheduler index per route/key-space. Shared ordinal log at most 1 GiB hot, sealed archive segments at most 64 MiB, retained hot plus archive at most 64 GiB. Capacity hold precedes root/ordinal publication. |
| Broker and external carrier | Membership carrier at most 1 MiB, 4,096 members and 4 KiB per member; external route set at most 256 KiB/1,024 rows; complete external carrier at most 2 MiB with dependency closure at most 1 MiB. Reject counted lengths before nested allocation. |
| Query authority and member proof | Per tenant at most 100,000 live query capabilities and 128 MiB encoded index, 1 KiB per record, five-minute lifetime; separately at most 100,000 active exact-key grant blobs and 1 GiB encoded blob/index overhead, each blob at most 256 KiB. Reserve both quotas before issuance; `ReadModelAccessUnavailable` on either cap. Per-member committed-batch inclusion proof at most 16 KiB and 10 sibling hashes for the admitted 1,000-event batch; a changed proof holds before publication/effect. |
| Authorized backup export | Existing configured count ceiling, fenced to validated page/legacy budgets, plus 64 MiB hard cap on total encoded JSON/CloudEvents downloadable bytes including escaping, metadata and framing. Bounded streaming or a provider-enforced preallocation bound; `ExportLimit` returns no partial artifact. |
| Registry/catalog | Each encoded row at most 64 KiB; each domain manifest at most 65,536 rows and 64 MiB total encoded bytes including G closure, options and charged sort workspace. `RegistryLimit` blocks readiness before fingerprint publication. |
| Metadata/transport | 512 KiB encoded per-event metadata and §7 extension sublimits, raw subscriber body 128 MiB and attestation 8 KiB, checked before allocation. |

The 100,000 small-event/maximum-metadata example **must** produce `LegacyArrayLimit` before exceeding 256 MiB: 8192 bytes/event alone is 819,200,000 bytes. A success vector uses a count below all three array bounds. A 64 MiB V1 source may fit the 128 MiB whole-result cap after Base64 only if its metadata fits; writer preflights it. A source exceeding permanent previously agreed schema/metadata ceiling, after complete authenticated immutable source verification, is deterministic poison and requires durable AD-31 capture **before** subscription ack. Valid historical source exceeding a newly deployed proof/provider capability is hold/retry and blocks readiness, never ack. No discretionary alternative. Deterministic schema/identity rejection is poison; unknown key, capability mismatch, unavailable protection/provider, unexpected upcaster exception or OOM is hold/retry. Explicit deterministic upcaster-input rejection has a typed `EventSchemaRejected`; cancellation is neither poison nor retryable failure classification.

## 9. Consumer and cancellation matrix

| Boundary / condition | Required result and durable progress |
| --- | --- |
| No-op command exact retry or ambiguous save | Resolve by the original command MessageId/OperationId and actor-owned no-event result/checkpoint readback. Exact retry returns the pinned original result bytes; changed input conflicts; ambiguous save holds without domain re-execution or event/outbox creation. |
| Append preparation identity conflict or missing capsule | `AppendPreparationConflict` rejects changed original input under one operation ID. Missing/ambiguous capsule is `AppendPreparationHold` before actor save; exact retry uses its original volatile values and bytes, never generates replacements. |
| No-op capsule retirement or late retry | After no-op result/checkpoint, intent and provider receipt readback and obligation closure, CAS-publish the dedicated compact no-op completion, then reclaim encrypted capsule chunks with authenticated deletion. Ambiguous deletion retains quota. Late retry proves the compact record and returns pinned result without new no-op execution; missing proof is `AppendPreparationHold`. |
| Append capsule or named index limit | `AppendPreparationLimit` rejects an overbound encoded batch before volatile generation or actor save; lost capsule chunks hold without regeneration. `NamedIndexLimit` rejects an overbound row/progress candidate or more than 4,096 changed keys before root publication and preserves last-good state; impossible safe pruning holds readiness. |
| Append malformed pair, unknown alias, bad identity, failed per-output F validation, sequence overflow or limit | Typed rejection before actor staging/reservation; no event mutation. A V1 alias `NoPayloadIdentity` skips only payload-ID extraction, never addressed envelope checks. |
| Raw read corrupt/duplicate names, unknown format, failed protection, missing sequence | Stop at offending sequence with source bytes/protection reason retained. No Apply, checkpoint, `Current` or success response. If parsing prevents StoredDigest, authenticate exact raw actor/provider evidence with addressed key, immutable head/sequence and raw-byte SHA-256; a complete permanent corrupt source is durably AD-31 captured with hash/scope/sequence/reason before ack. Incomplete or untrusted evidence holds/retries without ack; never fabricate StoredDigest. |
| Retained raw-corrupt cutover disposition | An immutable operator-approved `RawCorruptDisposition` may close inventory only for its exact actor/provider-proved key, sequence and raw-byte hash after durable AD-31 evidence. Replay still stops there with typed corruption and last-good prefix; no synthetic digest/origin/route, Apply, checkpoint advance or delivery ack follows from the disposition. Missing decision/evidence holds reader cutover. |
| Raw-source API or provider proof unavailable | `RawSourceUnavailable` holds reader activation or the addressed read before typed materialization; no typed `EventEnvelope` fallback may claim immutable provider bytes, original encoding or a trusted head. |
| Upcast unknown/gapped/ambiguous edge, mismatched result, invalid hop schema/identity, output limit | Startup/corpus mismatch blocks readiness; authenticated deterministic event failure stops prefix. No next hop or partial authoritative state. Unexpected hop failure retries. Input digest mutation or attempted source-owner exposure is `UpcasterContractViolation`, holds the route before next hop/Apply and blocks readiness until code/dependency repair; it is never acknowledged as deterministic source poison. |
| Paged public seam or proof mismatch | External async replay receives public immutable `PagedContext`, opaque authenticated byte[] token and in-process `IPagedReplayStateSession`, then returns public immutable `PagedProgress` naming its CAS-written successor. Gateway validates owner/generation/range/proof and state readback before committing once. Old adapters reject the context and never publish partial state. |
| Incomplete paged result | `InProgress` exposes null final state, final fields and timeline, `ErrorCategory=None`, only the verified page-end sequence and pinned `PagedProgress`; `/replay-state/pages` returns one next token with `isComplete=false`. Old `/replay-state` rejects it. Only complete-prefix proof permits final `Succeeded`. |
| Authorized backup export source mismatch or limit | Stop the bounded export before constructing a downloadable result; report a typed source/identity/protection reason or `ExportLimit` when count/64 MiB encoded bytes exceed the cap under the existing authorization policy. Valid V1 output labels the stored alias and source version, while V2 public identity is canonical with separate stored provenance. No partial content escapes as an ordinary Admin/trace response. |
| Backup export staging or final pointer uncertain | No response bytes, URL or partial success leave the private encrypted stage. Read back the fixed-head staged content/hash and atomically committed completion pointer; changed or missing proof holds for reconciliation, cancellation discards or quarantines uncommitted stage. |
| Oversized proof page | Projection dispatch and ordinary stream, Admin, trace, backup and other authenticated readers premeasure and reduce/re-sign a contiguous page against the fixed source head until both the 64 MiB proof and 128 MiB combined scratch caps fit. One event still over limit returns `ProofLimit` and holds without checkpoint advance, skipped event, partial export or unchanged-page retry. Command pages use the stricter 2 MiB outer cap. |
| Replay/command cancelled or proof/head mismatch | Discard private scratch/timeline/array; preserve last authoritative state. A page is never a complete prefix. Async replay propagates token; bounded sync adapter's limitation is explicit. |
| Live/rebuild projection deterministic bad event | Capture AD-31 where available; stop at event, preserve last contiguous completed checkpoint and mark state non-current. Repair/migration then retries from last good point. Missing sink cannot turn it into success. |
| Subscription bad authenticated permanent source | Durable AD-31 capture before ack. Addressed no-handler, new capability/proof limit, unavailable key/provider or untrusted ingress hold/retry without ack. Filter ack only for authenticated out-of-scope predicate. |
| Duplicate delivery | Verify global committed ID, canonical decoded body/attestation/core/header pin and same-mode exact outbound bytes first; an allowed mode conversion must strictly decode and canonically re-render to the same pin. Marker/receipt matching returns already-completed without side effect. After bulky pin reclamation, authenticate the immutable global MessageId tombstone, original actor source and route receipt before the same decision; missing verification evidence holds. Conflicting evidence fails closed. |
| Command-state proof absent/mismatch or partial page | `CommandStateUnproven` before admission/processor; perform bounded authenticated source lookup or completed paged replay. Stored `CurrentState.Events` cannot Apply a nonzero-hop source; no command effect or authoritative partial state. |
| Projection checkpoint=head>0 | Verify exact zero-route checkpoint-anchored prefix and fenced durable state/readback; metadata-only completion performs no event read, Apply, handler, state write or checkpoint advance. Wrong snapshot/checkpoint form fails closed. |
| Snapshot source unavailable, unreadable or byte/ETag/batch mismatch | Reject/discard the post-snapshot page before Apply, preserve approved v1 snapshot bytes, request a fresh authenticated page-1 proof from the gateway and start a new operation without snapshot authority; no partial tail Apply. |
| Cross-store checkpoint race or missing shared fence | `CheckpointFenceUnavailable`; immutable prior handler state and its checkpoint pointer remain authoritative. A new immutable state version alone cannot overwrite last good state or sign a mixed pair; ambiguous pointer CAS retains both versions until readback reconciliation. |
| Historical MessageId collision, preparation/batch-root or pin capacity | `HistoricalMessageIdCollision` holds rollout with both original events retained; `AppendPreparationHold` or `ReservationCapacityHold` rejects before actor save; `PublicationPinCapacityHold` retains committed outbox without send. No local uniqueness fallback or pin overwrite. |
| Binary membership revision/lease race | `ConsumerMembershipFenceUnavailable` holds publication; acceptance-time broker UTC/revision/fingerprint and all matching lease/probe expiries are checked atomically, including a lease that expired after publisher precheck. Ambiguous acceptance requires durable exact-operation/pin receipt lookup, with unknown outcome holding and no new nonce/send. One global topic pin/send serves all subscribers. |
| Named-projection batch visibility | Shared keys select one route/key-space committed generation root with checked per-source progress; aggregate-only pointers cannot publish them. The complete sparse Merkle row set, source progress, logical concurrency and TTL are verified under the fence, including a final authoritative-UTC expiry check after value readback. Partial shadow rows, failed/ambiguous root CAS or a reader seeing the prior root select only last-good unexpired values; at or after authenticated expiry, `ReadModelExpiryPending`/`ProjectionStateUnproven` holds until a scoped tombstone is certified. Bounded query overflow returns `ReadModelQueryLimit` without partial values. |
| Named read capability or key-space migration | Forged, expired, copied-principal or revoked authority returns `ReadModelAccessDenied` before key/root lookup. Overlapping unmigrated legacy physical keys return `KeySpaceMigrationHold` before guard activation; neither path exposes a partial row set or hidden-key existence. |
| Batch delete readback | Require absent-state readback and durable delete receipt/version under the same fence; absence alone or a present deleted row never advances checkpoint. |
| Legacy ingress or unknown binary consumer | Queued flat JSON resolves the authenticated publication revision and **complete addressed logical route set**, then durably commits/reads back the batch handoff manifest, every route record, one exact shared binary pin and send intent before old-message ack; partial/ambiguous work remains unacknowledged and reconciles by operation ID. Redelivery compares MessageId/source/full route set/pin; a difference is `LegacyHandoffConflict` with no second effect. Binary publishing remains fenced until every component/topic subscriber proves carrier and duplicate-safe capability. |
| Binary or `data_base64` dead-letter capture | Extract replay-safe identity only after attestation and pinned transport/body/header verification. Capture the admitted body durably with exact hash and actor index readback before HTTP 200; failure returns retryable non-2xx, and malformed identity is safe unidentified evidence, never an identified replay. Valid bytes above the old 1 MiB default are not acknowledged without retention. |
| Verified effect context or dependency route | Both new effect calls receive the additive verified wrapper around the unchanged public `EventStoreDomainEventContext`, with a signed non-null global position where required; mismatched wrapper/legacy members or an effect-capable DI dependency outside the selected write session/exact-key provider holds before invocation or ack. |
| Hopped handler view or production receipt missing | A projection/subscription hop requires verified `VerifiedEffectiveEventView`, a discoverable registered current-type handler and its §5 capability row; `HandlerCapabilityMismatch` blocks handler/effect/checkpoint or ack. A production route without registered `AtomicStore` or `ProviderIdempotency` and queryable receipt returns `HandlerCapabilityHold` before effect or marker. |
| Rollback after V2 persisted | Fence V1-only command/replay/projection/subscription endpoints before routing retained V2 history; `RollbackReaderCapabilityHold` retains work with no Apply/effect when no capable endpoint exists. |
| Snapshot absent/stale/unwitnessed | Preserve bytes; full replay before command. With 6.2 folded runtime, actor stages approved folded snapshot and side witness at `AggregateIdentity.SnapshotKey + ":evolution-witness"` together, saves once, reads both back, then may claim tail cost. Without 6.2 runtime, full replay only. |
| Ambiguous replay blob, ledger or final-result commit | Retain quota, operation ID and token; read back all three under one fence. Return exact pin only when all hashes/proof/transition match, release only after proven no transition, otherwise `ReplayCommitAmbiguous` holds without second Apply. |
| Encoded final response over 128 MiB | `FinalResponseLimit` before authoritative completion; no partial final state. An optional oversized timeline permits a fresh authenticated page-1 state-only replay with `includeTimeline=false`. |
| Exact final retry after token expiry | Within its retained 24-hour pin obligation, return exact bytes without scratch when current key/fingerprint and complete proof verify; expiry forbids fresh Apply or continuation. Invalid final proof restarts authenticated replay. |
| Prior approved delivery pin after registry rotation | Verify old purpose-02 claim only under exact historic obligation, authenticate immutable source and issue current purpose-01 view, compare source identity and pinned bytes; old fingerprint need not equal active fingerprint. Missing evidence holds without ack or effect. |
| Zero-tail completion at `long.MaxValue` | Count-zero start=end=head=target=`long.MaxValue` requires witnessed snapshot for aggregate replay or signed checkpoint/state for projection only; no event page, next token or increment. |

| Cancellation boundary | Required outcome |
| --- | --- |
| Before preparation CAS | Cancel with zero mutation. After a durable preparation capsule but before actor save, cancel with zero **event/actor** mutation; reconcile or release the capsule only with durable no-commit proof. |
| Pending or Ready batch root before save begins | Prove durable no actor commit, then release root and all per-ID references together; Pending partial indexing may resume only from the exact Prepared capsule under the same fence. Ambiguous proof retains the whole batch for bounded reconciliation. |
| No-op actor save begun or result ambiguous | Keep the immutable preparation and no-event result operation ID, then reconcile the actor-owned no-op checkpoint/result CAS by exact input hash, writer mode/fingerprint and result bytes. A proven commit returns pinned result even after cancellation; uncertain evidence holds without re-execution. |
| Save begun or result ambiguous | Preserve the Ready batch root and preparation capsule, read all actor events/metadata/outboxes/encoding and stored-digest sidecars **and pre-rendered operation-result side record** under bounded recovery token and actor fence; decide committed versus uncommitted from their exact durable witness, never caller-token rollback or fabricated failure. |
| After actor commit, before first send | Committed event, purpose-06 sidecar and immutable outbox routing intent remain; a staged exact pin remains if present. Cancellation leaves send pending. A missing first pin is derived once from durable event and readback routing intent only if prior send is disproven; ambiguous prior send holds. |
| Send begun, broker result ambiguous | Retain the same canonical pin and original same-mode outbound bytes; query the broker's durable `GetPublicationReceipt` under operation ID and exact pin digest before any new nonce/send. Accepted returns the prior receipt, rejected may retry only with a fresh valid fence under the same idempotent operation, and unknown/unavailable holds. No changed bytes under the same MessageId. |
| Subscriber effect committed, ack not durable | Cancellation leaves delivery retryable; retry reads effect receipt. After durable ack, cancellation cannot retract it. |
| Projection checkpoint/notification cleanup | Checkpoint only after durable handler state/effect and fenced readback. Cancel before that keeps old checkpoint. Notifications use durable outbox stable key and exact-byte retry. |

## 10. Migration and four ordered Story 6.6 slices

Before any reader cutover, inventory each provider's retained V1 aliases, raw/metadata/readable maxima, source versions, current-version reachability, unknown members, queued deliveries, old markers, in-flight writers, protection modes, old keys and snapshot/checkpoint evidence. Fence every V1-capable append path at gateway **and actor admission** before the evolved reader serves the domain: it either uses an upgraded producer that atomically stages and reads back the signed purpose-06 encoding sidecar with each new event, under the measured V1 bounds, or cannot append. Drain/reconcile in-flight old writes under that fence, finish sidecar and original StoredDigest/protection-decoder evidence backfill/readback for every valid retained event, durably classify any independently proven raw-corrupt item under §4 without fabricating a digest, and prove no unfenced binary can append before activating the new reader. V1 emission can continue only through upgraded sidecar-writing producers. A dormant reader may deploy earlier but cannot serve the domain. For each **valid** retained V1 event, separately approved offline work CAS-installs and verifies the purpose-06 encoding sidecar and authenticated original-digest/decoder manifest against immutable raw readback **before slice-1 reader activation/cutover**; missing sidecars block readiness. A new reader binary may be deployed dormant while old readers continue serving, but it cannot serve V1 events or advertise active read capability until this inventory, CAS installation and verification finish. Preflight retained migration source/review/decoder/key availability for the full readable lifetime and open backup/rollback/delivery obligations, protected-snapshot typed readability and raw/folded caps, named-projection shared key-space ownership, route-global row/progress index bounds and every legacy read/write guard. Preflight case-variant protection extension keys and data-dependent F output validation, and prove every subscribed component/topic consumer can decode the binary carrier before publisher cutover. Admit old both-absent V1 exchanges only under exact alias/readiness gate. Reader-first fleet advertises one approved **per-domain** capability; active V1 producers are fenced to measured legacy ceiling and bounded 128 MiB result **before** serving new gateways. An old endpoint that could allocate beyond that bound must be upgraded to bounded writer or have a provider-enforced preallocation bound. Legacy raw storage remains untouched. Unknown reused schema requires separately approved offline evidence migration, not an unimplemented discriminator. Queued V1 delivery lacking attestation obtains authenticated gateway source lookup, durably commits and reads back the §7 batch-wide handoff manifest, every addressed route record, shared send intent and exact attested binary pin before acknowledging old JSON, and reaches handlers only through that pin; missing lookup or handoff retries without old-message acknowledgement. Old outbox sends/pins drain or reconcile before stable pinning; old keys remain for every durable pin/queue/quarantine/rollback and encoding-sidecar obligation.

Cutover audits the §4 origin branch for **every valid** V1 event key. Valid historical rows need the independently signed manifest, reviewed docket, source evidence and version-2 digest sidecar; upgraded new V1 appends need their exact actor-save certificate and same-save event/sidecar/result readback. A raw-corrupt item uses only its separate cutover disposition and remains a replay stop. Do not demand an offline manifest for a new writer or accept a retained event under a fabricated new-writer branch. Advertise and compare `MigrationAuthorityDigest` beside the active per-domain RegistryFingerprint on every reader/publisher migration capability; a missing/mismatched signer or reviewer trust map, identity policy or retained old key holds mixed-reader activation. Binary publication readiness includes the §7 all-route physical acknowledgement contract, capped membership/external carrier, provider probe and bounded dead-letter capture. Named-projection readiness includes the callable fenced writer, query-token quota, changed-leaf TTL jobs, bounded ordinal archive and per-member committed-bundle proofs. Each provider probe must inspect durable bytes, route receipts, certified roots, old visible state and actor result/outcome records after injected crash and ambiguous CAS boundaries. Any provider unable to supply those exact capabilities holds the affected domain/route; no fixture is evidence that a production provider supports them.

1. **Reader foundation:** registry/manifest/codecs, bounded raw/protection read, shared evolution pipeline, proofs, corpus probes and additive DTOs. Slice 1 **owns an evidence-capable bounded V1 producer** before either an upgraded V1 writer or evolved reader serves: it premeasures legacy raw/readable/result preallocation ceilings, stages the exact raw event, purpose-06 encoding and version-2 StoredDigest sidecars, branch-01 origin, acyclic pre-save intent certificate, outbox and immutable domain result in one actor save, reads back those staged bytes, then obtains and verifies the separate **post-save** purpose-11 provider receipt under the committed generation. No V1 append is enabled until same-save, bounded-readback, receipt/key and provider capability probes pass. Retained valid V1 receives branch-02 independently reviewed offline manifest/docket/digest evidence before reader cutover; a retained raw-corrupt item may close inventory only through the §4 RawCorruptDisposition and remains unreadable. Fence or upgrade every live V1 appender; V1 admission remains active only for measured bounded evidence-writing producers after this gate. An unready reader binary stays dormant while old readers serve.
2. **Writer and transport dry run:** bounded mode-selected V1/V2 writer, raw publisher/ingress, exact pin/protection output, preparation capsules, atomic batch-root reservation, verification of Slice 1 stored-digest/origin/certificate evidence, explicit state-backend identity and provider probes. No V2 writes yet.
3. **Consumer integration:** command pages/replay, projections/rebuild/checkpoints, subscription effects/markers with verified context and audited effect-dependency graph, named-projection versioned read-model visibility, Admin/trace and all bypass reads through shared route with durable validation. Consumer code may deploy, but production subscriber activation that requires global committed reservations remains fenced until slice 4's complete historical ID backfill and marker reconciliation.
4. **Migration and final gate:** backfill IDs/markers/obligations and **reverify previously installed encoding sidecars** (never first install evidence required by an active slice-1 reader), block on every distinct-event historical MessageId collision until separately approved offline resolution, drain or reconcile old publications and queued legacy JSON, verify cross-instance backend and durable reservation/pin capacity, all serving domain peers' approved registry/trust/handler capability, production state-backend identity and transitive dependency closure, command-state proof and snapshot lookup paths, shared checkpoint fence, **every subscribed consumer's binary-carrier and duplicate-safety lease under a fenced authoritative membership revision**, one global pinned send per shared topic, registered production effect receipts, rollback reader-capability fences, corpus reachability and provider portability vectors; **only then** enable V2 writes.

Mixed-version rollback keeps V1 aliases/readers and old verifier keys while V2 persisted history, queues, pins or rollback targets need them; never emit V1 from a V2 current payload without proven F. Once **any** V2 event is persisted for a domain, every serving command, replay, projection, rebuild and subscription endpoint in both forward deployment and rollback MUST advertise and prove the minimum V2 read/evolution capability covering **all retained source versions** before receiving work. The gateway fences V1-only endpoints from those routes at the membership/load-balancer boundary; if no capable endpoint remains, commands and deliveries hold with `RollbackReaderCapabilityHold` before Apply/effect rather than being routed to a V1-only binary. Returning the writer mode to V1 does not downgrade immutable V2 history. Rollback drills include an old endpoint joining after first V2 append and a previously serving endpoint losing its capability; both are fenced before work can reach them. Advancing current payload version re-runs retained-source ≤16-hop proof. Adding a trust key fences affected-domain V2 writing, installs dual trust/verifier readiness first, attests one matching new RegistryFingerprint, then resumes signing/writes; old obligations remain verifiable under exact prior fingerprint and key interval. Changed `EventTransformHash` or route `HandlerCompatibilityHash` invalidates state witness and forces full replay/rebuild; trust-only change can re-attest a verified checkpoint without state mutation. Provider portability evidence includes exact-byte save/readback, raw ingress/send, bounded protection input/output, CAS/global reservation, effect idempotency and checkpoint fence under at least the configured production provider and a second independent provider or conforming harness.

Before enabling any versioned named-projection key-space prefix guard, inventory **all** physical keys under its exact `(tenant, domain, state store ID, byte prefix)` including old unversioned read-model rows, old shared-projection generation rows and marker keys. Classify each key with authenticated prior batch/marker evidence, not prefix spelling alone. For an overlap, either perform an explicit fenced copy/mapping to new immutable generation keys with exact bytes, owner, logical version, ETag and readback proof, or choose a nonoverlapping declared prefix with a bounded authenticated old-reader adapter. Retain the old keys and ownership evidence through checkpoint, backup, rollback and retry obligations. Fence old writers before the new guard can intercept their keys; until inventory, migration and readback complete, the new route is unready and legacy coordinator reads/writes remain under their old authorized path. An old unversioned key is never reinterpreted as a shadow row, and a guard may not block an active old coordinator without its proven replacement. Prefix collision, missing evidence or incomplete copy returns `KeySpaceMigrationHold`, preserving the last certified root and readable legacy path.

## 11. Verification vectors, rejected alternatives and decision state

Story 6.6 tests must cover: signed command-state proof admission before both processor routes, free typed/JSON state rejection, zero-hop legacy versus nonzero-hop effective Apply, completed paged result handoff and missing-result restart; snapshot source lookup of exact original v1 storage bytes/side record/folded bytes, ETag and actor batch/version mismatch; checkpoint=head>0 zero-route metadata-only proof separate from snapshot zero-tail and empty-stream forms; purpose-02/04/06 signed issuance-time tamper/backdate/expired-key obligations; same-store and two-store concurrent checkpoint advancement under shared fence and provider lacking fence support; scratch full-capacity clearing on success/failure/cancellation and cross-tenant pool reuse, encrypted spool crash cleanup/access/quota, response blob encryption, scope access and obligation-based CAS deletion; duplicate MessageIds in one proposed batch, cross-scope historical ID collision during backfill, Pending cleanup and durable reservation/pin capacity exhaustion; direct and transitive managed/native implementation dependency drift plus dynamic-load rejection; protection extension decoded scalar escape equality and empty/null/absent distinction; consumer join/configuration/fingerprint change racing a binary send, a lease expiring between publisher precheck and broker acceptance, ambiguous broker acceptance/receipt lookup with no new nonce, and a two-attempt external duplicate probe across restart; nonzero-hop live/retry/history/rebuild projection handler and subscription current-type dispatch using verified effective bytes; missing view, stale route or old-only handler hold before Apply/effect/checkpoint; legacy JSON crash before/after handoff ack and redelivery; rollback after V2 append with a V1-only endpoint; production legacy effect handler with no durable receipt; post-compute response pin capacity failure and lost final pinned response without scratch.
